# Muhurta Ganapati
<note>original: "Muhūrtagaṇapatiḥ"; This is the title of a classical Sanskrit treatise on *Muhurta* (electional astrology). This branch of knowledge is used to calculate the most auspicious times to perform rituals, start businesses, or conduct life events like marriages.</note>

No. 645 - Gha

<margin>
58 leaves <note>original: "58 patre"; This indicates the physical extent of the manuscript, consisting of 58 individual folios.</note>
</margin>

<vocab>Muhurta Ganapati (Proper Name), Muhurta (Auspicious Moment), Jyotisha (Vedic Astrology), Ganapati (The author's name)</vocab>

<page-num>60</page-num>

# ।। Om! Salutations to the Holy Lord Ganesha ।।

May the eternal Lord Shiva be victorious; He who is like the wish-fulfilling tree <note>original: "kalpadruma"</note> that grants true fruits, and who is embraced by the daughter of the Himalayas <note>original: "Haimavatya"; referring to the Goddess Parvati</note>, herself a divine wish-fulfilling vine. || 1 ||

In this world, the experts in *Muhurta* <note>The study of selecting auspicious moments for rituals</note> engage people in the performance of sacrifices and other sacred rites, for they recognize Time as the Lord, established through the authority of the Vedas. || 2 ||

Having bowed to the feet of the Guru, the venerable Vipresha, who is the very embodiment of sacrifice and of great fame; and having bowed to the lotus feet of my parents; and having thoroughly examined the *Samhitas* <note>Compendiums or collections of knowledge</note> of the best of sages—specifically those composed by Garga, Atri, Vashishtha, and Girisha, which serve as the foundation—and having understood the *Jyotir-nibandha* <note>A famous medieval digest of astrology</note> and the *Ratnamala* <note>"The Garland of Jewels," another foundational astrological text</note> with its varied and beautiful verses;

<margin>
Ga
<unclear>r</unclear>
ta
</margin>

|| 3 || I shall now compose this work for the sake of the quick and easy understanding of students <note>original: "balabodhaya"; literally "for the instruction of children," but refers to beginners in the science</note>. This book, based on the meanings declared in the classical texts of light <note>original: "Jyotir-grantha"; astronomical/astrological treatises</note>, uses simple and brief language to ensure success in determining the *Muhurta*. I am a member of the assembly favored by the King of Gauda <note>Medieval Bengal</note>, who is a crown among noble men. || 4 ||

The son of Ramadasa and grandson of Harishankara, of the Rawal family; I, Ganapati—endowed with humility, a treasury of knowledge, and an expert in the traditional sciences of mathematics and astronomy—now compose this work titled the *Muhurta Ganapati*. || 5 ||

### Now, the Calculation of the Year <note>original: "Samvatsaranayanam"; refers to identifying which year of the 60-year Jovian cycle is current</note>

Place the Shaka era <note>The Shaka era is a historical calendar era beginning in 78 CE, commonly used in Indian astronomy</note> in a separate place. Multiply it by twenty. Below it... <note>The text breaks off here as it begins the mathematical formula for calculating the planetary positions and the specific year name</note>

<summary>This page marks the beginning of the "Muhurta Ganapati." It opens with prayers to Ganesha and Shiva, establishes the author Ganapati Rawal's scholarly lineage, cites his classical sources (like Garga and Vashishtha), and begins the technical section on calculating the Samvatsara (the 60-year cycle).</summary>

<margin>Mu. Ga. <note>likely abbreviations for "Muhurta Ganita," the calculation of auspicious time</note></margin> ...added to the number four <gloss>4</gloss>, divided by one thousand eight hundred and seventy-five <note>original: "vana-shaila-gajendra-indu"; using the "Katapayadi" or "Bhuta-sankhya" system where Bana=5, Shaila=7, Gaja=8, Indu=1, read right-to-left as 1875</note> || 6 || Added to one hundred thousand <gloss>100,000</gloss> and divided by sixty, the remainders are the elapsed years according to the Jovian <margin>1</margin> measure <note>The Jovian or "Barhaspatya" cycle tracks time based on Jupiter's transit through the zodiac</note>, starting from the year Prabhava in their proper order. || 7 ||

### Now, the Names of the Sixty Years

The years are: Prabhava, Vibhava, Shukla, Pramoda, and then Prajapati; Angiras, Shrimukha, Bhava, Yuva, Dhata, and likewise Ishvara; || 8 || Bahudhanya, Pramathi, Vikrama, and then Vatsara <note>usually known as Vrishapra or Vishvavasu in other traditions</note>; Chitrabhanu, Subhanu, Tarana, Parthiva, and Vyaya; || 9 || Sarvajit, Sarvadhari, Virodhi, Vikrita, Khara; Nandana, Vijaya, Jaya, Manmatha, and Durmukha; || 10 || Hemalamba, Vilamba, Vikari, Sharvari, and Plava; Shubhakrit, Shobhana, Krodhi, Vishvavasu, and Parabhava; || 11 || Plavanga, Kilaka, Saumya, Sadharana, Virodhikrita; Paridhavi, Pramathi, Pramoda, Rakshasa, and Anala; || 12 || Pingala, Kalayukta, Siddhartha, Raudra, and Durmati; Dundubhi, Rudhirodgari, Raktaksha, Krodhana, and Kshaya. || 13 || These are the sixty years <term>Samvatsara<gloss>A full cycle of 60 years in the Hindu calendar</gloss></term> proclaimed in their sequence. The wise should understand that the fruit <note>the astrological influence or omen</note> of each year corresponds to its name. || 14 || <margin>l 2</margin>

### Now, the Calculation of the Year for the Region South of the Reva River

--- Page 6 ---
<meta>The text on this page continues the discussion of the sixty-year Jovian cycle, shifting from the list of names to the geographic and astronomical calculations that define these years. It transitions into the internal structure of the cycle—dividing it into five-year eras—and concludes with the definitions of the solar transits (ayanas) and the beginning of the seasonal classifications.</meta>

<meta>catchword: Head (Shira) <note>original: "shira," likely the beginning of "Shishira" or the cool season, which would appear on the following page</note></meta>

The Great Year <note>original: "Brihad-vatsara"</note> occurs in the region south of the Reva <note>another name for the Narmada River in central India</note> and is known as the "Manava" year by the wise. || 15 || That same year, when increased by nine, is used north of the Narmada. The Jovian year <term>Jaiva<gloss>related to Jupiter</gloss></term> is described according to the average transit of Jupiter <note>original: "Vachaspati"</note> through one zodiac sign. || 16 ||

### **Now, Knowledge of the Years Starting with Prabhava ||**

When Jupiter <note>original: "Guru"</note> rises in the first quarter of the lunar mansion Dhanishtha in the month of Magha <note>roughly January–February</note>, then it is known that the year Prabhava—the leader of the years—begins. || 17 ||

### **Now, the Names of the Eras of the Years ||**

In the beginning, this first year of the five-year era <term>Yuga<gloss>a small cycle of five years in this context</gloss></term> has Agni <note>original: "Anala"</note> as its presiding deity. The second is called Parivatsara, whose deity is said to be the Sun <note>original: "Bhauma," though traditionally the Sun/Arka rules the second year; Bhauma usually refers to Mars</note>. || 18 || The third is known as Idavatsara, with the Moon <note>original: "Soma"</note> as its deity. The fourth is Anuvatsara, which is declared to belong to Prajapati <note>the Creator deity</note>. || 19 || Then there is Idvatsara, which is the fifth, and its deity is the goddess Gauri. With twelve such eras <note>each consisting of five years</note>, the full sixty-year cycle is completed. || 20 ||

The lords of these <insert>twelve eras</insert> are known as: Agni, Jupiter, Indra, Agni, and Tvashtri <note>the celestial architect</note>; || 21 || Ahirbudhnya <note>the serpent of the depths</note>, the Ancestors, the Universal Gods, and the Moon; || 21 || Indra, Agni, the Twin Physicians <note>original: "Dasras" or the Ashvins</note>, and Bhaga—these are remembered in their proper order. || **Now,**

### **The Names of the Solar Halves ||**

When the Sun reaches the sign of Capricorn <note>original: "Makara"</note> and the five signs following it, it is the Northern Transit <term>Uttarayana<gloss>the period when the sun moves northward in the sky</gloss></term>. This is the "Day of the Gods," and it is praised for the performance of auspicious deeds. || 22 || In the six signs starting from Cancer <note>original: "Karka"</note>, it is known as the Southern Transit <term>Dakshinayana<gloss>the period when the sun moves southward</gloss></term>. This is always called the "Night of the Gods," and here success in worldly actions is spoken of. || 23 ||

### **Now, the Names of the Seasons ||**

These are known as Spring <note>original: "Vasanta"</note> and Summer <note>original: "Grishma"</note>; following those are Autumn <note>original: "Sharad"</note>, Winter <note>original: "Hemanta"</note>, and the Cool Season...

<summary>This page explains the regional differences in calculating the Jovian year and breaks down the sixty-year cycle into twelve five-year eras (yugas), each ruled by a specific deity. It also defines the two halves of the solar year—Uttarayana and Dakshinayana—as the "day" and "night" of the gods.</summary>

<margin>mu. ga.</margin> ...and the Cool Season <term>Shishira<gloss>late winter</gloss></term>—these are known as the six seasons. **॥ २४ ॥** When the Sun enters the signs of the Fish <term>Meena<gloss>Pisces</gloss></term> and the Ram <term>Mesha<gloss>Aries</gloss></term>, it is celebrated as Spring <term>Vasanta</term>. When in the Bull <term>Vrishabha<gloss>Taurus</gloss></term> and the

<margin>2 ya 2</margin> Twins <term>Mithuna<gloss>Gemini</gloss></term>, it is the Summer <term>Grishma</term>. The Monsoon <term>Varsha</term> occurs when the Sun is in the Lion <term>Simha<gloss>Leo</gloss></term> and the Crab <term>Karkata<gloss>Cancer</gloss></term>. **॥ २५ ॥** In the Maiden <term>Kanya<gloss>Virgo</gloss></term> and the Scales <term>Tula<gloss>Libra</gloss></term>, the season is declared to be Autumn <term>Sharad</term>. Pre-winter <term>Hemanta</term> occurs when the Sun is in the Scorpion <term>Vrischika</term>,

and the Cool Season <term>Shishira</term> occurs in the Deer-Catcher <term>Mriga<gloss>Capricorn</gloss></term> and the Water-Pot <term>Kumbha<gloss>Aquarius</gloss></term>. **॥ २६ ॥** <margin>ba 2</margin>

## ॥ Now, the Naming of the Months ॥

The months are known as Chaitra, then Vaishakha, Jyeshtha, and Ashadha. Shravana, Proshthapada <note>original: "Proshthapada," the ancient name for the month of Bhadrapada</note>, and likewise Ashvina. Karttika, Margashirsha, Pausha, Magha, and

Phalguna. **॥ २७ ॥** Now, the other names for these months, starting with Chaitra, are spoken: **॥ २८ ॥** Madhu <note>Sweet</note>, Madhava <note>Honey-like</note>, Shukra <note>Bright</note>, and Shuchi <note>Pure</note>; then Nabhas <note>Cloudy</note>, Nabhasya <note>Misty</note>, Isha <note>Master</note>, and Urja <note>Energy</note>; Sahas <note>Strength</note>, Sahasya <note>Forceful</note>,

Tapas <note>Heat</note>, and Tapasya <note>Produced by Heat</note>. These are the names of the months in order. A month is defined by the learned as ending with the New Moon <term>Darshanta<gloss>also called Amanta</gloss></term>, or by the solar transit of the Sun. A civil month <term>Savana</term> is thirty days. A

<margin>the text reads "positions of planets, etc."</margin> sidereal month <term>Nakshatra</term> is declared by the rotation of the Moon. These months should always be understood by the wise according to their specific purpose. **॥ ३१ ॥** Solar calculations should be used for marriages, the positions of the planets, and the days of the week.

Civil calculations are for the protection of the womb <note>likely referring to medical and pregnancy rituals</note> and similar matters. Sidereal calculations are for the formation of rain clouds. Vows and sacrifices are performed in the lunar <insert>month</insert>, though sometimes they are avoided <note>likely referring to the avoidance of intercalary months for rituals</note>. This lunar month is of two types: that ending in the New Moon and that ending in the Full Moon. For matters of the Gods, the month ending in the Full Moon is used; for ceremonies for the Ancestors, the month ending in the New Moon is used.

## ॥ Now, the Naming of the Fortnights ॥

Both the Waxing <term>Shukla</term> and Waning <term>Krishna</term> are the two fortnights, used respectively for the Gods and the Ancestors. <margin>Rama 2</margin>

It also categorizes the four types of months (Solar, Civil, Sidereal, and Lunar) and specifies their appropriate uses in ritual and daily life.</summary>

...[In auspicious] actions, as well as [general] well-being, everything is praised during the Bright Fortnight <term>Shukla Paksha<gloss>the waxing phase of the moon</gloss></term>. **॥ ३५ ॥**

### Thus ends the first chapter, "The Section on the Year and Related Matters,"
### in the *Muhurta Ganapati*, composed by Ganapati,
### the son of the illustrious astrologer Ravala Harishankara.

### Now begins the Section on Lunar Days.

The first day <term>Pratipad</term>, the second <term>Dvitiya</term>, the third <term>Tritiya</term>, and following that, the fourth, fifth, sixth, seventh, and then the eighth. **॥ १ ॥** The ninth, tenth, and eleventh; then the twelfth, then the thirteenth is to be known, and then the fourteenth is declared. **॥ २ ॥** There is the Full Moon <term>Purnima</term> in the Bright Fortnight, while in the Dark Fortnight <note>the waning phase</note>, it is known as the New Moon <term>Amavasya</term>. The lords of these lunar days <note>the presiding deities</note> are the Creator <term>Vidhatri<gloss>Brahma</gloss></term> and the others, the gods, starting with the Sun and the Planets. **॥ ३ ॥** The Sun, Water <note>Varuna</note>, the All-gods <term>Vishvedevas</term>, Vishnu <margin>ba</margin>, the God of Love <term>Smara</term>, and Shiva. The Sun, Brihaspati <note>the Guru of the gods</note>, the Destroyer <term>Ghara</term>, and the Ancestors <term>Pitris</term> are thus celebrated [as the lords of the remaining days]. **॥ ४ ॥** 

<note>The scribe has repeated the verse number 4 below.</note>

Nanda <note>Joyful</note>, Bhadra <note>Gentle</note>, Jaya <note>Victorious</note>, Rikta <note>Empty</note>, and Purna <note>Full</note> are the [five groups of days], starting from the first day <term>Pratipad</term>. **॥ ४ ॥** <insert>In the middle <unclear>of these</unclear></insert> it is auspicious during the Bright Fortnight, but not for the fifth in the sequence. **॥ ५ ॥** In the Dark Fortnight, they are auspicious in the middle, and always auspicious without doubt. 

<note>The scribe has skipped verse number 6 in the manuscript.</note>

### Now, the Activities for the "Joyful" (Nanda) and other Lunar Days.

Singing, dancing, as well as field work, painting, and collecting [supplies]; clothing, <margin>va</margin> ornaments, and craftsmanship—these are remembered as auspicious when occurring on a Nanda <note>the 1st, 6th, or 11th</note> day. **॥ ७ ॥** Marriage, the sacred thread ceremony <term>Upanayana</term>, travel, jewelry, arts and crafts, and so on; tasks involving elephants and chariots bring success on the Bhadra <note>the 2nd, 7th, or 12th</note> days. **॥ ८ ॥** For military matters, battle, weapons, processions, festivals, and house-building; for medicine and commerce, success is found in all these on the Jaya <note>the 3rd, 8th, or 13th</note> days. **॥ ९ ॥** For the killing or capture of enemies, and for the employment of poison or weapons; these should be performed <unclear>at night</unclear>...

<summary>This page concludes the first chapter of the Muhurta Ganapati and introduces the classification of lunar days (Tithis), assigning them presiding deities and categorizing them into groups like Nanda and Bhadra to determine their suitability for various social, religious, and military activities.</summary>

<margin>
Muhurta Ganapati
Chapter 3
-
</margin>
On an "Empty" <term>Rikta<gloss>lunar days 4, 9, and 14, considered unfavorable for new beginnings</gloss></term> day, no auspicious work is successful at all. **॥ 10 ॥** Rituals such as the student's initiation <term>Vratabandha<gloss>the thread ceremony</gloss></term>, marriage, travel, and the decrees of kings—as well as rites for peace <term>Shantika</term> and prosperity <term>Paushtika</term>—attain complete success on a "Full" <term>Purna</term> day. **॥ 11 ॥**

**Now, the general auspicious and inauspicious lunar days.**

The second, the fifth, the third, and the seventh; likewise the tenth, the eleventh, the "Empty" days, the first day of the fortnight <term>Pratipad</term>, and the thirteenth. **॥ 12 ॥** These "Full" lunar days are declared the best and bring prosperity when used for the activities prescribed for them; for other activities, the lunar days are considered undesirable and are avoided by the wise. **॥ 13 ॥** The "Empty" fourteenth day, the first day of the Bright Fortnight, and the day known as the New Moon <term>Darsha<gloss>Amavasya</gloss></term>—these must be avoided with great effort for all auspicious works. **॥ 14 ॥**

**Now, the "Joint-Hole" days of the fortnight.** <term>Paksharandhra<gloss>Specific points in the lunar cycle considered spiritually "vulnerable" or unlucky</gloss></term>

The fourth, sixth, eighth, ninth, twelfth, and the fourteenth—these lunar days are called the "Joint-Holes" of the fortnight. They are to be abandoned in all ritual works. **॥ 15 ॥** 
<margin>Indeed</margin>
Of these days, the portions related to the Vasus <note>the 8th day</note>, Indra <note>the 14th day</note>, the Elements <note>the 5th/25th</note>, the Directions <note>the 10th</note>, and the Arrows <note>the 5th</note> are to be given up. These specific portions <note>measured in Ghatikas or 24-minute units</note> are to be abandoned as if they were consumed by a shadow; the remaining parts of the day are auspicious. **॥ 16 ॥**

**Now, the auspiciously-named days.**

For the four zodiac signs starting with Aries <term>Mesha</term>, the four days starting from the first <term>Pratipad</term> are auspicious; the fifth day is also celebrated as favorable for this group of four. **॥ 17 ॥** For Leo <term>Simha</term>, Virgo <term>Kanya</term>, Libra <term>Tula</term>, and Scorpio <term>Ali</term>, the days from the sixth onward are auspicious in order; thus the group of four ends with the tenth, and the eleventh is also a happy day. **॥ 18 ॥** For the four signs starting with Sagittarius <term>Dhanu</term>, the Full Moon is the most supreme day. When a lunar day is joined with an inauspicious planet or sign, it does not bring good fortune. **॥ 19 ॥**

**Now, the specific activities for each lunar day.**

Marriage, festivals, travel, and the installation of deities; these works, as well as those involving stone and similar materials, one should perform on the first day <term>Pratipad</term> of the Bright Fortnight. **॥ 20 ॥** 
<margin>Not</margin> 
State affairs, marriage and similar auspicious ceremonies, architecture <term>Vastu</term>, and the making of ornaments; student initiation and installations are remembered to be performed on the second lunar day <term>Dvitiya</term>. **॥ 21 ॥** 
<margin>Rama 3</margin>
The first feeding of a child <term>Annaprashana</term>, music...

<summary>This page defines which lunar days (Tithis) are auspicious for specific activities like marriage, construction, and royal decrees, while warning against "Empty" (Rikta) and "Joint-Hole" (Paksharandhra) days. It begins a detailed list of rituals permitted on the first and second days of the lunar month.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the specific instructions for each lunar day (Tithi), building on the classifications of the previous page. While the previous page categorized the days into groups like "Empty" or "Full," this section provides a detailed list of activities—ranging from marriage to warfare—recommended for each individual day of the lunar month.</meta>

<margin>Knowledge</margin> <gloss>Science</gloss> Science, education, and all manner of crafts are favored on the Second day <note>original: Dwitiya</note>; all such activities are also highly praised when performed on the Third day <note>original: Tritiya</note>. **॥ 22 ॥** The destruction or piercing of enemies, battle, and the preparation of weapons should be carried out on the Fourth day <note>original: Chaturthi</note>, though some auspicious acts may rarely succeed there. **॥ 23 ॥** All auspicious works, whether they are "stationary" <note>like building a house</note> or "moving" <note>like starting a journey</note>, attain success on the Fifth day <note>original: Panchami</note>, except for the lending of money. **॥ 24 ॥** Working with ivory, bathing with oils, and crafts, as well as warfare, architecture <term>Vastu<gloss>the traditional Indian system of architecture and design</gloss></term>, and making jewelry, are successful on the Sixth day <note>original: Shashthi</note>. **॥ 25 ॥** Tasks involving elephants, marriage and similar rites, music, and the wearing of new clothes or jewelry; likewise, journeys, entering a new place, and battles are successful on the Seventh day <note>original: Saptami</note>. **॥ 26 ॥** Activities concerning the king, women, gems, and jewelry, as well as battle, the bearing of weapons, architecture, and crafts, shall find success when performed on the Eighth day <note>original: Ashtami</note>. **27** Battle, quarrels, gambling, drinking alcohol, and hunting; likewise, deeds involving poison, fire, and weapons, shall find success on the Ninth day <note>original: Navami</note>. **28** All auspicious acts like marriage, the wearing of jewelry, travel, and entering a new home; as well as tasks concerning elephants, horses, and the king, are successful on the Tenth day <note>original: Dashami</note>. **॥ 29 ॥** The initiation of vows <term>Vratabandha<gloss>the sacred thread ceremony for students</gloss></term>, marriage, warfare, crafts, festivals for the gods, coming and going, and making jewelry are praised on the Eleventh day <note>original: Ekadashi</note>. **30** One should perform "moving" or "fixed" tasks, marriage, and the initiation of vows on the Twelfth day <note>original: Dwadashi</note>; however, one should avoid the use of oil and travel as prescribed. **॥ 31 ॥** <margin>Day 7</margin> Travel, entering a house, battle, and auspicious acts involving clothes and jewelry—excluding the initiation of vows—are suitable for the Thirteenth day <note>original: Trayodashi</note>. **॥ 32 ॥** Harsh acts such as piercing, fire-rites, and weapons should be used on the Fourteenth day <note>original: Chaturdashi</note>; one should avoid auspicious work, wine, and travel. **॥ 33 ॥** Auspicious rites, jewelry, crafts, the consecration of idols <term>Pratishtha</term>, battle, and household duties are successfully accomplished on the Full Moon day <note>original: Purnimasi</note>. **॥ 34 ॥** Establishing the sacred fire, making great gifts <term>Mahadana</term>, and sacrifices for the ancestors <term>Pitri-yaga</term>—these are the deeds to be performed on the New Moon <term>Darsha<gloss>Amavasya</gloss></term>; no other auspicious action should be done. **॥ 35 ॥**

It lists specific days when routine activities like brushing teeth or bathing with certain oils are forbidden, followed by the significant dates that mark the beginning of cosmic eras.</meta>

## ## Now, the Forbidden Lunar Days for Cleaning the Teeth

On the New Moon <note>original: Amavasya</note>, as well as on the sixth day <note>original: Shashthi</note> and the eleventh day <note>original: Ekadashi</note>, and when one is fasting: one should not perform oral hygiene <term>Dantadhavana<gloss>The traditional practice of cleaning teeth using a medicinal twig</gloss></term> during the first three hours <note>original: Yama</note> after the start of the first lunar day. **॥ 36 ॥**

**॥ Now, forbidden days for bathing with Indian Gooseberry <term>Amalaka<gloss>The fruit of the Emblica officinalis tree, often used in ritual baths for its cooling and purifying properties</gloss></term> ॥**

On the tenth, the seventh, the New Moon, the second, and the ninth days; likewise on the thirteenth day, a person should never bathe with Indian Gooseberry. **॥ 37 ॥**

**॥ Now, forbidden days for activities involving oil and other acts ॥**

Shaving <note>original: Kshaura</note> on the fourteenth day, applying oil on the sixth, eating meat on the eighth, and sexual intercourse on the New Moon day should be avoided if one desires a long life. **॥ 38 ॥**

**॥ Now, the items to be avoided for the sixteen lunar days starting from the first ॥**

One should avoid mangoes on the first day <note>original: Pratipada</note>, and the *Brihati* fruit <note>a type of Indian nightshade/eggplant</note>. [Also avoid] salt, radish, jackfruit, and the application of oil. 

<margin>Pu</margin> 
<margin>Pumpkin 5</margin> 

[Avoid] the Indian Gooseberry, coconut, pointed gourd <note>original: Patola</note>, beans, meat, radish, banana, and honey in that order [through the lunar cycle]. **॥ 40 ॥** Sexual intercourse on the New Moon or the Full Moon is considered a great disrespect.

**Now, regarding the start-dates of the Eras <term>Yugadi<gloss>Sacred days marking the beginning of the four cosmic ages of the world</gloss></term> and the Manvantaras <term>Manvadi<gloss>Dates marking the beginning of the reign of a Manu, a progenitor of humanity</gloss></term> ॥**

The ninth day of the bright half of Kartika and the third day of the bright half of Vaishakha; **॥ 41 ॥** the thirteenth day of the dark half of Ashvina and the New Moon of Phalguna. The *Treta* Era began in the month of Kartika <note>on the 9th</note>, and the *Treta* <insert>also</insert> began in Madhava <note>Vaishakha</note>. **॥ 42 ॥** The *Dvapara* Era began in Phalguna, and the *Kali* Era <note>our current age</note> began in the month of Shravana. On these days, one should give gifts of sesame, grain, salt, gold, and cows. **॥ 43 ॥** These Era-beginnings <note>Yugadis</note> are holy; however, the wise should avoid common auspicious works on them. Among these, the third day <note>original: Akshaya Tritiya</note> is exceptionally beautiful for all types of work. **॥ 44 ॥** 

The ninth of the bright half of Ashvina, the seventh of Magha, the third of the bright half of Bhadrapada, and the auspicious twelfth of Kartika; **॥ 45 ॥** the eighth of the dark half of Bhadrapada, and the eleventh of the <insert>bright</insert> half of Ashvina... **॥ 46 ॥** **Now, there are three such Manvantara dates in the month of Bhadrapada. **॥ 47 ॥** Here, the months should be known to end with the Full Moon <note>original: Rakanta</note>.** This is known by the astrologer Rama <note>original: Ganako Rama</note>.

...by the best [of people]. **॥** 

### Now, the Yoga of the Half-Sun's Rise <note>original: Ardhodayayoga</note>
<margin>Nor</margin> In the month of Magha <note>approximately January/February</note>, when the Sun is at the New Moon <note>Amavasya</note> on a Sunday, coinciding with the astronomical alignment of *Vyatipata* and the lunar mansion of *Shravana*: this is called the "Rise of the Half-Sun" <term>Ardhodayayoga<gloss>An extremely rare and auspicious conjunction occurring only once every many years</gloss></term>. It is considered more meritorious than a hundred other festivals. **॥**

### Now, the Yoga of the Released Day <note>original: Muktadivayoga</note>
This occurs when the alignment is slightly less than that of the *Ardhodayayoga* <note>lacking one of the specific factors mentioned above</note>. **॥** 

### Now, the Elephant's Shadow <note>original: Gajachhaya</note>
During the fortnight of the ancestors <term>Pitru-paksha<gloss>The 15-day lunar period in the month of Ashvina dedicated to honoring deceased relatives</gloss></term>, on the thirteenth lunar day, if the Moon is in the lunar mansion of *Hasta* and the Sun is in *Magha*, it is called the "Elephant's Shadow." Performing ancestral rites <note>original: Shraddha</note> during this yoga is highly fruitful. **॥ 5 ॥** 

### Now, the Kapila Sixth Day <note>original: Kapila Shashti</note>
In the month of Ashvina, during the dark fortnight, on the sixth lunar day, if it falls on a Tuesday <note>original: Bhauma</note> with the Moon in the lunar mansion of *Rohini* and the *Vyatipata* alignment: then that sixth day is the "Kapila Sixth," which bestows infinite merit. **॥ 51 ॥** 

### Now, contextually, the Rising of the Star Agastya <note>Canopus</note>
When the Sun reaches the degree equivalent to the shadow of the gnomon <term>Palabha<gloss>The length of the shadow cast by a vertical pillar at noon on the equinox, used to determine latitude and astronomical timing</gloss></term> increased by eight, the sage born from a pot <note>original: Kumbhajanman; a title for the sage Agastya</note> rises in the sky. **॥ 52 ॥**

## <margin>Ri</margin> Thus ends the second chapter concerning Lunar Days in the *Muhurta Ganapati*, composed by Ravishankara, the son of the illustrious Maheshvara. **॥** 

## Now, the Chapter on Weekdays
Sunday, Monday, Mars-day [Tuesday], Mercury-day [Wednesday], and then Jupiter-day [Thursday], Friday, and Saturday—these are proclaimed as the weekdays. **॥ 1 ॥** 

Shiva, Durga, Guha <note>the warrior god Kartikeya</note>, Vishnu, Brahma, Indra, and Time <note>original: Kaala; often associated with Yama, the god of death</note> are proclaimed as the presiding deities of the Sun and the other planets in their respective order. **॥ 2 ॥** 

Thursday, Monday, Wednesday, and Friday are considered auspicious days for performing good deeds. The others are considered "cruel" <note>malefic</note> days, and those with names associated with Mercury and Jupiter are specifically noted for their traits. **॥ 3 ॥** 

The Sun is steady, the Moon is moving <note>fickle</note>, Mars is fierce, Mercury is neutral, Jupiter is light <note>quick</note>, Venus is soft, and Saturn is intense <note>sharp</note>. Thus it is said. **॥ 4 ॥**

### <margin>Nai</margin> Now, the specific activities for each weekday
Royal coronations, auspicious ceremonies, matters of self-interest, wearing [new clothes/jewelry], obtaining a title or position, service, and the mining of gold and other gems [should be done on Sunday]. **॥ 5 ॥** 

Activities involving conch shells, pearls, silver, and the like; wearing ornaments; singing; Vedic sacrifices; the pursuit of wisdom; eating; and matters regarding women or the sale of cows should be performed on Monday <note>original: Somavasara</note>. **॥ 6 ॥** 

Acquiring land, travel, governing a region, use of poison, weapons, or fire; and matters involving coral, gold, and similar items—these should one do on Tuesday <note>original: Kujavasara</note>. **॥ 7 ॥**

<summary>This page concludes the discussion of rare lunar alignments (Yogas) and introduces the classification of the seven weekdays, their presiding deities, and the specific activities recommended for Sunday, Monday, and Tuesday.</summary>

Proficiency in the arts, commerce, concluding peace treaties, physical exercise, and service; as well as instrumental music, study, and writing—these should be undertaken on **Wednesday**. **॥ 8 ॥**

Religious activities for women, learning, sensory enjoyment, nourishment, and the collection of resources; travel, medicine, and the wearing of ornaments should be performed on **Thursday**. **॥ 9 ॥**

Singing, [matters involving] women, gems, fine bedding, and the like; clothing, festive celebrations, ornaments, aesthetic interests, and managing treasuries—all these find success on **Friday**. **॥ 10 ॥**

Entering a new home, spiritual initiation, the taming of elephants, and actions intended for stability; [matters involving] servants, queens, and kings—all this is successful on **Saturday**. **॥ 11 ॥**

Activities aimed at the growth or increase of a planet's influence should be performed on that planet's own day for auspicious results. Conversely, activities aimed at decrease or removal will not succeed, even with great effort. **॥ 12 ॥**

->**Now, the Hora [Planetary Hour] for Daily Activities is Discussed.**<-

Adjusting for one's own location and the distance from the prime meridian <note>original: Rekha</note> by yojanas <note>an ancient unit of distance, roughly 8-9 miles</note> minus a quarter... by that many minutes <term>Palas<gloss>A traditional unit of time; 60 palas make one ghati (24 minutes)</gloss></term> added or subtracted, the lunar days are determined as before. **॥ 13 ॥** When the difference in minutes is subtracted from or added to midday, the entry of the day is reckoned from sunrise... **॥ 14 ॥**

The number of *ghatis* <note>24-minute periods</note> passed since the start of the day should be multiplied by two and divided by five. The resulting number indicates the current <term>Hora<gloss>A planetary hour, roughly one-twenty-fourth of the time from one sunrise to the next</gloss></term>, starting sequentially from the ruler of that day. **॥ 15 ॥**

The Sun, Venus, Mercury, the Moon, Saturn, Jupiter, and Mars—in this specific order, the planets are known as the Lords of the Hours. **॥ 16 ॥** Whatever activity was prescribed by the ancient teachers for a particular day will surely find success if it is performed specifically during the *Hora* of that planet. **॥ 17 ॥**

It is said that the "defects" or inauspicious influences of the days do not manifest at night; however, some scholars state that this applies specifically to the days of Saturday, Tuesday, and Sunday. **॥ 18 ॥**

->**The Results of Oil Massage on Specific Weekdays.**<-

<margin>Dhotar = 2 <note>This likely refers to a correction or a specific reference in a different text or ledger</note></margin>

[Massaging the body with oil on] **Sunday** causes heat <note>interpreted as fever or bodily inflammation</note>; on **Monday** it brings fame; on **Tuesday**, sharp intellect; on **Wednesday**, popularity; on **Thursday**, loss of wealth; on **Friday**, misfortune; and on **Saturday**, happiness. Such are the effects of an oil massage for men—of this there is no doubt. **॥ 19 ॥**

On **Sunday**, **Tuesday**, during the astronomical alignments of *Vyatipata* or *Sankranti* <note>when the sun enters a new zodiac sign</note>, or on the *Vaidhriti* day... **॥ 20 ॥** on the sixth and eighth lunar days...

> The defects <note>inauspicious influences</note> of the days belonging to Jupiter, Venus, and the Sun manifest at night. During the day, the defects of the Moon, Saturn, and Mars manifest. The defects of Wednesday are never present. ॥ 5 ॥

### Now, Exceptions to the Prohibition of Oil Massage

<margin>Su. Ri.</margin>
Anointing oneself with oil <term>Tailabhanga<gloss>The traditional practice of oil massage, which is restricted on certain days of the week in Hindu astrology</gloss></term> is not considered a fault if it is performed in public, during a festival, in times of illness, or when specifically prescribed by scriptural injunctions. Furthermore, oil massage is not harmful when done on a Friday with cow dung, on a Tuesday with earth <note>original: "mrid," likely referring to sacred clay or soil</note>, on a Thursday with Durva grass, or on a Sunday when the Moon is in the Pushya mansion <unclear>yat</unclear>. **॥ 23 ॥** Oil that has been consecrated with mantras, mustard oil, oil scented with flowers, or oil mixed with other substances never brings about a negative astrological effect. **॥ 24 ॥**

# Thus ends the third chapter on Weekdays in the *Muhurta Ganapati*, composed by the astrologer Ganapati, son of the illustrious astrologer Ravala Hari Shankara. 

## Now, the Chapter on the Lunar Mansions

The deity of Ashvini is the two Ashvins <note>the divine twin physicians</note>; Bharani has Yama <note>the god of death</note> as its deity. Krittika is said to belong to Agni <note>fire</note>, Rohini to the Creator <note>original: Vidhata/Brahma</note>, and [the next] to the God of Love. **॥ 1 ॥** Mrigashira is ruled by the Moon <note>original: Chandra</note>, while Ardra is ruled by Shiva. Aditi is the mistress of Punarvasu, and Brihaspati <note>original: Vakpati, the Lord of Speech</note> is the lord of Pushya. **॥ 2 ॥** The lord of Ashlesha is the Serpent; the Ancestors <note>original: Pitris</note> are remembered as the lords of Magha. Bhaga is the lord of Purva Phalguni, and Aryaman is the lord of Uttara Phalguni. **॥ 3 ॥** The Sun <note>original: Surya</note> is the lord of Hasta; Tvashtr <note>the divine architect</note> is the lord of the mansion named Chitra. Vayu <note>wind</note> is the deity of Svati, and Indra and Agni are the deities of Vishakha. **॥ 4 ॥** Mitra is the lord of Anuradha, and Indra is said to be the lord of Jyeshtha. The deity of Mula is the Rakshas <note>often identified with Nirriti, the goddess of destruction</unclear>, and Water <note>original: Jala/Apah</note> is the lord of Purvashadha. **॥ 5 ॥** The Universal Gods <note>original: Vishvedevas</note> are the deities of Uttara Phalguni <note>likely a scribal error for Uttarashadha</note>... 

<margin>and 4</margin>
...Brahma <note>original: Vidhi</note> is the lord of Abhijit. Vishnu is the lord of Shravana, and the Vasus are the deities of Dhanishtha. **॥ 10 ॥** Varuna is the deity of Shatataraka <note>Shatabhisha</note>; <unclear>Ramesha</unclear> is said to be the lord of the next. Ajaikapada is the lord of Purva Bhadrapada, Ahirbudhnya is the lord of the next <note>Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, and Pushan is the lord of Revati. **॥ 11 ॥**

## Now, Generally Auspicious and Inauspicious Lunar Mansions

Rohini, Pushya, Mrigashira, Hasta, Ashvini, the three "Uttara" mansions <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttarashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, Revati, Shravana, Dhanishtha, Punarvasu, Anuradha, and Svati—these lunar mansions are auspicious. All favorable actions are successfully accomplished during these times. **॥ 1 ॥** The three "Purva" mansions <note>Purva Phalguni, Purvashadha, and Purva Bhadrapada</note>, Vishakha, Jyeshtha, Ardra, Mula, and Shatataraka—these fifteen are considered of middling or "ordinary" quality. Bharani, Krittika, Magha, and Ashlesha... Now...

### Now, the Specific Activities for Each Lunar Mansion

<term>Ashvini<gloss>The first Lunar Mansion, symbolized by a horse's head, associated with speed and healing</gloss></term>: Under the mansion of the Ashvins, one should undertake travel, administer medicine, adorn oneself with ornaments, handle weapons, pursue learning, practice arts and crafts, manage elephants, perform marriages, and engage in auspicious festivities. **॥ 16 ॥**

<term>Bharani<gloss>The second Lunar Mansion, ruled by Yama, the god of death; associated with transformation and restraint</gloss></term>: During this mansion, one may carry out daring or harsh deeds, the destruction of enemies, imprisonment, acts involving poison or fire, and other such fierce activities <note>original: "krurukhya," likely referring to cruel or formidable tasks</note>. **॥ 17 ॥**

<term>Krittika<gloss>The third Lunar Mansion, associated with the Pleiades and the element of fire</gloss></term>: In Krittika, one should always perform acts of courage, take possession of property, or carry out the slaying of enemies. It is also suitable for marriage and for tasks that require a combination of harshness and friendship <note>original: "loman-maitra," perhaps referring to tasks like weaving or reconciling opposites</note>. **॥ 18 ॥**

<term>Rohini<gloss>The fourth Lunar Mansion, often considered the most auspicious for growth and beauty</gloss></term>: One should perform strengthening rituals <term>Paushtika<gloss>Rituals intended to increase health, wealth, or prosperity</gloss></term>, marriage, the accumulation of wealth, the building of palaces or temples, auspicious ceremonies, and the making of ornaments. **॥ 19 ॥**

<term>Mrigashirsha<gloss>The fifth Lunar Mansion, symbolized by a deer's head, associated with searching and wandering</gloss></term>: Marriage, opening shops, travel, the installation of deities, architectural work, and starting service for others are all successful when done under the auspicious Mrigashirsha. **॥ 20 ॥**

<term>Ardra<gloss>The sixth Lunar Mansion, associated with the star Betelgeuse; its name means "the moist one" and it is linked to stormy emotions</gloss></term>: In Ardra, one should engage in conflict, binding, cutting, killing, and work involving poison, fire, or sorcery <note>original: "darunocchatane," referring to the magical expulsion of enemies</note>. **॥ 21 ॥**

<term>Punarvasu<gloss>The seventh Lunar Mansion, associated with the return of light and renewal</gloss></term>: Under Punarvasu, one should perform rituals for peace <term>Shanti<gloss>Ceremonies to pacify negative planetary influences or ill omens</gloss></term>, strengthening rituals, travel, wearing ornaments, architecture, religious vows, the acquisition of vehicles, and the pursuit of higher learning. **॥ 22 ॥**

<term>Pushya<gloss>The eighth Lunar Mansion, considered the best of all stars for most activities, except marriage</gloss></term>: All movable and fixed works, peace rituals, and festivals for prosperity may be conducted here. One may perform dances and theatrical arts, but one must exclude marriage under the Pushya mansion. **॥ 23 ॥**

<term>Ashlesha<gloss>The ninth Lunar Mansion, symbolized by a coiled serpent, associated with secrets and intensity</gloss></term>: In Ashlesha, one may strike at enemies, conduct commerce, or perform daring acts. It is also a time for dealings with poison or snakes, theft, and acts of deceit. **॥ 24 ॥**

<term>Magha<gloss>The tenth Lunar Mansion, associated with the "Pitris" or ancestral spirits</gloss></term>: Under Magha, one should perform ancestral rites <note>original: "paittrikam karyam"</note>, the planting of crops, water-related projects, marriages, and funeral ceremonies <term>Shraddha<gloss>Rituals performed to honor and nourish deceased ancestors</gloss></term>, as well as acts of boldness. **॥ 25 ॥**

6
</margin>

<term>Purva Phalguni<gloss>The eleventh Lunar Mansion, associated with rest, relaxation, and the arts</gloss></term>: During Purva Phalguni, one should attend to matters of imprisonment, harsh deeds, craftsmanship, deceitful maneuvers, and protection. Creative works such as painting are also perfected here. **॥ 26 ॥**

<term>Uttara Phalguni<gloss>The twelfth Lunar Mansion, associated with patronage and the rewards of hard work</gloss></term>: Under the mansion of Uttara Phalguni, one should perform marriages, take religious vows, engage in permanent or fixed works, make ornaments, begin the construction of a house, or enter a new home for the first time. **॥ 27 ॥**

<term>Hasta<gloss>The thirteenth Lunar Mansion, symbolized by a hand, associated with the Sun and skill</gloss></term>: In the mansion of the Sun <note>original: "ravibhe," referring to Hasta</note>, one should carry out travel, learning, marriage, the making of ornaments or clothing, the beginning of house construction, and rituals of consecration. **॥ 28 ॥**

<meta>This page continues the catalog of auspicious activities assigned to the Lunar Mansions (Nakshatras), picking up mid-sequence with Chitra. It maintains the instructional tone of the *Muhurta Ganapati*, advising the practitioner on the ideal timing for professional, domestic, and military tasks.</meta>

...rituals for nourishment, craftsmanship, <term>Vastu<gloss>the traditional Indian system of architecture and environmental harmony</gloss></term>, and matters concerning land and marriage. One should perform religious vows, ceremonial piercings, stable works, and farming <note>original: "rushi," a regional or scribe's variant of "krishi," meaning agriculture</note> under the mansion of **Chitra**. **॥ 29 ॥**

For the construction of temples <note>original: "surasadma," literally 'the dwelling of the gods'</note>, auspicious ceremonies, selecting a bride, and the wearing of ornaments, as well as sowing seeds, engaging in combat, and handling weapons, one should act when the Moon is in the mansion of **Svati**. **॥ 30 ॥**

Architecture for houses, the donning of jewelry, <gloss>possibly referring to painting or artistic work</gloss> crafts, and striking or crushing tasks are suited here. In the mansion of **Vishakha**, one should undertake work involving chariots, farming, and the administration of medicine. **॥ 31 ॥**

Commerce, the undertaking of vows, the fashioning of ritual vessels, and the decoration of elephants, horses, and men; any task that is intended to be stable and auspicious should be performed by the wise in the mansion of **Anuradha**. **॥ 32 ॥**

For the slaying of enemies, creating discord among foes, for striking blows, and for smithing or iron-work; for craftsmanship and rituals involving oils <note>original: "sneha-vidhau," which can also refer to acts of friendship or affection</note>, the mansion of **Jyeshtha** is declared to be the most excellent. **॥ 33 ॥**

Training intoxicated elephants, planting gardens, engaging in battle, making treaties or declaring war, and the construction of step-wells, wells, and ponds, as well as farming, are all to be performed— **॥ 34 ॥**

Indeed, the building of step-wells and wells, farming, war, ceremonial piercing, and the liberation of captives; likewise, harsh deeds and the cutting of trees should be carried out in the mansion of **Purva Ashadha**. **॥ 35 ॥**

The installation of idols, the building of pavilions, architectural planning, entering a new home, and the first use of weapons; sowing seeds and the wearing of ornaments should be done in the mansion of **Uttara Ashadha**. **॥ 36 ॥**

The consecration of deities, house construction, rituals for prosperity, and craftsmanship; auspicious trade, painting, and rites for peace should be conducted in **Shravana**. **॥ 37 ॥**

For rituals of nourishment, the <term>Upanayana<gloss>the sacred thread ceremony marking the start of formal education</gloss></term>, war, and the management of elephants, horses, and camels; for the building of homes and gardens and the wearing of jewelry, the mansion of **Dhanishtha** is said to be the best. **॥ 38 ॥**

Regarding elephants, horses, chariots, and boats; for work with silver, pearls, and weaponry; and for architecture, battle, and the preparation of arms, one should perform such tasks in **Shatabhisha**. **॥ 39 ॥**

In the mansion of **Purva Bhadrapada**, one should engage in crafts, daring or risky deeds, cutting, and farming; the sale of buffaloes and the construction of water-machines <note>original: "jala-yantra," referring to fountains, irrigation systems, or mechanical waterworks</note> are also favored...

<summary>This page details the specific worldly and religious activities—ranging from temple construction and military strikes to farming and jewelry making—that are auspiciously timed to the lunar mansions from Chitra to Purva Bhadrapada.</summary>
<keywords>Nakshatra, Muhurta, Vastu Shastra, electional astrology, temple construction, irrigation, agriculture, Jyeshtha, Shravana, Shatabhisha</keywords>

<meta>This page continues the manual of electional astrology (Muhurta), concluding the specific list of Lunar Mansions (Nakshatras) before pivoting to a general classification system. It maintains the instructional tone of a practitioner's guidebook.</meta>

<margin>Muhurta Ganapati 7
7</margin>

Marriage, the ceremonial piercing associated with vows <note>original: "vratavedha," likely referring to the ritual ear-piercing (Karnavedha) performed during childhood or initiation</note>, the installation of deities, house construction, architecture, and coronations should occur in those [the Bhadrapada mansions]. **॥ 41 ॥**

Success is attained in the mansion of **Revati** for works involving houses, temples, royal affairs, marriage, the performance of vows, and activities concerning water or land. **॥ 42 ॥**

### **Now, the Praise of the Mansion Pushya**

Even if the planets are influenced by malefic forces, and even if the Moon or the <term>Strength of the Stars<gloss>tara-bala; a calculation of the auspiciousness of a star relative to an individual's birth star</gloss></term> is lacking, all actions are successful in **Pushya** <note>original: "Pushpa," a common scribe’s variant for the mansion Pushya</note>, with the single exception of marriage <note>original: "karagrahe," literally 'taking the hand'</note>. **॥ 43 ॥**

Pushya is the leader of the stars. Even if the Moon is in Leo or is otherwise ill-placed, all works are accomplished in my mansion of Pushya. **॥ 44 ॥**

### **Now, the Special Classifications of the Mansions: "Fixed" and "Steady"**

The mansion **Rohini** and the three "Uttara" mansions <note>Uttara-Phalguni, Uttara-Ashadha, and Uttara-Bhadrapada</note> are known as **Fixed**. In these, one should perform stable actions such as planting gardens, building houses or villages, and coronations. **॥ 45 ॥**

**Mrigashira**, **Anuradha**, **Chitra** <insert>and Revati</insert>, and **Rohini** <note>The text includes Rohini here as well, though it is usually strictly 'Fixed'; this may reflect a regional tradition or a scribe's emphasis on its auspiciousness</note> are categorized as **Soft and Friendly**. In these, one should perform music, arts, marriage, and various auspicious rituals. **॥ 46 ॥**

**Pushya**, **Ashvini**, **Abhijit**, and **Hasta** are declared to be **Light and Swift**. In these, one finds success in medical knowledge, crafts, sports, and pleasures. **॥ 47 ॥**

**Jyeshtha**, **Ardra**, **Mula**, and **Ashlesha** are called **Sharp and Dreadful**. In these, one should carry out acts of division, killing, imprisonment, and the mastery of deceitful or ghostly rituals. **॥ 48 ॥**
<margin>Re-</margin>

The three beginning with **Shravana** <note>Shravana, Dhanishta, and Shatabhisha</note>, along with **Svati** and **Punarvasu**, are the **Movable** mansions. In these, one should handle horses, elephants, gardens, camels, and travel. **॥ 49 ॥**

**Bharani**, **Magha**, and the "Purva" mansions <note>Purva-Phalguni, Purva-Ashadha, and Purva-Bhadrapada</note> are described as **Cruel and Fierce**. They are suited for acts involving poison, weapons, fire, striking, and the destruction of enemies. **॥ 50 ॥**
<margin>Rama</margin>

**Vishakha** and **Krittika** are called **Mixed and Ordinary**. The ritual of releasing a bull <note>original: "vṛṣotsarga," a Vedic rite of atonement or honoring ancestors</note> and other common tasks are accomplished in these. **॥ 51 ॥**

# **The Downward, Upward, and Sideward-facing Stars and Their Fruits**

### **The Downward-facing Stars**
The Lunar Mansions of **Mula, Magha, the two Purvas** <note>Purva-Phalguni and Purva-Ashadha</note>, **Vishakha, and the two Bharanis** <note>Bharani and its associated pair, often implying Krittika in certain contexts, though here listed as a group</note> are considered **Downward-facing**. **॥ 53 ॥**

In these mansions, one should perform activities directed toward the earth, such as digging wells, small tanks, or large ponds. They are also suitable for the extraction of hidden treasures, entering underground cavities <note>original: "randhra-pravesha," likely referring to mining or exploring tunnels</note>, and performing mathematical calculations.

### **The Sideward-facing Stars**
**Punarvasu, Anuradha, Jyeshtha, the three beginning with Hasta** <note>Hasta, Chitra, and Swati</note>, **Mrigashira, and the two ending with Revati** <note>Revati and likely Ashwini</note> are **Sideward-facing** <note>original: "tiryak," meaning horizontal or across</note>. When the Moon is in these stars, one should perform the following actions: **॥ 54 ॥**

The training or movement of elephants, horses, and chariots; the operation of machinery; the launching or travel of boats; and the use of palanquins, plows, and other mechanical tools are all understood by the wise to be performed in these Sideward-facing mansions. **॥ 55 ॥**

### **The Upward-facing Stars**
**The three Uttaras** <note>Uttara-Phalguni, Uttara-Ashadha, and Uttara-Bhadrapada</note>, **Rohini, Pushya, Ardra, and the three Shravanas** <note>Shravana, Dhanishtha, and Shatabhisha</note> are the **Upward-facing** mansions. <margin>Planets and Houses</margin> The wise have declared that activities directed upward should be performed in these stars: **॥ 56 ॥**

The construction of palaces <note>or temples</note>, the raising of banners, the crafting of ceremonial umbrellas, the building of boundary walls and archways, the coronation of kings, and the planting of gardens or tall structures should be undertaken at these times. **॥ 57 ॥**

### **The Number of Stars in Each Mansion**
The counts of stars are: **three, three, six, five, three, one, four, three, five, and five**; followed by **two, four, three, and three**; and **one hundred with two** <note>referring to Shatabhisha</note>, and finally **thirty-two**. **॥ 58 ॥** These are the number of stars in the mansions starting from **Ashwini**, given in their proper sequence. <margin>54 mansions <note>This likely refers to a specific sub-count or total in a different system</note></margin>

### **The Symbolic Shapes of the Mansions**
**Ashwini** is shaped like a **horse's head**.
**Bharani** resembles a **womb** <note>original: "bhaga," referring to the female reproductive organ</note>. **॥ 60 ॥**
**Krittika** is known to look like a **razor** <note>original: "sura," meaning a blade or sharp tool</note>, while **Rohini** has the appearance of a **cart**.
**Mrigashira** is shaped like the **head of a deer**, and **Ardra** resembles a **shining gem**. **॥ 61 ॥**
**Punarvasu** is like a **house**, and **Pushya** is shaped like an **arrow**.
**Ashlesha** is in the form of a **wheel**, while **Magha** is remembered as looking like a **palatial hall**. **॥ 62 ॥**
Both **Phalgunis** <note>Purva and Uttara</note> are shaped like **carts**, and **Hasta** is in the form of a **hand**. **Chitra** appears like a **pearl**.

<insert>Numbers for star counts: 2, 2, 0, 1, 2, 2, 3, 4, 5, 6, 100 with 2, 32. 58</insert>

### **Knowledge of the Stars during Day and Night: The Paths of the Stars**

The two **Purva-Ashadhas**, **Mula**, **Anuradha**, the swift **Hasta** <gloss>corrected from "kara"</gloss>, **Jyeshtha**, **Punarvasu**, and **Ashlesha**—these stars are known to travel along the **Southern Path**. **॥ 67 ॥** **Rohini**, **Krittika**, **Mrigashira**, **Revati**, and the three beginning with **Shravana** <note>Shravana, Dhanishtha, and Shatabhishak</note>, **Pushya**, and **Ashwini**—these are the stars that travel the **Middle Path**. **॥ 68 ॥** **Ashwini**, **Aditi** <note>another name for Punarvasu</note>, **Magha**, **Vishakha**, the two **Phalgunis**, and the two **Bhadrapadas**—these are the stars that travel the **North Path**. **॥ 69 ॥**

### **Knowledge of the Mid-Heaven Star from the Rising Star**

<margin>Mo</margin> When the three stars beginning with **Vishakha**, the **Purva-Ashadhas**, or **Ashlesha** are rising, the star at the mid-heaven <note>original: "nabho-madhya," the zenith or meridian</note> should be understood as follows: **॥ 70 ॥** When **Rohini** is rising, the star at the zenith is the ninth from it. When **Ashlesha** is rising, it is the ninth. For the remaining stars, the star at the zenith is the seventh from the rising star. **॥ 71 ॥**

### **Knowledge of the Zenith and Rising Stars Relative to the Sun**

To find the star at the zenith <margin>Calculated</margin> relative to the Sun's position, subtract the Sun's star from the Northern star... <note>The text here becomes highly technical, involving a formula where the number 9 or 15 is subtracted or divided to calculate celestial positions</note>. **॥ 72 ॥** This determines the number of *nadis* <note>a unit of time equal to 24 minutes</note> passed in the night. Alternatively, from the setting star to the Sun's star, subtract the lunar day <note>original: "tithi"</note>...

Subtract the degrees of the planets from the target position. If the remainder is excessive, one should calculate the remaining time of the night for clarity. Subtract seven, eight, or nine accordingly, based on the precise measurements of the sky. **॥ 79 ॥** In the middle of the stars, this specific distinction should be applied by the wise through skillful reasoning.

### **The Five-fold Period**

<margin>Ga</margin> A wise person should avoid the five stars starting from **Dhanishtha** <note>The "Panchaka" stars are Dhanishtha, Shatabhishak, Purva-Bhadrapada, Uttara-Bhadrapada, and Revati</note> for certain purposes: traveling toward the Southern direction, making or repairing a bed, building a house, raising a structural pillar, cremating a corpse, and collecting grass or wood. If these acts are done during this time, their effects—and the necessity to repeat them—will be multiplied five-fold.

### **The Classification of Stars as Blind and Other Vision Types**

<margin>De</margin> The stars are categorized as **Blind**, **Dim-sighted**, **Middle-sighted**, and **Good-sighted**. To find them, count in groups of seven starting from the star **Rohini**. In a **Blind** star <note>original: "Andha-nakshatra"</note>, there is a gain of wealth. In a **Dim-sighted** star, there is slow or little gain. In a **Middle-sighted** star, one hears news of the object but does not attain it. In a **Good-sighted** star, there is no gain at all. **॥ 80 ॥**

When an object is lost: if it occurs under **Blind** stars, the item is nearby. If under **Dim-sighted**, it is to the South. If under **Middle-sighted**, it is to the West. If under **Good-sighted**, the item is to the North. **॥ 81 ॥**

<margin>Hu</margin> ### **The Auspicious Moments of Day and Night**

The fifteen <term>Muhurtas</term> <note>A Muhurta is a traditional unit of time lasting approximately 48 minutes</note> of the day are governed by these deities in order: Shiva, Hari, Mitra, the Ancestors, the Vasus, the Universal Gods, Vidhi, Indra, Indra-Agni, the Lord of Demons <note>Nirriti</note>, Yama, and Bhaga. These are the fifteen moments declared for the day. **॥ 83 ॥**

The night moments are governed by: Rudra, Ajaikapad, Ahirbudhnya, the Ashvins, Yama, Agni, Aditi, Brihaspati, the Universal Gods, Vishnu, the Sun, and the Wind. **॥ 83 ॥** <note>The manuscript repeats verse number 83</note>. The length of the day and the night should be divided into fifteen parts to find the duration of each moment. <margin>Perform</margin> In this fourth part of the calculation, one should perform the prescribed tasks. **॥ 84 ॥**

In the middle of the day, there is a moment known as **Abhijit**. Even if a multitude of astrological faults are present, it is considered auspicious to perform all types of good work during this time, with the sole exception of traveling toward the South. **॥ 85 ॥**

### **Moments to be Avoided on specific Days of the Week**

On **Sunday**, the moments of Aryaman and Bhaga should be avoided. On **Monday**, avoid the moments of the Serpent and the Demon <note>Nirriti</note>. On **Tuesday**, avoid the moments of the Ancestors and Agni. On **Wednesday**, avoid the moments of the Moon’s eye and the **Abhijit** moment. **॥ 86 ॥** On Thursday...

### **The Chapter on Astrological Alignments (Yogas)**

<margin>L 2</margin> The twenty-seven alignments <note>original: "Yogas"</note> are as follows: **Vishkambha** (The Support), **Priti** (Affection), **Ayushman** (The Long-lived), **Saubhagya** (Good Fortune), **Shobhana** (Splendor), **Atiganda** (The Great Obstacle), **Sukarma** (Virtuous Action), **Dhriti** (Constancy), and **Shula** (The Spear). **1** Then **Ganda** (The Knot), **Vriddhi** (Growth), **Dhruva** (The Fixed), **Vyaghata** (The Striking), **Harshana** (Joy), **Vajra** (The Thunderbolt), **Siddhi** (Success), **Vyatipata** (Calamity), and **Variyan** (The Excellent). **22** Then **Shiva** (The Auspicious), **Siddhi** (Achievement) <note>usually listed as 'Siddha'</note>, **Sadhya** (The Amenable), **Shubha** (The Lucky), **Shukla** (The Bright), **Brahma** (The Divine), **Indra** (Lordship), and finally **Vaidhriti** (The Disturbance). The results of these alignments should be understood to match their very names. <note>In Vedic astrology, a person's character or the day's "flavor" is often interpreted through the meaning of the Yoga name active at that time.</note>

-> **Portions to be Avoided in Unfavorable Alignments** <-

The alignments named **Vaidhriti** and **Vyatipata** are entirely inauspicious and should be avoided for all good works. In **Vajra** and **Vishkambha**, one should avoid the first three *ghatikas* <note>A term for a 24-minute period; three ghatikas equal 1 hour and 12 minutes</note>. In **Parigha**, avoid the first half. In **Vyaghata**, avoid nine *ghatikas*. In **Ganda** and **Atiganda**, six *ghatikas* should be discarded. <margin>5</margin> All of **Sukarma** is considered acceptable. For the remaining parts of these alignments, the effects are considered ordinary. Some authorities say that for any unfavorable alignment, one should simply avoid the first quarter or half.

-> **The Chapter on Half-Lunar Days (Karanas)** <-

There are seven "moving" half-lunar days <note>original: "Karanas"</note>: **Bava**, **Balava**, **Kaulava**, **Taitila**, **Gara**, **Vanija**, and **Vishti**. These follow the lunar days in sequence. <margin>25</margin> Starting from the first day of the bright fortnight, the **Bava** Karana occurs in the first half of the day, followed by the next in the second half. **2** After the second half of the fourteenth day of the dark fortnight, the "fixed" Karana **Shakuni** occurs. Then follow **Chatushpada**, **Naga**, and **Kinstughna** during the four halves of the dark moon period <note>Amavasya</note>. **3** These appear before the start of the next bright fortnight. <margin>Rama</margin>

### **Specific Rules for Bhadra** 

In the bright fortnight <note>original: "shukla paksha"</note>, **Bhadra** <note>The Vishti Karana, personified as a fierce goddess</note> occurs in the second half of the 4th and 11th lunar days; it is also said to occur in the first half of the 8th and 15th (Full Moon) days. **9** In the dark fortnight <note>original: "krishna paksha"</note>, it occurs in the second half of the 3rd and 10th lunar days, and in the first half of the 7th and 14th days. **11** 

### **Now, the Divisions of Bhadra’s Limbs and their Results** 

Five units of time <note>original: "nadis", a unit of 24 minutes</note> are in the **mouth**, one in the **throat**, eleven in the **heart**, five in the **navel**, six in the **waist**, and three units in the **tail**. **12** The mouth causes the destruction of work; the throat leads to the loss of wealth; the heart leads to the loss of intellect and to strife; while the tail brings victory. Therefore, in these specific results, one should strictly avoid only the time associated with the mouth. **13** 

### **Now, the Directional Location of Bhadra** 

According to the lunar days and the watches of the day <note>original: "yama"</note>... **14** ...one should know the directions starting from the East in order. One should recognize the mouth as poisonous, whereas the rest of the body is auspicious. **26** 

### **Now, the Tail of Bhadra**

<meta>This page continues the technical analysis of the "Bhadra" (also known as Vishti), an astrological period usually considered inauspicious. While the previous page introduced the Karanas (half-lunar days), this section provides the specific timing, mythological origin, and physical description of Bhadra, as well as the "exceptions" where its negative influence is neutralized based on the Moon's zodiacal position.</meta>

<margin>
Mu. Ga.
10
</margin>

[Bhadra occurs] on the 4th [lunar day] in the 8th *yama* <note>A *yama* is a three-hour period; the 8th yama is the final part of the day</note>; on the 8th day in the 1st yama; on the 11th in the 6th yama; and on the Full Moon day <note>original: "Purnima"</note> in the 4th yama. **18**

At the very end of these periods, the last three *ghatis* <note>A *ghati* is 24 minutes; three ghatis equal 72 minutes</note> are known as the "Tail" <note>original: "puccha"</note> of the Nakshatra; these are considered auspicious. On the 3rd lunar day, it occurs in the 7th [yama]; on the 7th day, in the 2nd; on the 10th day, in the 5th; and on the 14th day, in the 4th. In the "Fortnight of Vishnu" <note>The bright fortnight or Shukla Paksha</note>, these times are victorious in battle, but other auspicious works should be performed elsewhere. **19**

### **Now, the Nature and Form of Bhadra**

During the ancient war between the Gods and Demons <note>original: "Sura-Asura"</note>, she emerged from the body of Lord Shiva <note>original: "Shambhu"</note> to complete his work. She is the slayer of demons, possesses the face of a donkey, breathes out sparks of fire, and has three feet. **20**

She has the neck of a lion, is mounted upon a donkey <note>or "makes a terrifying braying sound"</note>, possesses seven hands, and has a slender waist. By the command of Shiva, she was appointed by the immortals to reside at the end of the *Karanas*. **21**

She is massive, terrifying, and possesses a wide, distorted mouth; she holds a water-pot and is fearful to behold. In any undertaking, she brings fear, appearing as restless and intense as a bolt of lightning.

### **Now, the Exceptions to the Faults of Bhadra**

If Bhadra is born in the first half of a lunar day, her effects are felt at night; if she is born in the second half, her effects are felt during the day. The "fault" of Bhadra does not apply if one's work is performed while she is "veiled" <note>i.e., performing daytime activities during a "night-Bhadra"</note>. **22**

When the Moon resides in [the zodiac signs of] Aries, Taurus, Gemini, or Scorpio, Bhadra resides in the **Heavenly Realm** <note>original: "Svarloka"</note>. When the Moon is in Virgo, Sagittarius, Libra, or Capricorn, Bhadra resides in the **Underworld** <note>original: "Rasatala"</note>. **23**

When the Moon is in Aquarius, Pisces, Cancer, or Leo, Bhadra resides on **Earth** <note>original: "Bhurloka"</note>. Only when she resides on Earth must she be strictly avoided for all tasks; when she is in Heaven or the Underworld, she is actually auspicious. **24**

During the *Vaidhriti* or *Vyatipata* <note>specific inauspicious astrological yogas</note>, or on Sundays and Tuesdays, or during periods of "corrupt stars," even a small amount of honey-like sweetness <note>marginal benefit</note> becomes extremely auspicious. **25**

### **Special Opinions Regarding Bhadra**

Some teachers state that in the bright fortnight, she is known as *Raviki* <note>Solar/Saturnian</note>, and in the dark fortnight, she is *Mangala* <note>Martial/Auspicious</note>. Others say she is *Sarpi* <note>Serpent-like</note> by day and *Vrischiki* <note>Scorpion-like</note> by night. **26**

One should always abandon the "Mouth" of Bhadra, but the "Tail" of *Raviki* is acceptable. She is considered favorable for the birth/prosperity of one's nation, the worship of the Goddess Durga, and acts of charity. **27**

In tasks such as wood-cutting and similar labors, Bhadra is actually praised and considered excellent, though she remains unfavorable for other auspicious ceremonies.

### [Mnemonic for Nakshatra quarters in Cancer: 8-5]

This is the method according to the illustrious astrologer Rama, son of Ganapati, in the <term>chapter on vertical extension</term> <note>original: "Lamba-prakarana"; refers to celestial calculations</note> of the *Muhurta Ganapati*. **Now begins the chapter on the strength of the Moon and the Stars.** <note>original: "Chandra-tara-vala-prakaranam"</note> <unclear>Always</unclear> <term>continuously</term>. <margin>White/Gora</margin>

The transit through the zodiac signs begins with Aries in sequence. From the star Ashwini until the star Revati, these are joined with the signs ending in Pisces. **1** <insert>Now follows the transit of each star (Nakshatra) through each sign.</insert>

Ashwini, Bharani, and the first quarter <note>original: "pada"; a Nakshatra is divided into four quarters</note> of Krittika constitute Aries. The remaining three quarters of Krittika and the whole of Rohini [belong to Taurus]. **2**

[The remaining parts of Mrigashira] and Ardra, along with three quarters of Punarvasu, are known as the sign of Gemini. **3** <note>The transcription contains various numerical and phonetic abbreviations for degrees here</note>.

Magha, Purva Phalguni, and the first part of Uttara Phalguni belong to Leo. The remaining three parts of Uttara Phalguni, Hasta, and the first half of Chitra belong to Virgo. <margin>Chitra 7</margin> The second half of Chitra, Swati, and the first three quarters of Vishakha constitute Libra. The last quarter of Vishakha, Anuradha, and Jyeshtha are known as Scorpio. **5**

Mula, Purva Ashadha, and the first quarter of Uttara Ashadha are Sagittarius. The remaining three quarters of Uttara Ashadha, Shravana, and the first half of Dhanishta constitute Capricorn. **6**

The last half of Dhanishta, Shatabhisha, and the first three quarters of Purva Bhadrapada are Aquarius. The last quarter of Purva Bhadrapada, Uttara Bhadrapada, and Revati are proclaimed to be the sign of Pisces. **7-8**

### ॥ Thus ends the chart of the transit of each star through the signs ॥

### ॥ Now, the Hoda-Chakra ॥
<note>The Hoda-Chakra assigns specific syllables to each quarter of a Nakshatra. These are used to determine the first letter of a person's name based on their birth star.</note>

*Chu, Che, Cho, La* are proclaimed for Ashwini. *Li, Lu, Le, Lo* are Bharani. *Vi* is Krittika. *E, U, A, I* <note>original: "Ai"</note> are known as Rohini. *Va, Vi, Vu*... *Ve, Vo, Ka, Ki*... *Ku, Gha, Nga, Cha*... <note>These represent the sounds for Mrigashira, Ardra, and Punarvasu</note>. *Ke, Ko, Ha, Hi* are the Mother of Gods [Punarvasu]. *He, Ho, Da* belong to the <term>excellent Pushya star</term>. *Di, Du, De, Do* as well as Ashlesha. *Ma, Mi, Mu, Me* are known as Magha. *Mo, Ta, Ti, Tu* are Purva Phalguni. *Ta, U, Pha, Te, To, Pa* are forgotten [assigned to Uttara Phalguni]. *Pu, Sha, Na, Ho* are proclaimed for Hasta. *Pe, Po, Ra, Ri* for Chitra. *Ru, Re, Ro, Ta* for Swati. *Ti, Tu, Te, To* for Vishakha...

### Now, the Auspicious and Inauspicious Results of the Moon
The results are determined by the position of the Moon in the zodiac signs, counting from one's own birth sign. **15** When the Moon is in the <term>Birth Sign</term> <note>The sign the Moon occupied at the moment of one's birth</note>, there is physical nourishment and strength. In the second sign, there is no happiness. In the third, there is gain of wealth. In the fourth, various calamities occur. **16** In the first <term>waxing half of the month</term> <note>original: "Shukla Paksha"; the fortnight of the increasing Moon</note>, there are special considerations. In the second and sixth signs from birth, there is a great arrival of wealth. In the seventh, one receives beauty and honor. In the eighth, death is certain <note>This usually refers to "death-like" suffering or extreme obstacles in astrological jargon</note>. In the ninth, fear is known. In the tenth, there is a flurry of activity and success in work. In the eleventh, gain of profit. In the twelfth, various calamities. **17** **Now, the specialty of the waxing half of the month:** In the waxing phase, the Moon in the second, fifth, and ninth signs is as auspicious as the planet Jupiter. In the <term>waning half of the month</term> <note>original: "Krishna Paksha"; the fortnight of the decreasing Moon</note>, there is a special rule, though other factors remain equal for both halves. **18** The strength of the Moon is primary; the influence of the Stars is not considered as significant when the Moon is strong. Just as a woman has no independence when her beloved husband is present and powerful, **20** so it is with the stars. However, in the waning half of the month, the strength of the <term>Stars</term> <note>original: "Tara"; the strength of the lunar mansion relative to the birth star</note> should always be taken, for when the "husband" (the Moon) is weak or away, the "mistress of the house" (the Star) manages the household affairs. **Now, the Moon's strength in the other half:** The bright phase is seen as powerful, while the dark phase is less so. The Moon gives auspicious results after it has passed through the bright phase, even in difficult circumstances. **22** **Now, the strength of the Stars (Tara-bala):** One must count from the birth star to the star of the day and divide by nine. The results are, in order: 1. Birth, 2. Wealth, 3. Calamity, 4. Well-being, 5. Sin/Obstacle, 6. Success, 7. Death/Destruction, 8. Friend, and 9. Great Friend. **23** These names indicate the results they grant. The birth star (number 1) is known as "middling"—it is neither highly desired nor completely rejected. **24** **Now, regarding the waning phase and the stars to be avoided:** One should avoid the stars that are obstructed <note>original: "vedha"</note>, specifically the first, fifth, third, and seventh... **25**

# = The duration of each state is eleven and a quarter nadis, starting from Ashvini 4

All "pure" states are remembered as favorable for necessary tasks. Now, the remedy for an inauspicious <term>Tara</term> <note>The "star" or lunar mansion calculated relative to one's birth star</note>. On the seventh and fifth stars, the procedure is the same. **26** To remove the "sin" or negative influence of these stars during the three "birth" mansions, one should offer gold.

### The Twelve States of the Moon
Now, the <term>Vamshavastha</term> <note>original: "vamsavastha"; refers to the twelve chronological states or "dispositions" of the Moon as it moves through the zodiac</note>:
1.  **Travel** <note>Pravasa</note>
2.  **Destruction** <note>Vinasha</note>
3.  **Death** <note>Marana</note>
4.  **Victory** <note>Jaya</note>
5.  **Laughter** <note>Hasya</note>
6.  **Passion** <note>Rati</note>
7.  **Play** <note>Krida</note>
8.  **Sleep** <note>Supta</note>
9.  **Liberation** <note>Mukta</note>
10. **Eloquent** <note>Vachya</note>
11. **Trembling** <note>Kampita</note>
12. **Stable** <note>Susthira</note>
**27-28**
These are known as the twelve states of the Moon. Their duration is remembered as eleven and a quarter <term>nadis</term> <note>One 'nadi' is 24 minutes; therefore, each state lasts 4 hours and 30 minutes</note>. Starting from the sign of Aries, these states—beginning with "Travel"—give results exactly as their names suggest. **29**

#### The Sequence of States
Here is the example of their sequence: In the sign of Aries, the Moon begins with "Travel." In Taurus, the sequence of the Moon begins with "Destruction." **30** In Gemini, it begins with "Death," and so on, cycling through the twelve signs.

### Planetary Strength for Specific Tasks
Now, the specific planetary strength for various activities, and the Moon’s strength in all matters:
*   For **marriages and festivals**: Jupiter <note>original: "Jiva"</note> is the primary strength.
*   For **meeting a king**: Jupiter. **31**
*   For **battle**: Mars <note>original: "Dharaniputra," literally 'Son of the Earth'</note>.
*   For **education and studies**: Mercury <note>original: "Budha"</note> is powerful.
*   For **travel**: Venus <note>original: "Bhargava"</note> is prescribed.
*   For **initiation** <note>Diksha</note>: Saturn <note>original: "Shanaishchara"</note>.
<margin>18</margin>
The Moon is praised by the wise as beneficial for **all tasks**.

### Special Considerations for Moon-Strength
There is a special rule regarding the Moon’s strength: The Moon is considered auspicious unless it is in the twelfth house, the seventh house from a malefic planet, or joined with a malefic. **32** It is also weak if it is placed between two malefic planets, if it is waning, if it is in its sign of debilitation, or if it falls within the division of an enemy planet.

### Remedies for Inauspicious Times
Now, the donations for bad lunar dates, weekdays, stars, and lunar positions when a task is necessary:
*   For an **inauspicious lunar date** <note>Tithi</note>: Give rice.
*   For a **bad weekday**: Give a gem with gold.
*   For a **bad star** <note>Nakshatra</note>: Give a cow.
*   For a **bad planetary alignment** <note>Yoga</note>: Give gold.
*   For a **bad ritual division** <note>Karana</note>: Give grain.
*   For an **afflicted Moon**: Give a conch shell with silver.
*   For an **inauspicious star-count** <note>Tara</note>: Give rock salt <note>original: "Saindhava"</note>. **35**
Or one may give gold measured in <term>nadis</term> when a task is absolutely necessary.

Thus ends the seventh chapter, titled **The Section on the Moon and Stars**, in the *Gems of Timing* <note>original: "Muhurta Ganapati"</note>.

### Section on Auspicious and Inauspicious Alignments
Now begins the chapter on auspicious and inauspicious combinations. First, the auspicious alignments arising from lunar dates and weekdays:
When "Nanda" dates fall on a Wednesday, "Bhadra" dates on a Saturday, or "Rikta" dates on a Tuesday—or if a task is completed on a Thursday—these are **Siddhi Yogas** <note>Alignments that bring success</note> which bring good fortune. **1**
According to the school of Vashistha, the lunar dates "Nanda," "Bhadra," "Jaya," and "Rikta" are auspicious when they fall on the days starting from the first day of the fortnight. **2**

*   **Wednesday** + Anuradha star
*   **Friday** + Revati star
*   **Saturday** + Rohini star

### Now, some exceptions regarding the Nectar-Alignment.
