# Now, the Chapter on Planetary Transits is written.

### The Specific Results for Each House Relative to a Person's Birth Sign
<note>In Jyotisha (Vedic astrology), "Gocara" refers to the current position of planets relative to the Rashi (zodiac sign) where the Moon was located at the time of a person's birth.</note>

**The Sun’s results from one's birth sign are as follows:** 
1. Arrival or gain, 2. Loss, 3. Wealth, 4. Disease, 5. Misery, 6. Happiness, 7. Movement or travel <note>original: "gati-rujah" — can also imply illness related to movement</note>, 8. Sin, 9. Happiness, 10. Wealth, 11. Success, 12. Pain. 1.

**The Moon’s results in order:**
1. Vitality/Nourishment, 2. Wealth, 3. Prosperity, 4. Disease, 5. Happiness, 6. Profit, 7. Wealth, 8. Illness, 9. Honor, 10. Happiness, 11. Pleasant things, 12. Pain. 2.

**Mars’s results are remembered as:**
1. Fear, 2. Loss, 3. Pleasant things, 4. Enmity, 5. Lack of profit, 6. Fruitless labor, 7. Sin, 8. Disease, 9. Happiness, 10. Sorrow, 11. [Gain], 12. [Loss]. 3.

**Mercury’s results are:**
1. Bondage/Imprisonment, 2. Profit, 3. Fear, 4. Wealth, 5. Sorrow, 6. Prosperity <note>original: "Lakshmi" — the goddess of fortune</note>, 7. Speed/Vehicles, 8. Wealth, 9. Disease, 10. Enjoyment, 11. Happiness, 12. Obstacles. 4.

**Jupiter’s results are:**
1. Fear, 2. Wealth, 3. Illness or fear, 4. Profit, 5. Sorrow, 6. Happiness, 7. Illness, 8. Honor, 9. Misery, 10. Wealth, 11. Disease, 12. [Loss]. 5.

**Venus’s results are declared as:**
1. Happiness, 2. Wealth, 3. Wealth, 4. Happiness, 5. Disease, 6. Pain, 7. Calamity, 8. Happiness, 9. Virtue/Duty, 10. Sorrow, 11. Wealth, 12. Profit. 6.

**Saturn’s results in order are:**
1. Fear, 2. Sorrow, 3. Pleasant things, 4. Grief, 5. Loss, 6. Happiness, 7. Fear, 8. Illness, 9. Sin, 10. Sorrow, 11. Wealth, 12. Hardship. 7.

**Rāhu and Ketu’s results in order are:**
1. Loss, 2. Expenditure, 3. Wealth, 4. Enmity, 5. Sorrow, 6. Wealth, 7. Calamity, 8. Illness, 9. Sin, 10. Enmity, 11. Happiness, 12. <gloss>Loss of possessions</gloss>. 8.

### Planets That Grant Auspicious Results
All planets are auspicious when they transit the eleventh house from the birth sign. 

The Sun is auspicious in the 3rd, 6th, and 10th houses. Mars <note>original: "Dharaputra" — Son of the Earth</note>, Rahu, Ketu, and Saturn are auspicious in the 6th and 3rd houses. 9.

The Moon is auspicious in the 10th, 7th, 3rd, 6th, and 1st houses. During the bright fortnight <term>Shukla Paksha: the fifteen days of the waxing moon</term>, it is also auspicious in the 9th, 2nd, and 5th houses. 10.

In transit, Mercury grants good results in the 10th, 8th, 4th, and 2nd houses. Jupiter is said to be auspicious in the 2nd, 5th, 9th, and 7th houses. Venus is auspicious in the 1st, 2nd, 3rd, 4th, 5th, 9th, 8th, and 12th houses. 11.

### The Sequential "Obstruction" of the Sun and Other Planets
<note>Vedha (obstruction) is a concept where a planet in a specific house cancels out the good or bad effects of another planet in a corresponding house.</note>

If the 6th and 11th houses are obstructed, a planet in the 10th or 4th house... 
<margin>22</margin> 12.

The Sun in the 3rd and 9th, and when in the 11th, the 5th house [obstructs]. 

**Now for the Moon:**
The 2nd and 7th houses, the 1st and 5th. The 6th and 12th are obstructed, as are the 10th and the 11th. The 3rd and 9th are always to be praised, along with the 10th house. 13.

The 3rd and 12th, the 6th and 9th, or the 12th and 11th. These are the obstructions for Mars, Rahu, Ketu, and Saturn. 14.

The 2nd, 5th, 4th, 3rd, and 9th... the 1st, 8th... for Mercury...

Having established the general effects of each planet in the twelve houses, the text now moves into the technicalities of "Vedha" (obstruction), the influence of the "Ashtakavarga" system on transit results, the duration of each transit, and the appropriate remedial donations (Dana) to mitigate negative planetary effects.</meta>

<page-num>१८</page-num>

Jupiter provides results in the 2nd, 5th, 4th, 8th, 11th, 7th, 3rd, 9th, and 10th houses. 17. 

Venus <note>original: "Bhargava"</note> is auspicious in the 8th, 3rd, 1st, 2nd, 4th, 11th, 9th, and 12th houses. 18.

### Obstructions to Planetary Results
<note>The concept of "Vedha" (obstruction) implies that a planet in a certain house can "pierce" or cancel the auspicious results of another planet in a corresponding house.</note>

A planet in the 2nd is obstructed by one in the 7th; the 9th by the 5th; and the 5th by the 8th. This is known as the sequence of obstruction <note>term: "Vedha" — a system where planets in specific positions relative to each other neutralize their respective effects</note>. 19.

The 11th and 8th, and the 9th and 10th houses also form pairs that obstruct one another. If a planet is blocked in this way, even a beneficial transit becomes "pierced" and fails to give its full result, unless the obstruction is "reverse" <note>original: "savilomata" — refers to specific conditions where the obstruction is bypassed</note>. 20. 

Just as the Himalaya and Vindhya mountains stand in the middle, the planet in the middle becomes the obstacle. This system of the "obstructer and the obstructed" should be understood relative to the birth sign <note>term: "Janma Rashi" — the zodiac sign the Moon occupied at birth</note>. 21.

### The Results of Planets According to the Eight-Fold Strength System
<note>term: "Ashtakavarga" — a sophisticated system of points (Rekhas) used to determine the specific strength of a planet for an individual.</note>

When a planet travels through its own Ashtakavarga and possesses a high number of points <note>original: "Rekha"</note>, then even if the transit <note>Gocara</note> is technically "wicked" or poorly placed, it will not cause much harm to the person. 22.

A planet may be "Full" <note>Sakala — strong and effective</note> when joined with benefics, or "Void" <note>Nishkala — weak</note> when joined with malefics or aspected by them. These states must be understood both in the current transit and in the original birth chart. 23.

A planet in the house of an enemy, or in its sign of debilitation <note>Nicha</note>, or moving in an adverse way, should be known as "fruitless" <note>Nishphala</note>, even if it receives a "good" aspect from another planet.

### २४। The Duration of Planetary Transits
<note>This section describes how long each celestial body stays in a single zodiac sign.</note>

| Planet | Duration in one Zodiac Sign |
| :--- | :--- |
| Venus, Mercury, and the Sun | One Month |
| Mars <note>original: "Mahisuta" — son of the Earth</note> | One and a half Months |
| Jupiter <note>original: "Guru"</note> | One Year |
| Saturn | Two and a half Years |
| Moon <note>original: "Indu"</note> | Two and a quarter Days |

25. This is the general duration for a planet's passage through a sign.

### The Timing of Planetary Results
<note>Planets do not give their results at the same time during their stay in a sign; some are active at the beginning, some in the middle, and some at the end.</note>

Mars and the Sun give results at the **beginning** of the sign; Jupiter <note>Vrihaspati</note> gives results in the **middle**; the Moon and Saturn give results at the **end**. Mercury <note>original: "Jnya"</note> is known to give results throughout the **entire** transit. 26.

The Sun's effect begins after 5 days; the Moon's after 3 ghatis <note>about 72 minutes</note>. 27. Mars gives results after 8 days, Mercury after 7 days, Jupiter after 2 months, and Saturn after 6 months. 28. Rahu <note>the North Node of the Moon</note> is said to give results throughout the month. The combination of signs and constellations <note>Nakshatras</note> provides the definitive results. 29.

### General Auspicious and Inauspicious Houses
Planets moving through the 10th, 11th, 6th, and 3rd houses from the birth sign are considered best. 30.
They are moderate in the 5th, 7th, 2nd, and 9th. 
They are not considered good in the 1st, 8th, 4th, and 12th houses; these positions may cause illness or even death. 31.

### Remedies for Afflicted Planets
Peace <note>Shanti</note> can be attained through prayer <note>Japa</note> and the donation of gold and cows when the transits are inauspicious.

**What to avoid:** When planets are in "wicked" positions, one should avoid hunting, traveling to distant lands by order of kings, riding unruly horses or elephants, or entering the houses of others. One should not perform daring or reckless acts when the planets are unfavorable. 32.

Those planets which are "wicked" in the transit, in their major period <note>Dasha</note>, or in their Ashtakavarga, may be appeased through prayer, charity, and ritual bathing.

**Donations to please the Sun:**
Rubies, cows, wheat, gold, copper, and red sandalwood should be given by leaders to please the Sun.

**Donations to please the Moon:**
Pearls, silver, white cloth, conch shells, bamboo, and rice. 18.

**Donations to please Mars:**
Coral, gold, wheat, red cloth, a bull, oleander flowers, jaggery, copper, and lentils <note>Masoor dal</note> for the satisfaction of Mars. 19.

**Donations to please Mercury:**
Ivory, gold, green cloth <note>original: "nilavaram"</note>, emeralds <note>original: "suryaka pushyam"</note>, and ghee for the pleasure of Mercury. 20.

**Donations to please Jupiter <note>Vachaspati</note>:**
Turmeric, gold, yellow cloth, yellow grain, salt, and topaz <note>Pushparaga</note>. 21.

**Donations to please Venus <note>Bhrigu</note>:**
A cow, a white horse, gold, rice, silver, and perfumes for the pleasure of Venus. 22. Sesame seeds...

<margin>24</margin> To please Saturn <note>original: "Shaneh"</note> and bring joy, one should give a sapphire <note>term: "Indranila" — a blue sapphire, the primary gemstone for Saturn</note>, black gram, a black cow, a great buffalo, or a swan. 49.

To please the son of Simhika <note>term: "Samhikeya" — a name for Rahu, the North Node of the Moon</note>, O King, one should give a hessonite <note>term: "Gomeda" — a cinnamon-colored garnet</note>, a horse, a sword, blue cloth, a hide, gold, oil, and sesame seeds. 50.

To satisfy Ketu <note>the South Node of the Moon</note>, the ruler of the earth should give musk, a blanket, weapons, a goat, a cat’s eye gem <note>term: "Vaidurya" — a chrysoberyl stone known for its chatoyancy</note>, gold, sesame seeds, and oil. 51.

### Ritual Baths for Planetary Peace
<note>The following verses describe "Graha Snana," where specific herbs and minerals are added to bathwater to neutralize the negative "heat" or influence of a planet.</note>

For the satisfaction of the Sun <note>original: "Ravi"</note>, one should bathe with water infused with cardamom, licorice, copper-colored flowers, saffron, realgar <note>original: "Manah-shila"</note>, and Himalayan cedar. 

To remove the afflictions of the Moon <note>original: "Chandra"</note>, the bath should contain the five products of the cow <note>term: "Panchagavya" — milk, curd, ghee, urine, and dung</note>, conch shells, crystal, pearls, and <unclear>Kumi</unclear> herbs. 47.

To destroy the suffering caused by Mars <note>original: "Bhaoma"</note>, one should use a bath of asafoetida <note>original: "Hingu"</note>, wood-apple <note>original: "Bilva"</note>, spikenard <note>original: "Mansi"</note>, Bakula flowers, sandalwood, and red flowers. 48.

To destroy the suffering caused by Mercury <note>original: "Bodhana"</note>, use cow dung, unbroken rice, pearls, yellow orpiment <note>original: "Rocana"</note>, honey, gold, and various fruits and roots. 46. <note>The verse numbering in the manuscript is inconsistent here.</note>

To bring joy to Jupiter <note>original: "Kuru"</note>, O King, one should bathe with jasmine flowers, white mustard, and <unclear>Lodhra</unclear> powder combined with lotus seeds. 47.

To remove the obstacles caused by Venus <note>original: "Bhuruja"</note>, a single bath with cardamom, realgar, Amalaka fruit, and saffron water is effective. 48.

To remove the afflictions of Saturn <note>original: "Pada-badha"</note>, one should bathe with water containing black sesame, oil, and dill <note>original: "Shatapushpa"</note>. 49.

For Rahu and Ketu <note>implied by "Indu-purah-sarah"</note>, use parched grain, costus <note>original: "Kustha"</note>, mustard, Himalayan cedar, turmeric, and Lodhra. 50.

All these baths are prescribed for the pacification of planetary suffering. 51. Just as a disease is destroyed when a medicine is properly consecrated with mantras, so too do planetary faults subside through the procedure of the ritual bath. 52.

### The Planetary Metals and Gems
To please the planets, one should wear these materials on the body:
- **Venus:** Silver
- **Sun and Mars:** Coral <note>original: "Vidruma"</note>
- **Mercury:** Gold
- **Saturn:** Iron
- **Jupiter:** Pearl
- **Rahu and Ketu:** Lapis Lazuli <note>original: "Laja-varta"</note>

### The Nine-Planet Gemstone Ring
<note>term: "Navaratna Mudrika" — a talismanic ring where nine specific gems are arranged to represent the solar system.</note>

<margin>24</margin>
The arrangement of the gems in the ring is as follows:
- **Center:** Ruby <note>original: "Manikya"</note> for the Sun.
- **East:** Diamond <note>original: "Vajra"</note> for Venus.
- **Southeast:** Pearl <note>original: "Mouktika"</note> for the Moon.
- **South:** Coral <note>original: "Pravala"</note> for Mars.
- **Southwest:** Hessonite <note>original: "Gomeda"</note> for Rahu.
- **West:** Blue Sapphire <note>original: "Nila"</note> for Saturn.
- **Northwest:** Cat's Eye <note>original: "Vaidurya"</note> for Ketu.
- **North:** Yellow Topaz/Sapphire <note>original: "Pushpaka"</note> for Jupiter.
- **Northeast:** Emerald <note>original: "Garunmata"</note> for Mercury. 54-55.

Even if planets are situated in "evil" houses <note>unfavorable positions in a birth chart</note>, they will not cause suffering to a man who wears this ring, speaks truthfully, honors the gods, and maintains a pure mind. 56.

Thus ends the thirteenth chapter on Planetary Transits in the 'Muhurta Kalpalata' composed by Shri Maheshvara Rao. 13.

### On Life-Cycle Rituals
<note>term: "Samskara" — Hindu rites of passage that mark important stages of life.</note>

First, the observation of the first menstruation <note>term: "Rajo Darshanam"</note>. The auspicious months are Vaishakha, Phalguna, Magha, Margashirsha, Shravana, and Ashvin. It should occur on a beneficial day and during the daytime. The favorable stars are Shravana, Anuradha, Revati, and Mrigashirsha.

<meta>This page continues from the previous discussion on planetary pacification rituals. Having concluded the remedies for the nine planets, the text now transitions into the timing (Muhurta) for various life-cycle rituals (Samskaras), specifically focusing on women’s health, conception, and the early stages of pregnancy and birth.</meta>

In the three stars of the Hand <note>term: "Hasta-traya" — the lunar mansions of Hasta, Chitra, and Svati</note>, in the Chariot <note>term: "Rohini"</note>, in the Nourisher <note>term: "Pushya"</note>, and in the three "Latter" stars <note>term: "Uttaras" — Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, it is auspicious for women to wear white garments upon the first appearance of their menses. The results are mediocre in the stars of the Eldest <note>term: "Jyeshtha"</note>, the Return of Wealth <note>term: "Punarvasu"</note>, and the Root <note>term: "Mula"</note>; in other stars, it is condemned. The eighth, twelfth, and sixth lunar days, the "empty" days <note>term: "Rikta" — the 4th, 9th, and 14th lunar days</note>, the new moon, and the time of the Sun's transit into a new sign are avoided. The astrological "Obstacle" <note>term: "Bhadra"</note>, the "Sleep" <note>term: "Nidra"</note>, the "Great Calamity" <note>term: "Vyatipata"</note>, and during an eclipse <unclear>while suffering from disease</unclear>. If the first menses appear at twilight, in a forbidden land, or while wearing black clothes, it is not desired; however, it can become auspicious through the performance of pacification rituals <note>term: "Shanti"</note>. When the faults mentioned previously are present at the first appearance of menses, all these defects are destroyed if the rising sign <note>term: "Lagna"</note> is associated with a beneficial planet.

**Now, the Ritual Bath after Menstruation.**
In the stars of Punarvasu, Chitra, Jyeshtha, Pushya, and the Ashvins <note>original: "Bhishak"</note>, a menstruating woman should bathe on an auspicious day and an auspicious lunar date. In Rohini, the two Mrigashiras, Svati, Hasta, and the two Revatis, a young woman who bathes there conceives a child and attains prosperity <note>original: "Lakshmi"</note>.

**Now, the Rite of Conception.**
<note>term: "Garbhadhana" — the first of the sixteen traditional life-cycle rituals, performed to ensure a healthy conception.</note>
The birth star, the stars of Bharani and Mula are considered harmful, as are Revati and Magha. Likewise, avoid the malefic astrological combinations of Vyatipata, Vaidhriti, and Parigha. Avoid the twilight, the daytime, the sixth lunar day, and festival days. One should also avoid the "Cutter" <note>term: "Vishti" or Bhadra</note>, the "empty" lunar days, and the Sun's transit <note>term: "Sankranti"</note> for conception. Similarly, one should avoid the "cruel" weekdays <note>Tuesday, Saturday, and Sunday</note>. The stars of Svati, Hasta, Anuradha, Rohini, and the three Shravanas, as well as the "Latter" stars and Mrigashira, are praised for **Conception** <note>term: "Garbhadhana"</note> on an auspicious day to produce a child, especially when the Moon and the Ascendant are in masculine divisions. 

When the beneficial planets are in the angles <note>term: "Kendra" — 1st, 4th, 7th, 10th houses</note> or trines <note>term: "Trikona" — 1st, 5th, 9th houses</note>, and the malefic planets are in the 3rd, 6th, or 11th houses, in a masculine sign or masculine division, and when the Ascendant is aspected by masculine planets: the rite of **Quickening** <note>term: "Pumsavana" — a ritual performed to favor the birth of a male child and for the healthy development of the fetus</note> is traditionally performed in the second or third month. In the sixth or eighth month of the first pregnancy, the **Parting of the Hair** <note>term: "Simantonnayana" — a ritual performed for the mother's mental health and the fetus's protection</note> is known as *Shrimantaka*. 

The stars of Punarvasu, Mula, Shravana, Mrigashira, and Hasta, and the weekdays of Jupiter, Mars, and the Sun are stated to be auspicious for the Quickening rite. The wise say it should be done when the Moon and the Lord of the Month are strong, and the Ascendant is in a masculine division. When beneficial planets are in the angles or trines and malefics are in the 11th, 3rd, or 6th houses. The lords of the months [of pregnancy] are Venus, Mars, Jupiter, the Sun, the Moon, Saturn, Mercury, the Lord of the Ascendant, the Moon, and the Sun respectively. For marriage and for the rituals of the womb, the purity of the Moon is required for the woman, and the strength of the Sun is required for the man. In the rituals of conception and the **First Feeding of Solid Food** <note>term: "Annaprashana"</note> for the infant, the combustions of Jupiter and Venus or the presence of an intercalary month <note>term: "Adhika-masa"</note> do not act as defects.

**Now, the Worship of Vishnu.**
During the **Parting of the Hair** <note>term: "Simanta"</note> rite, in the bright fortnight, under the stars of Rohini or Shravana, and on the twelfth or seventh lunar day, one should offer garments to Vishnu for his satisfaction.

**Now, the Entry into the Maternity Chamber.**
In the three Shravanas, the three Uttaras, the three Hastas, Pushya, or Anuradha, or in Punarvasu, Rohini, or the two Revatis, one should enter the **Maternity** <note>term: "Sutika"</note> chamber on an auspicious day suitable for childbirth. After the **Birth Rituals** <note>term: "Jatakarman"</note> are performed, one should observe the birth-impurity period. On the eleventh or twelfth day, the **Naming Ceremony** <note>term: "Namakarana"</note> should be performed according to the rules. In the two Revatis, Pushya, Punarvasu, Anuradha, the three Shravanas, the three Hastas, the two Rohinis, or <unclear>the birth star</unclear>...

<meta>This page transitions from the general timing of life-cycle rituals to the specific astrological dangers surrounding childbirth. It focuses on the transition between the lunar mansions of Jyeshtha and Mula, known for their volatile spiritual energy, and prescribes specific days for the care and ritual bathing of the new mother.</meta>

<page-num>25</page-num>

Experts understand the <unclear>highest</unclear> degree of the first star <note>term: "Jyeshtha" — literally 'the eldest'</note> to be the point of transition. The final two units of time <note>term: "Ghatika" — a unit of 24 minutes</note> of the star Jyeshtha and the first two units of the star Mula <note>term: "Mula" — 'the root'</note> are known as the "Inedible Root" <note>term: "Abhukta-mula" — a particularly inauspicious astrological junction</note>; so says the sage Narada. However, the sage Vasistha says the junction consists of the first day of the transition, while Angiras says it is one unit of time, and others say five. The father should avoid or not look upon a child born during this time for six months or a year. <insert>27</insert>. If born in the first quarter of the Root star, the father is affected; in the second, the mother; in the third, wealth is destroyed; and in the fourth, it brings auspiciousness to oneself. <insert>28</insert>.

In the months of Magha, Ashadha, Ashvin, and Bhadrapada, the Root star resides in heaven. In Kartika, Shravana, Chaitra, and Pausha, it resides on the earth. In Vaishakha, Phalguna, Jyeshtha, and Margashirsha, it is situated in the underworld. The defect <note>term: "Dosha" — a flaw or negative astrological influence</note> is only known to be active when it resides on the earth; otherwise, there is no fault. <insert>30</insert>. 

What has been said regarding the quarters of the Root star is the opposite for the Eldest star <note>term: "Jyeshtha"</note>; a birth in its final three parts is inauspicious. <insert>31</insert>. Similarly, the birth of twins <note>original: "yamala"</note> is not considered auspicious. 

### The Results of the Quarters of the Eldest Star
The first quarter destroys the elder brother; the second destroys the younger brother. <insert>32</insert>. The third destroys the mother, and the child born in the fourth destroys himself. The astrological junctions <note>term: "Gandanta"</note>, and the configurations of "Iron Bar" <note>term: "Parigha"</note>, "Spear" <note>term: "Shula"</note>, "Great Calamity" <note>term: "Vyatipata"</note>, and "Total Darkness" <note>term: "Vaidhriti"</note>; the stars of Mula, Ashlesha, and Jyeshtha; the "Bell of Death" <note>term: "Yamaghanta"</note>, the Sun’s transit, the "Thunderbolt Bell" <note>term: "Vajra-ghanta"</note>, the day of Death, the "Obstacle" <note>term: "Bhadra"</note>, the "Killing Blow" <note>term: "Vyaghata"</note>, and the "Burnt Day" — birth during these, or on the fourteenth day of the dark fortnight, or during the birth-star of a sibling, is also not desired. Similarly, the "Omitted Day" and the "Triple-Defect" <note>term: "Trika"</note> at the time of a child's birth are undesired. To remove these faults, one should perform a pacification ritual <note>term: "Shanti"</note> according to the traditional rites. 

If a fourth child is a girl after three sons, <insert>36</insert>, or if a fourth child is a boy after three girls, that is called the "Triple-Defect." According to the principles of horoscopy, I shall speak again on the star of the "Return of Wealth" <note>term: "Punarvasu"</note>. <insert>37</insert>. Having abandoned independence, a virtuous day is declared auspicious.

**Now, the Care of the Woman in Childbed.**
When the stars and days mentioned by the doctors are free from malefic influences, <insert>39</insert>, rituals should be performed for the health of the woman in childbed and her child. On an auspicious day under the stars prescribed for the first feeding of solid food, and on the garland-bearing day of the Earth, avoiding the "empty" lunar days and bad planetary combinations, the birth-ritual is performed. On the fifth day from birth, the mother's delivery-room rituals occur at night. On the sixth day, the Goddess of the Sixth <note>term: "Shashtika"</note> is worshipped with songs, keeping vigil, and other festivities. <insert>40</insert>.

**Now, the Ritual Bath after Childbirth.**
In the stars of the Hand <note>term: "Hasta"</note>, the Deer’s Head <note>term: "Mrigashira"</note>, the Heart of the Lotus <note>term: "Anuradha"</note>, the Chariot <note>term: "Rohini"</note>, the Wealthy <note>term: "Revati"</note>, the star of Wealth <note>term: "Dhanishtha"</note>, the three "Latter" stars <note>term: "Uttaras"</note>, and the Sword <note>term: "Svati"</note>, on the days of Jupiter, the Sun, or Mars.

It also outlines the timing for postpartum care and the ritual worship of the Goddess Shashti on the sixth night after birth.</summary>

<meta>This page continues the sequence of postnatal rites (samskaras) for a newborn and the mother. Following the discussion of the "Inedible Root" (Abhukta-mula) star on the previous page, the text now transitions into the timing for the mother’s ritual bath, the child’s first outing, and the astrological implications of early teething.</meta>

### On the Commendable Ritual Bath of the Mother 

The ritual bath of the new mother <note>term: "Suti-snana" — a purifying bath taken by the mother after the period of birth-impurity</note> is favorable under the lunar mansions <note>term: "Nakshatra" — one of the 27 sectors of the lunar path</note> of Ardra, Shravana, Vishakha, Bharani, Mula, Chitra, Krittika, and Magha. 

42. One should avoid the "Empty" days <note>term: "Rikta" — the 4th, 9th, and 14th days of the lunar fortnight, considered hollow or unlucky</note>, Wednesdays, Saturdays, and the sixth, twelfth, and eighth lunar days. 

### On the Results of the Eruption of Teeth

Now, regarding the results of the eruption of teeth <note>term: "Dantotpatti" — the appearance of the first milk teeth</note>: if they appear in the first month, it affects the child's own self; in the second month, it affects the younger brother. 43. In the third month, it affects the sister; in the fourth, the mother; in the fifth, it kills the elder brothers <note>original: "hani hanyadrisha" — implies destruction or severe ill-fortune</note>. Teething during these times is not considered auspicious by the wise. 44. From the sixth month onward, it brings enjoyments and is always favorable.

### On the Worship of Water

Now, for the Worship of Water <note>term: "Jala-puja" — a ritual usually involving the local well or water source after childbirth</note>: it should be done under the two Punarvasus, Hasta, Mrigashirsha, Mula, and Anuradha. 45. Shravana, Jupiter <note>Thursday</note>, Mercury <note>Wednesday</note>, or the Moon <note>Monday</note>, and the seventh lunar day are suitable for Water Worship. It is auspicious when Jupiter, Venus, or Mercury are rising, and in the months of Chaitra and Pausha. 46. One should not perform the worship of water if the month has already been completed or if the day is otherwise forbidden. 

### On Placing the Child in the Cradle

Now, for the Placing in the Cradle <note>term: "Dolarohana"</note>: one should perform the placing in the cradle under the same lunar mansions prescribed for the first breastfeeding. 

<margin>
In the months of Chaitra and Pausha, it is auspicious.
</margin>

On an auspicious day from the birth of the child, 47. avoid the Sun's day <note>Sunday</note> or the Saturn's day <note>Saturday</note>. Then, on the fifteenth day, or according to the father's tradition, the child is placed in the cradle. In the bed, the mother or a woman whose husband is living <note>term: "Suvasini" — a woman whose presence is considered auspicious at rituals</note> should meditate on the Lord in his state of cosmic sleep <note>term: "Yogashayi" — refers to Vishnu sleeping on the serpent Shesha</note> and place the infant with their head toward the East. 48. 

The first feeding of solid food <note>term: "Annaprashana"</note> is mentioned for the twenty-first day or during an auspicious season. Under the preceding two "moveable" stars, the drinking of milk is traditionally held. 50. 

The First Outing <note>term: "Nishkramana" — the first time the baby is taken out of the house</note> should be in the fourth month, on a day deemed auspicious for travel. Or, one may perform it on the twelfth day from birth, preceded by auspicious ceremonies. 51. <gloss>A yellow stain covers the text here</gloss> Under the stars of Pushya, Hasta, Ashvini, Mula, Mrigashirsha, and Rohini. 21.

<meta>This page continues the sequence of childhood samskaras (rites of passage). While the previous page concluded with the worship of water and early teething, this page transitions into the rituals for the child's first sitting, the determination of their future profession through a "livelihood test," the first feeding of solid food (Annaprashana), the first tasting of betel (Tambula-bhakshana), and the piercing of the ears (Karnavedha).</meta>

### On the Ritual of Sitting the Child

Under the lunar mansions <note>term: "Nakshatra" — the 27 divisions of the sky based on the moon's path</note> of Jyeshtha or Anuradha, on an auspicious day in the fifth month (52), avoiding Tuesdays, the shadow-planet Rahu, and the junction-days <note>term: "Parva" — specific lunar days like full or new moon which are considered spiritually charged but often volatile</note>, one should perform the ritual of sitting the child down <note>term: "Upaveshana" — the first time a child is formally seated on the ground, often on a tiger skin or a rug</note>. 53.

### On Testing the Child's Livelihood

One should place a book, a pen, scriptures, cloth, silver, and gold [before the child]. <margin>26</margin> Whichever object the child first grasps, that shall be the means of the child's livelihood for life. 54. 

### On the First Feeding of Solid Food

<insert>**The wise say for the first feeding:**</insert> Under the two Revatis <note>original: "revati dvitaye" — refers to the star Revati and its neighboring influence</note>, Pushya, Punarvasu, and Anuradha; <margin>27</margin> the three stars starting with Shravana <note>Shravana, Dhanishtha, and Shatabhisha</note>, the three starting with Hasta <note>Hasta, Chitra, and Swati</note>, Rohini, and Mrigashirsha (55). Also under the three "Uttara" stars, in an auspicious ascendant <note>term: "Lagna" — the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon</note>, specifically Pisces or Aries. 

For a boy, this should occur in an even month, such as the sixth; for a girl, in an odd month, such as the fifth (56). This timing for the First Feeding <note>term: "Annaprashana" — the first time a baby is given solid food, usually rice</note> is agreed upon by all in an auspicious month. 

Some say this should not be done in the mother's birth month, but others maintain that the birth-star itself is acceptable for the feeding. 

### On the First Tasting of Betel

For the first tasting of betel <note>term: "Tambula-bhakshana" — the ritual of offering the child a betel leaf preparation, marking a transition in digestive and social development</note>, the favorable stars are the three beginning with Anuradha <note>Anuradha, Jyeshtha, and Mula</note>, the three beginning with Hasta <note>Hasta, Chitra, and Swati</note>, the two Revatis, the three Uttaras, Rohini, the two starting with Shravana <note>Shravana and Dhanishtha</note>, and Mrigashirsha (58). Also favorable are Punarvasu and Pushya, occurring on a Monday, Wednesday, or Thursday. The tasting of betel should be done in a pure month, never on a Saturday (59). 

### On the Piercing of the Ears

<insert>**Ear Piercing**</insert> Under the two Revatis, Pushya, Punarvasu, Anuradha, the two starting with Shravana, Chitra, Mrigashirsha, and Hasta, on an auspicious lunar day (60). It should be done on a favorable day of the week, either on the 12th or 16th day from birth, or in the 6th or 8th month. <margin>26</margin> One should avoid the months of Pausha and Chaitra, the time of the "Sleep of Vishnu" <note>term: "Hari-shayana" — the four-month period when Lord Vishnu is said to sleep, during which many auspicious ceremonies are restricted</note>, and the child's own birth-star (61). 

The stars prescribed for ear piercing are also considered excellent for the piercing of a girl's nose <note>term: "Ghrana-vedhana" — nose piercing</note>.

<meta>This page continues from the discussion of early childhood rites (Samskaras). ### On the Annual Birthday Rituals

Every year, on the day of one's birth star <note>term: "Janmṛkṣe" — the specific lunar mansion (Nakshatra) the moon was in at the time of birth</note>, one should perform a celebratory bath. One should worship Ganesha <note>The elephant-headed deity, traditionally invoked at the beginning of any ritual to remove obstacles</note> and the personal or family deities who oversee one's life. 96. One should perform various acts of charity and ritual giving. The binding of the auspicious marriage thread <note>term: "Mangalasutra"</note> or seeking liberation/blessings should be performed alongside learned priests.

### On the Consecration of Reservoirs and Gardens

<insert>Now, the procedure for the consecration of water reservoirs, gardens, and similar works.</insert>

The consecration of water reservoirs <note>term: "Jalashaya" — tanks, wells, or ponds</note>, temples, and gardens, as well as the binding of sacred vows, should not be performed during the southern path of the sun <note>term: "Dakshinayana" — the six-month period when the sun moves south, traditionally considered less auspicious for "building" rituals than the northern path (Uttarayana)</note>. 68. 

### On the "Infancy" and "Old Age" of Planets

Rituals should be avoided when the planets Jupiter <note>term: "Guru"</note> or Venus <note>term: "Bhargava"</note> are combust <note>term: "Asta" — when a planet is too close to the sun to be seen, losing its astrological "strength"</note>, or when they are in their "infancy" or "old age" phases. For Venus, "infancy" <note>term: "Balya" — the period immediately after a planet becomes visible after being combust</note> lasts ten days in the west, and "old age" <note>term: "Vardhaka" — the period just before it becomes combust</note> lasts five days. In the east, its infancy is said to be three days and its old age five days. 

For Jupiter, both infancy and old age always last for a fortnight (fifteen days) when in the sign of Scorpio. Some sages, such as Garga, suggest that in other signs, infancy lasts ten days and old age lasts seven days. For others, the necessity is only three days. 69. Some know the infancy and old age of the Sun <note>original: "Arkasya"</note> to be three days. In certain regions, a period of seven days is observed, while others follow a ten-fold classification. <insert>70</insert> 

In some regions, this lasts five days, and in the "Shadha" country, three days. In the eastern regions, Venus is considered the "King." 71. In this context, when the planet has moved to a position where it is no longer visible, it is "combust." Venus is seen again when it reaches a distance of fifteen units <note>original: "kha-bana-agni" — a numerical code where 'kha' is 0, 'bana' is 5, and 'agni' is 3, though here interpreted as degrees or cubits of separation</note>. 72. 

When the planet is seized by the rays of the sun, it remains invisible for nine days. Generally, Jupiter <note>original: "Vachaspati"</note> remains invisible for about a month when it is in the sign of Capricorn. 73. When it rises in the east, it remains for a month, and later in the west for a year. 74.

### On Favorable Times for Rituals

During the northern path of the sun <note>term: "Saumyayana" or "Uttarayana" — the auspicious half of the year</note>, specifically in the months of Chaitra, Jyeshtha, or on Fridays. <margin>A</margin> Or when the sun is in a Brahminic sign, or on a Tuesday. 75. On Saturdays, or on the specific lunar days and stars mentioned previously. In an ascendant <note>term: "Lagna"</note> that is strong or exalted, and when the Moon and the stars are in a powerful position. 76. 

In the month of Jyeshtha, but not for the eldest child <note>original: "jyeshtha sanyano mase" — a common taboo against the firstborn marrying or undergoing major rites in the month of Jyeshtha</note>, and some say the same for the month of Margashirsha. Regarding the child in the mother's womb, one should not perform certain rites if the child is more than five months along. 77. If the pregnancy is further along, the ritual should wait until the child is five years old. 

<insert>Now, concerning Internal Pregnancy/Conception.</insert>

In the northern path of the sun, in an auspicious month, with clean clothes and after study. 78. Under the influence of Jupiter, Mercury, or Venus, in an ascendant that is strong or exalted. Under the stars of Revati (both parts), Pushya, Punarvasu, Anuradha, or Ardra...

<meta>This page continues the sequence of life-cycle rituals (Samskaras). While the previous page discussed infancy and early childhood milestones, this text shifts to the formal start of education and the "Sacred Thread" ceremony, which marks a child's second birth into spiritual and social responsibility.</meta>

### On the Initiation of Learning

One should commence the initiation of learning <note>term: "Vidhyarambha" — the ritual beginning of a child's formal education, usually involving writing the first letters in sand or grain</note> under the lunar mansions <note>term: "Nakshatra" — one of 27 sectors of the sky through which the moon passes</note> of Rohini, Shravana, Hasta, Swati, or those named Chitra. 80. A wise person should always perform a man's initiation into learning in the fifth year. At this time, one should worship Ganesha <note>original: "Heramba" — a five-faced form of the elephant-headed deity</note>, the Mother Goddess <note>original: "Amba"</note>, and the Goddess of Speech <note>original: "Vagdevi" — Saraswati, the patron of arts and sciences</note>, as well as one's personal guardian deities.

### On the Sacred Thread Ceremony

Now, the procedure for the Sacred Thread ceremony <note>term: "Upanayana" or "Vratabandha" — a rite of passage marking the start of Vedic studies</note>. For those of the priestly class <note>term: "Brahmin"</note>, the ritual is performed in the eighth year from conception. However, if the child is particularly desirous of knowledge <note>original: "vidyakama"</note>, it may be performed in the fifth, sixth, or seventh year. 

<margin>ॐ</margin> For the warrior class <note>term: "Kshatriya"</note>, the ceremony is prescribed for the sixth or the eleventh year. For the merchant class <note>term: "Vaishya"</note>, it should occur in the twelfth year, or the eighth year from conception. 

<margin>11</margin> The maximum age limit for this initiation is the sixteenth year for a Brahmin, the twenty-second year for a member of the warrior class, and the twenty-fourth year for the merchant class. This timing is traditionally upheld. The binding of the sacred thread is most auspicious for the "twice-born" <note>term: "Dvija" — members of the three upper social classes who undergo spiritual initiation</note> when their respective planetary lords and the lords of the lunar mansions are astrologically strong.

### The Planetary Lords of the Vedas

The different branches of Vedic scripture are governed by specific celestial bodies:
- Jupiter <note>original: "Guru"</note> is declared the lord of the **Rigveda** <note>The oldest Veda, consisting of hymns to the gods</note>.
- Venus <note>original: "Bhargava"</note> is the master of the **Yajurveda** <note>The Veda of ritual formulas and mantras</note>.
- Mars <note>original: "Mauma"</note> is the lord of the **Samaveda** <note>The Veda of melodies and chants</note>.
- Mercury <note>original: "Budha"</note> is the lord of the **Atharvaveda** <note>The Veda of everyday procedures, healing, and protection</note>. 81.

Just as the strength of Jupiter and the Sun is sought for a marriage, so too should the strength of the Moon and the stars be considered for the student. 88. The most excellent months begin with Magha <note>falling around January/February</note>, during the bright fortnight <note>term: "Shukla Paksha" — the waxing phase of the moon</note>, on days ruled by Jupiter, Mercury, or Venus. Some authorities claim the first three days of the dark fortnight may also be used. 89.

The Sacred Thread ceremony performed in the birth month or under the birth sign of a Brahmin provides great wisdom, even for those who are still developing. 90.

### Auspicious Timings for the Sacred Thread

The ritual of binding the girdle <note>term: "Mekhala-bandhana" — a specific part of the Upanayana where a sacred belt is tied</note> is highly recommended on the 2nd, 3rd, 11th, 10th, 5th, or 15th lunar days <note>term: "Tithi"</note>. 81. The most suitable lunar mansions are the three "Hand" stars <note>Hasta</note>, Mrigashira, Pushya, and the two Shravana stars. 81. Revati is also excellent for the student's initiation. 82. Some also prefer the stars Punarvasu, Anuradha, and Rohini.

<insert>Worship of the respective deities</insert> 

For those who study the **Rigveda**, the binding of the vow is best when Jupiter is influential and during the stars Ardra, the three Hastas, or Mrigashira. For the **Yajurveda**, the ritual should be held during the northern stars <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, Revati, Pushya, Punarvasu, or Anuradha, 84. and specifically under Mrigashira, Hasta, and Rohini when Venus is well-placed. 

For the singers of the **Samaveda**, the ritual is prescribed under Ashvini, Shravana, Pushya, Dhanishta, or the three northern stars. 95. Finally, under the stars Mrigashira, Hasta, Ashvini, Pushya, Dhanishta, and Revati, when Mercury is strong, the vows for the **Atharvaveda** students are auspicious. 96. Punarvasu...

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for the timing of the Sacred Thread ceremony (Vratabandha) and transitions into the ritual for the completion of studies (Samavartana). It concludes the fourth chapter of the "Muhurta-Ganapati," a classic 16th-century astrological manual used to determine auspicious moments for life-cycle rituals.</meta>

For those who follow the Atharva Veda, the lunar mansions of Swati, Anuradha, and the one ruled by Aryama <note>original: "Aryama" — refers to the Nakshatra Uttara Phalguni</note> are considered auspicious. Some authorities state that performing the ritual under Punarvasu is also excellent. 1.

**Sage Kashyapa** states: One should not perform the Sacred Thread ceremony <note>term: "Vratabandha" — the ritual of "tying the vow," another name for the Upanayana initiation</note> on a Saturday, at night, during the twilight hours <note>original: "Pradosha"</note>, on "Throat-seizing" days <note>term: "Galagraha" — specific days in the lunar cycle deemed unlucky for starting Vedic study as they are said to "clutch the throat" of the student</note>, on non-study days <note>term: "Anadhyaya" — holidays or specific astronomical periods when Vedic chanting is forbidden</note>, or in the late afternoon. The transitions of the sun <note>original: "Sankranti"</note>, the beginnings of the world-ages <note>original: "Yugadi"</note>, the beginnings of the Manu-cycles <note>original: "Manvadi"</note>, the second lunar day, and the bright half of the months of Jyeshtha and Ashada should be avoided. So too the third day of the bright half of Chaitra and the twelfth day of the bright half of Magha. 2.

The first, eighth, and the three-day period beginning with the fourteenth lunar day are considered non-study days; these must be avoided for both the commencement of Vedic study and the Sacred Thread ceremony. 100. There is a fault <note>original: "dosha" — an astrological blemish or negative influence</note> if the ritual is performed in the first or fourth watch of the day, or during the two-way watch of the thirteenth lunar day, or the middle watch of the seventh day at the start of the night. 9. The three days starting from the seventh, and the four days starting from the thirteenth, as well as the fourth lunar day—these eight are the "Throat-seizing" days to be avoided for the ceremony. 3.

Regarding planetary positions: Cruel planets <note>term: "Khala" — malefic planets like Mars or Saturn</note> are best placed in the third or sixth houses. The Sun and Moon are auspicious in the eleventh, second, or third houses. Benefic planets should be in the quadrants or trines <note>term: "Kendra-Trikona" — the 1st, 4th, 7th, 10th and 5th, 9th houses, which are the pillars of a birth chart</note>. For the ritual to be successful, all planets are favorable in the eleventh house. 22.

Jupiter, the Moon, Venus, and the Lord of the Ascendant are not desired in the sixth or eighth houses during the ritual. Venus and the Moon in the twelfth house are also unfavorable, as are malefics in the first, eighth, or fifth houses. 4. For the ceremony, benefics are said to be auspicious except when in the sixth or eighth houses. The Moon is not recommended when it carries the blemish of its own waning phase, and the Sun is rarely desired in the Ascendant. 5.

The Moon or the Ascendant falling in the divisions of Jupiter or Venus is considered highly praised for the ritual. No other positions are as favorable, except perhaps their own divisions or the mansion of Punarvasu. The Sun’s position in the quadrants <note>the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses</note> results in the boy becoming: one who relies on kings, a protector of the strong, an excellent teacher <note>original: "pathakottamah"</note>, a scholar, one who is wealthy, or one who serves the sacred fire. 7.

The Moon, Jupiter, and Venus are not desired if they are joined with the Sun, Mars, or Saturn. In the month of Chaitra, even when the Sun is in the sign of Pisces, the ritual is permissible for a young student <note>original: "Vato" — a brahmacharin or celibate student</note>. 8. The Sacred Thread ceremony is specifically praised in the month of Chaitra when the Sun is in Pisces, even if there are concerns regarding the mother’s menstrual cycle or the ancestral rites <note>original: "Nandi-shraddha"</note>. 8. If the ritual is necessary, one may perform the tonsure ceremony <note>term: "Chaula" — the ritual first haircut</note> along with a pacification rite. The tonsure should be done in a single day, or the "shaving of the head" rite <note>original: "Keshanta"</note> in the sixteenth year. 10.

The same timing prescribed for the Sacred Thread ceremony is also taught for the "Return Home" ceremony <note>term: "Samavartana" — the graduation ritual marking the end of student life and the return to the household</note>. In the month of Chaitra, this is done when Mars is strong. 9. For kings, the "Tying of the Knife" ritual <note>original: "Kshurika-bandha"</note> should be performed before marriage, except on a Tuesday.

Thus concludes the fourth chapter on the Rituals <note>original: "Samskara-prakaranam"</note> in the *Muhurta-Ganapati*, composed by Ganapati, the son of Ananta, the king of astrologers, who was the son of the illustrious Harishankara, the beloved of astrologers. 7.

Now begins the section on Marriage rituals. For the sake of Dharma, Wealth, and Desire...

<meta>This page transitions from the timing of Vedic initiation rituals to the astrology of marriage. It discusses "Vivaha Prasna" (Marriage Inquiry)—predicting the outcome of a marriage proposal based on the moment the question is asked—and begins explaining "Guna Milana," the traditional eight-fold system for calculating compatibility between a bride and groom.</meta>

...if the means for a solution are found. I shall quickly declare the auspicious Ascendant <note>term: "Lagna" — the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of a birth or an inquiry</note> based on her chart. On such a favorable day, as previously described, having honored the gods with offerings like the "fruit of fortune" <note>original: "Shriphala" — a coconut, used as a sacred offering in rituals</note>, the wise should perform the marriage of the groom and the bride. 2.

In an inquiry <note>term: "Prasna" — a branch of astrology where a chart is cast for the moment a question is posed to the astrologer</note>, if the Lord of the Ascendant or the Moon is situated in the seventh house <note>note: the house of marriage</note>, the marriage between the groom and the bride occurs quickly, especially if the house is aspected by Jupiter <note>original: "Jiva" — the planet Jupiter, representing wisdom and blessings</note>. 3.

<margin>29</margin> If the Ascendant is Taurus, Libra, Cancer, or Aquarius, or if the Moon and the Sun are in Leo or Taurus in an odd division <note>original: "Vishamamsha" — referring to a specific harmonic division of the zodiac sign</note>, the marriage happens immediately. 4.

If the planetary lords are strong, a groom is granted; if they are in an even division, a bride is granted. If the Moon is in the sixth or eighth house and a malefic planet is in the Ascendant, or if Mars <note>original: "Kuja"</note> is in the seventh... <margin>or</margin> if Mercury is in the Ascendant and Mars is in the seventh, widowhood occurs in the eighth year. If a malefic planet is in the fifth house from the Ascendant, and is in its sign of debilitation or is aspected by an enemy planet, that maiden will either lose her children <note>original: "Mritavatsa" — literally "one whose calves are dead," a mother whose children do not survive</note> or will certainly be unfaithful <note>term: "Kulata" — a woman of wandering or unchaste character</note>. If malefic planets are in the seventh house, she becomes a widow; even if only Mars or Saturn is there, she is unchaste. 5.

If Jupiter and Mercury are in the Ascendant, she is of good character and fortunate. If the Moon is strong in the eighth or sixth house in an even sign... 6.

A cruel planet <note>original: "Krura" — a malefic planet like Mars or Saturn</note> situated there is said to cause the breaking of the marriage. The wise astrologer, seeing a combination <note>term: "Yoga" — a specific alignment of planets</note> for children, should speak of it in the inquiry. If the sound of a conch or a drum is heard at that time, it indicates an auspicious omen for the couple. 10.

The sounds of crows, jackals, or donkeys should be declared inauspicious. In such cases, there is no friendship between the couple and the marriage will not bring happiness. 11.

I shall now speak of the various types of friendship and compatibility. After first understanding the "Ghati-system" <note>note: a technical method for measuring time-based compatibility</note> for the groom and bride, the marriage should be performed... Friendship of the house lords, the temperament groups <note>term: "Gana" — the classification of birth stars into Divine, Human, or Demonic types</note>, the zodiac signs, and the pulse <note>term: "Nadi" — the most vital compatibility factor, representing the couple's physiological or energetic constitution</note>...

<margin>28</margin> These should be understood in their increasing order of strength. When there is an abundance of points <note>term: "Guna" — the 36 points used to calculate marital compatibility</note>, the union of the groom and bride should be carried out. 18.

...If the birth star <note>term: "Janma Bham" — the Nakshatra or lunar mansion the Moon occupied at birth</note> is in the first part, the husband is beloved of the girl. 16. If it is in the latter part, the girl is beloved of the husband. If in the middle part, there is mutual affection between the couple.

...between each other. 

The 'A' group <note>referring to the vowels of the alphabet</note> is called the Eagle; the 'Ka' group is the Cat; the 'Cha' group is called the Lion; the retroflex 'Ta' group is proclaimed the Dog; the dental 'Ta' group is known as the Serpent; the 'Pa' group is considered the Mouse; the 'Ya' group is the Deer; and the 'Sa' group is called the Ram. 1.

There is enmity between the Eagle and the Serpent, and between the Mouse and the Cat. There is enmity between the Tiger and the Deer, as well as between the Ram and the Dog. 2. 

Enmity between name-groups <note>term: "Varga" — here referring to the classification of the first letter of a person's name</note> causes great suffering; sharing the same group brings the best results. This applies to the bride and groom, as well as to a master and servant. 2.

Cancer, Scorpio, and Pisces are called Brahmins <note>term: "Varna" — the spiritual or social classification; Brahmins represent the priestly or intellectual class</note>. Aries, Leo, and Sagittarius are called Kshatriyas <note>the warrior or ruling class</note>. <unclear>Taurus</unclear>, Virgo, and Capricorn are Vaishyas <note>the merchant or agricultural class</note>. 22.

Libra, Gemini, and Aquarius are Shudras <note>the laboring or service class</note>, as are those born in the final signs. A groom with a higher class is better; a bride should not have a higher class than the groom. 24.

Gemini, Aquarius, Libra, and Virgo, and the first half of Sagittarius, are "bipedal" <note>human-like in nature</note>. The second half of Sagittarius and the first half of Capricorn, along with Leo, Taurus, and Aries—these are "quadrupeds" <note>four-legged animal natures</note>. 25. 

The latter half of Capricorn and Pisces are known as "water-dwellers." 28. Cancer is called a "creature" <note>original: "Kita" — often referring to a crab</note> and Scorpio is a "reptile" <note>original: "Sarisripa" — though often categorized as an insect or scorpion in this context</note>. All humans are under the control of others, except for the Lion <note>Leo</note>. Water is a place of excellence for water-dwellers, but Scorpio is a place of fear. 30.

<margin>Marriage matching</margin> 

The bride and groom must be compatible. If they are not under each other's "dominance" <note>term: "Vashya" — a compatibility category determining who will follow or lead in the relationship</note>, the union is not recommended. 38.

Ashvini and Shatabhisha <note>term: "Nakshatra" — lunar mansions</note> are the Horse; the Buffalo is associated with Svati and Hasta; the Lion is for Dhanishtha and <unclear>Purva Bhadrapada</unclear>; the Elephant is for Bharani and Revati. 41. 

Krittika and Pushya are the Ram; the Monkey is for Purva Ashadha and Shravana; the Mongoose is for Uttara Ashadha; the Serpent is for Rohini and Mrigashira. Jyeshtha and Anuradha are the Deer; the Dog is for Mula and Ardra; the Cat is for Punarvasu and Ashlesha; the Rat is for Magha and Purva Phalguni. The Cow is for Uttara Phalguni and Uttara Bhadrapada; the Tiger is for Chitra and Vishakha. 70. 

The following pairings are hostile and should be avoided: Cow and Tiger, Buffalo and Horse, Mongoose and Serpent, Cat and Rat, Lion and Elephant, Monkey and Ram. These should be abandoned by couples and by those devoted to their masters. 

Friends of the Sun are Mars, the Moon, and Jupiter. Venus and Saturn are friends of each other. The Sun and Mercury are friends. <note>original: "Maitri" — planetary friendship, another layer of compatibility</note> The Moon has no enemies. For the son of Earth <note>Mars</note>, the friends are Jupiter, the Moon, and the Sun... 45. The son of the Sun <note>Saturn</note> is a friend to Mercury, but the Moon is the enemy of Mercury.

# Preceding Rules, the Latter Triad 8

...to. Sun and Moon are friends of Jupiter. Mercury and Venus are friends <note>original: "mitra"</note> of Saturn, while Sun, Mars, and Moon are his enemies. For all other planets, those not specifically mentioned are considered neutral. In a marriage, the friendship of the zodiac sign lords <margin>29 15</margin> is auspicious when mutual. 39. Enmity between these lords should be known as unfavorable, while neutrality is considered mediocre.

**The Compatibility of Temperaments** <note>term: "Gana Maitri" — a system categorizing personalities into Divine, Human, or Demonic based on the Moon's position at birth</note>

The lunar mansions <note>term: "Nakshatra" — the 27 sectors of the sky the Moon passes through</note> of Hand <note>original: "Hasta"</note>, Sword <note>original: "Svati"</note>, Hearing <note>original: "Shravana"</note>, <margin>15</margin> Nourisher <note>original: "Pushya"</note>, Success <note>original: "Anuradha"</note>, and the Wealthy <note>original: "Revati"</note>, along with <gloss>2</gloss> the Returning Wealth <note>original: "Punarvasu"</note> and the Deer's Head <note>original: "Mrigashirsha"</note> are called the **Divine group** <note>term: "Deva Gana" — representing a refined, peaceful nature</note>.

The mansions of the Red One <note>original: "Rohini"</note>, the Bearer <note>original: "Bharani"</note>, the First and Latter Reddish Ones <note>original: "Purva Phalguni" and "Uttara Phalguni"</note>, and the First and Latter Victors <note>original: "Purvashada" and "Uttarashada"</note>, as well as the First and Latter Blessed Feet <note>original: "Purvabhadrapada" and "Uttarabhadrapada"</note> are the **Human group**. 40.

The mansions of the Bright One <note>original: "Chitra"</note>, the Embracer <note>original: "Ashlesha"</note>, the Mighty <note>original: "Magha"</note>, the Root <note>original: "Mula"</note>, the Wealthiest <note>original: "Dhanishta"</note>, the Hundred Healers <note>original: "Shatabhisha"</note>, the Forked <note>original: "Vishakha"</note>, the Cutters <note>original: "Krittika"</note>, and the Eldest <note>original: "Jyeshtha"</note> are considered the **Demonic group** <note>term: "Rakshasa Gana" — representing a dominant, forceful, or assertive nature</note>. <note>erased/struck-out word</note> 41.

When the groups are the same, there is great love. Between Divine and Human, the result is average. Between Divine and Demonic, there is enmity. Between Human and Demonic, there is the risk of death. 42. However, if there is friendship between the lords of the zodiac signs or the lords of the ninth-divisional charts <note>term: "Navamsha" — a subtle division of the sign used for deeper analysis</note> of the groom and bride, then the defect of the temperament group does not apply; such a marriage is considered auspicious. 43. 

If a girl of the Demonic group is given to a man who is not of that group <note>literally "Nagalo" or non-fiery</note>, then the marriage should only be performed if the other qualities <note>term: "Gunas" — points in the compatibility score</note> are exceptionally high. 44. If the woman is of the Demonic group and the man is of a different group, they should not wed; however, Sage Garga and others state that if there is harmony in the biological types <note>term: "Yoni"</note>, it may proceed. 45.

If the zodiac signs are in a six-eight relationship <note>term: "Shashtashtaka" — when the bride's sign is six places from the groom's and his is eight from hers</note>, it leads to death. If they are in a five-nine relationship <note>term: "Trikona"</note>, it leads to a lack of children. A two-twelve relationship <note>term: "Dvidvadasa"</note> leads to poverty. In other positions, there is happiness for both. 46. From an odd-numbered sign, the sixth is considered the "death" sign and is not favorable. From an even-numbered sign, the sixth is auspicious, while the opposite is considered unfavorable. 47. 

Even if the zodiac relationship is considered "corrupt," if the signs share the same ruler, friendship and discipline are paramount, provided they do not share the same life-force channel <note>term: "Nadi"</note>. <margin>29</margin> 48. Between Libra/Taurus, Pisces/Leo, and Aquarius/Virgo, there is affection despite the six-eight positioning; other such pairings should be avoided. 49. Even if the zodiac signs do not match, if their divisional lords are friends, and if the life-force channel, dominance, and birth stars are pure, the marriage is auspicious. 50.

**The Life-Force Channels** <note>term: "Nadi" — the most important compatibility factor, representing the Ayurvedic constitutions: Vata, Pitta, and Kapha</note>

The Eldest <note>original: "Jyeshtha"</note>, the Root <note>original: "Mula"</note>, the Horsemen <note>original: "Ashwini"</note>, the Moist One <note>original: "Ardra"</note>, the Hundred Healers <note>original: "Shatabhisha"</note>, the First Reddish One <note>original: "Purva Phalguni"</note>, and the First Victor <note>original: "Purvashada"</note>, along with the First Blessed Foot <note>original: "Purvabhadrapada"</note>—this pair makes the **First Channel** <note>term: "Adi Nadi"</note>. 51.

The Bright One <note>original: "Chitra"</note>, the Nourisher <note>original: "Pushya"</note>, Success <note>original: "Anuradha"</note>, the Wealthiest <note>original: "Dhanishta"</note>, the Bearer <note>original: "Bharani"</note>, the Deer's Head <note>original: "Mrigashirsha"</note>, the Latter Victor <note>original: "Uttarashada"</note>, and the Latter Blessed Foot <note>original: "Uttarabhadrapada"—this is the **Middle Channel** <note>term: "Madhya Nadi"</note>. 

The Red One <note>original: "Rohini"</note>, the Cutters <note>original: "Krittika"</note>, the Embracer <note>original: "Ashlesha"</note>, the Mighty <note>original: "Magha"</note>, the Sword <note>original: "Svati"</note>, and the Wealthy <note>original: "Revati"</note>, as well as...

# Compatibility of the Life-Channels and Final Scoring
### Page 9

...the mansions of the Star, the Boat, the Three-Footed, the <unclear>unclear</unclear>, and the Hearing <note>original: "Shravana"</note> are categorized by their life-channels <note>term: "Nadi" — vital energy channels in the body, categorized as Beginning, Middle, and End</note>. If the bride and groom share the same channel, it is <unclear>not</unclear> auspicious for taking the hand <note>original: "karagraha" — a formal term for marriage</note> in marriage. Specifically, if both belong to the **Middle Channel** <note>original: "madhya nadi"</note>, it results in death; therefore, one should avoid such a match at all costs. 54.

However, if the zodiac signs <note>original: "rashi"</note> are different but the mansions <note>original: "nakshatra"</note> are the same, or if the signs are the same but the mansions are different, then the flaws <note>term: "dosha" — an astrological affliction or mismatch</note> arising from temperament <note>original: "gana"</note>, life-channels <note>original: "nadi"</note>, and sign-positions <note>original: "bhakuta"</note> are nullified. 55.

The mansions of the Priest, the Archer, the Moist One, the Hearing, the Nourisher, the Red One, the Latter Blessed, the Wealthy, the Magnificent, and the Embracer <note>original: "Vipra, Shravana, Pushya, Rohini, Uttara Bhadrapada, Revati, Magha, Ashlesha"</note> are exempt from these rules even if they share the same mansion. To remove the affliction of the life-channel flaw <note>original: "nadi dosha"</note>, one should perform the necessary remedies: the gifting of a lotus, gold, clarified butter, gems, and the **Gifting of a Cow** <note>original: "go-dana"</note>, as well as the recitation of the **Conqueror of Death** mantra <note>term: "Mrityunjaya japa" — a powerful Vedic chant used for health and longevity</note>. 56.

One should give copper for a two-twelve sign relationship <note>note: a "Dwirdhasha" placement where signs are adjacent</note>, gold for a six-eight relationship <note>note: "Shashtashtaka," often considered a very difficult placement</note>, and a pair of cows for a nine-five relationship. For other minor flaws, a small amount of wealth is sufficient. 57.

To remove all remaining flaws, one may offer gold, food, clothing, and a milch cow. The points <note>original: "guna"</note> for social class and other categories should be understood to increase one-by-one in order. 59. In marriage <note>original: "vivaha"</note>, the union is considered auspicious if the total points exceed eighteen.

<margin>Another method for calculating the points of social class etc.</margin>

Regarding the **Social Class** <note>term: "Varna" — the four traditional social categories</note>: if the groom's class is superior or equal, one point is given, as stated by the sages. If his class is inferior, zero points are given. Some say that for the **Dominance** category <note>original: "vashya"</note>, two points are given for a match, and half a point for others. If there is natural enmity in the Dominance category, only one point is recorded. Two points are given if there is friendship between the two, but if there is enmity, zero points are recorded. 61.

If the ruling stars are auspicious, points are given to both. If both are highly auspicious, three points are heard to be given. If there is primary friendship, four points are awarded to both. If the relationship is neutral, two points; if there is one-sided friendship, one point; if there is enmity, zero; and if there is extreme hostility, zero. 62.

In cases of mutual enmity, the score shall be zero; if the enmity is equal, half a point. If one has an enemy and the other a friend, or if both are equal, three points are awarded. 63. If they share a common friend, four points; if they have the same planetary lord, five points. This applies to the friendship of the bride and groom.

Regarding **Temperament** <note>original: "Gana"</note>: if both are from the **Divine group** <note>original: "Deva"</note> or both from the **Human group** <note>original: "Manushya"</note>, or if the bride is Human and the groom is Divine, six points are awarded. Otherwise, if the woman is Divine and the man is of the **Demonic group** <note>original: "Rakshasa"</note>, only one point is given. 64. If the bride is of the Demonic group and the groom is Divine, zero points are given. 65. If both are of the same group, or if there is friendship between their biological types <note>original: "yoni"</note>, it overcomes the bad sign-position <note>original: "koota"</note>. If the bride's sign is distant, she may be detached; however, if there is a good sign-position <note>original: "sut-koota"</note> but enmity between biological types, zero points are given. If the groom is distant, six points are said to be awarded. If the sign is the same but the mansion is different, five or seven points...

# Compatibility and the Celestial Afflictions
### Page 10

The distance between the bride's and groom’s lunar mansions <note>original: "Nakshatras"</note> is significant; if the groom’s mansion is far from the bride’s, it is considered beneficial and auspicious. 70.

When the life-channels <note>term: "Nadi" — vital energy channels used to determine biological compatibility</note> are different, the points <note>original: "Gunas" — units of compatibility in the 36-point Hindu system</note> are meaningful. However, if the life-channels are the same, even a high score in other areas is of no value; for a wise person, the life-channel match is the most vital of all qualities. 71.

### The Affliction of Mars
If Mars <note>original: "Bhauma" or "Kuja"</note> is positioned in the first, fourth, seventh, eighth, or twelfth house from the ascending sign <note>original: "Lagna"</note> in a birth chart, <margin>2 30</margin> it leads to the destruction of the husband if in a woman's chart, or the destruction of the wife if in a man's chart. 72. 

Marriage should never be conducted when such a Mars placement exists, unless both the bride and the groom share the same affliction, in which case the flaw is neutralized and they may lead a virtuous and beautiful life together. 73.

### The Three Types of the "Poison Girl" Combination
<note>term: "Visha Kanya" — literally "Poison Girl," an astrological combination thought to bring misfortune to a marriage, though often remediable through rituals</note>

**The First Type:** If a girl is born on a Sunday, Tuesday, or Saturday, falling on the second, seventh, or twelfth lunar day <note>original: "Bhadra Tithi"</note>, coinciding with the mansions of the **Embracer** or the **Cutter** <note>original: "Ashlesha" or "Krittika"</note>, she is known as a "Poison Girl." 74.

**The Second Type:** If the rising sign <note>original: "Lagna"</note> at birth is occupied by a malefic planet <note>original: "papa-kheta"</note> in an enemy's sign, and two benefic planets are positioned in a specific alignment, she is born a "Poison Girl." 75.

**The Third Type:** If Saturn is in the first house, the Sun is in the fifth, and Mars is in the ninth, or if she is born under the **Branched One** <note>original: "Vishakha"</note> mansion, she should not be accepted as a bride, as these are the three types of "Poison Girls." 76.

### Remedies and Exceptions
**The Remedy:** By performing the vow of the **Goddess Savitri** <note>original: "Savitri-vrata"</note> to prevent widowhood, or by first performing a symbolic marriage to a **Sacred Fig tree** <note>original: "Ashvattha"</note> or similar, one may then give the girl in marriage to a long-lived groom. 77.

The birth stars can affect specific family members: one born under the **Embracer** <note>original: "Ashlesha"</note> harms the mother-in-law; the **Root** <note>original: "Mula"</note> harms the father-in-law; the **Eldest** <note>original: "Jyeshtha"</note> harms the husband's elder brother; and the **Branched One** <note>original: "Vishakha"</note> harms the husband's younger brother. 78.

**The Exception:** However, the first quarter of **Ashlesha**, the last quarter of **Mula**, and the first three quarters of **Vishakha** and **Jyeshtha** are actually auspicious and do not carry these flaws. 79.

### Timing for Rituals and Deeds
For marriages and other deeds, the mansions of **The Wealthy**, **The Cutter**, and **The Horsemen** <note>original: "Dhanishtha, Krittika, Ashwini"</note> are favorable for the bride and groom on their chosen day. 80.

Auspicious timing is also required for: starting the construction of water reservoirs or houses, consecrating icons, beginning or <margin>8</margin> completing a religious vow, priestly initiations, and Soma sacrifices. 81.

Also: great acts of charity, the study of Vedas <note>original: "Upakarma"</note>, beginning the writing of a book, ancestral rites, <margin>re</margin> the first hair-cutting <note>original: "Keshanta"</note>, the release of a bull, or the installation of a deity. 82.

Furthermore: ear-piercing <note>original: "Karna-vedhana"</note>, childhood tonsure, and the various sacraments <note>term: "Samskaras" — rites of passage</note> from conception onwards, if the proper time for the child has passed. 83.

Additionally: royal consecrations, the establishment of the sacred fire, entering the renounced life <note>original: "Sannyasa"</note>, the four-month vow <note>original: "Chaturmasya"</note>, and entering a new home. One must avoid these when <unclear>Venus</unclear> or Jupiter are combust or transiting the sign of Leo, or during the thirteen-day lunar fortnights or intercalary months. Thus say the great teachers.

Some authorities suggest that ornaments should be given on the hands and other limbs. Others maintain that gold, jewels, and ivory should be worn. Some say that even when Jupiter <note>original: "Guru"</note> is in fast motion <note>term: "Aticara" — a planet moving faster than its average speed, crossing into the next sign prematurely</note> or has entered a debilitated <gloss>zodiac sign</gloss>, or when the Sun is in Sagittarius or Pisces, or joined with Jupiter, such periods are to be avoided. For two months, when Jupiter is in such states, no auspicious activity should be performed by those seeking welfare. 88. 

However, if Jupiter is positioned in the trine <gloss>kona</gloss> <note>the 1st, 5th, or 9th houses</note> or the house of gains <note>the 11th house</note> while in retrograde or fast motion, no flaw is known to exist; in such cases, one may perform marriage and other rituals. 89.

When Jupiter is in the sign of Leo or in the Leo-division <note>original: "Simhamsha" — a specific sub-division of a zodiac sign</note> of another sign, one should not perform marriages. This rule specifically applies to the region north of the Godavari river and north of the Ganges <note>original: "Bhagirathi"</note>. 90. 

Indeed, all tasks and marriages may be performed even when Jupiter is in Leo [elsewhere]. 91. In the region south of the Godavari and north of the Ganges, there is a flaw in all lands, but there are exceptions. If Jupiter is in the sign of Leo while Ketu <note>term: "Ketu" — the south node of the moon, often considered a shadowy or malefic planet</note> is in Aries, there is no corruption. For necessary marriages, this exception is remembered in all lands. 92.

In the regions of Magadha <note>ancient kingdom in modern-day Bihar</note>, Gauda <note>modern-day Bengal</note>, Sindhu <note>the Indus river region</note>, and Konkan <note>the western coastal region of India</note>, auspicious rituals like marriage should be avoided for ten months [during these transits], without a doubt. 93. When a planet moves between signs, except for the four signs starting with Aquarius <note>original: "Kumbha"</note>, if it returns quickly, that is known as a "Lost Year" <note>term: "Lupta-samvatsara" — a year omitted in certain calendrical calculations due to the overlapping of lunar and solar cycles</note>. 94. 

In the land between the Ganges and the Narmada rivers, this is highly censured. For specific tasks, the birth star <note>original: "Janma-nakshatra"</note> is the primary consideration. In matters concerning the country, village, house-building, gambling, disputes, war, or fever, 95. in giving, reciting mantras, service, or using the Kakinya system <note>a mathematical method for determining the strength of names or places</note>, 96. and in re-uniting people, the name-sign <note>original: "Nama-rashi" — the zodiac sign determined by the first letter of a person's given name</note> is the primary factor. 97. 

Otherwise, in marriage and similar rituals, the birth star is indeed primary. 98. If the birth sign is unknown because of a lack of records, only then should one consider the name-sign. For a husband and wife, the group-compatibility <note>term: "Bhakuta" — one of the eight major factors in marriage matching, assessing the distance between the couple's moon signs</note> and the current planetary transits should be fully examined. 99. 

Even if the birth star is known for only one of the couple, the matching is performed using the birth star for that person and the name-sign for the other. Counting from the day of birth, or after the fifth year, up until the twelfth year, the marriage of a girl is auspicious in even-numbered years. 100. 

An eight-year-old girl is called **Gauri**; at nine years, she is **Rohini**; at ten years, she is known as **Kanyaka**; and beyond that age, she is considered **Rajasvala** <note>meaning a girl who has reached the age of menstruation</note>. 1. 

Gauri is known by the strength of Jupiter; Rohini by the strength of the Sun; the Kanyaka should be chosen by the strength of the Moon, and thereafter by the strength of the Ascendant <note>original: "Lagna"</note>. 2. The wise should calculate the strength of Jupiter for the girl and the strength of the Sun <note>original: "Arka"</note> for the groom; the strength of the Moon must be favorable for both. 3. 

From the birth sign, Jupiter is considered excellent when in the 5th, 9th, 2nd, 11th, or 7th houses for a girl's marriage or a young boy's <note>original: "Vatu"</note> religious initiation. 4. Its position in the 3rd, 6th, or 10th houses is mediocre; it is not desired when in the 4th, 8th, 12th, or 6th.
