in whichever sign the conjunction occurs, (7) it yields the fruit of that specific sign; Jupiter <note>original: "Jiva"</note> does not give the results of all signs simultaneously. For the groom’s marriage, the Sun in the third, sixth, tenth, or eleventh houses from the birth sign is auspicious. In the middle houses <note>the 2nd, 5th, 7th, 1st, or 9th</note>, it is excellent if worshipped. In the twelfth, fourth, or eighth houses, it requires triple worship. For a night wedding, the strength of the Moon for both the bride and groom is essential. Marriage of the groom and bride is recommended in their birth month or the month of Jyeshtha <note>the lunar month falling in May–June</note>. (10) <insert>For both,</insert> marriage is not desired if they share the same birth month, birth star, or birth day. A "Triple Jyeshtha" <note>term: "Tri-Jyeshtha" — the combination of the first-born son, the first-born daughter, and the month of Jyeshtha</note> is forbidden in marriage; a "Double Jyeshtha" is considered medium. Some say when the Sun is in Taurus <note>original: "Krittikastha Ravau" — specifically in the constellation Krittika</note>, a Triple Jyeshtha is actually auspicious. Others say marriage is not desired between two people who are both the eldest in their families. A daughter's wedding should not take place within six months of a son's wedding, nor should a girl be given in marriage <gloss>immediately after another</gloss>. Tonsure <note>ritual head-shaving</note> or festive celebrations within the family are otherwise praised. The ritual of parting the hair <note>original: "Simanta"</note>, marriage, and the thread ceremony <note>original: "Vratabandhana"</note> are considered "Great Auspicious" events <note>original: "Guru Mangalam"</note>. Other minor rites are "Lesser Auspicious" <note>original: "Laghu Mangalam"</note>. It is not desirable to have two Great Auspicious events within six months, though a Lesser one may occur. Three auspicious rites, or a funeral rite <note>original: "Pitryam"</note> for the father, should not be performed in the bride's family in close succession. For brothers born of the same womb, separate rituals should not be performed simultaneously. (11). Within an interval of six months, it is not desired for two daughters of the same family <gloss>to marry</gloss>. One may perform the previously mentioned auspicious rites or give the bride away later. (12). In times of crisis, an interval of four days or even one day is permitted. If the wedding is performed at a different river, mountain, or house, it is acceptable. (13). Likewise, if the ritual pavilion <note>original: "Mandapa"</note> or the astrological timing <note>original: "Lagna"</note> is different, it is proper. For twins, however, this is a special case: all their rituals should be performed together. Two daughters born of the same womb should not be given <note>in marriage</note> to the same groom. 
<margin>81</margin>
Nor should they be given to two brothers who are themselves born of the same womb. (14). Once a marriage is decided, if a death occurs in the family of the bride or groom (such as a father), the marriage is no longer auspicious for them. (15). Or, they should wait for one year, or seven months, or twelve days. After that time has passed, the marriage <note>original: "Karagraha" — taking of the hand</note> may proceed. When the Sun is in Aries, Scorpio, Aquarius, Capricorn, Gemini, or Taurus, the taking of the hand is best, after considering the pairs. (16). The months of Vaishakha, Phalguna, Margashirsha <note>original: "Madha"</note>, and Jyeshtha are auspicious. These months are excellent for marriage; the middle months are medium, and others are considered inauspicious. (17). In Scorpio, Capricorn, and Aries, the rites...

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for determining an auspicious time for marriage. ...not when the Sun is in certain positions. The months of Karttika <note>roughly October–November</note>, Pausha <note>December–January</note>, and Chaitra <note>March–April</note> are not considered auspicious for marriage. (24). In the month of Margashirsha <note>November–December</note> when the Sun is in Sagittarius, or in Phalguna <note>February–March</note> when the Sun is in Pisces, it is auspicious. A solar month <note>original: "Saura masa"</note> is generally praised for the commencement of religious vows and marriages. (25). A lunar month is considered acceptable if it is free from impurities <note>referring to "Mala Masa" or intercalary months which are deemed unlucky</note>. The conjunction of both solar and lunar factors is considered auspicious for marriages and similar rites. (26).

The sixth, tenth, and the "empty" lunar days <note>term: "Rikta" — the 4th, 9th, and 14th days of a lunar fortnight, often avoided for new beginnings</note>, along with the fifth, and the first day of the bright fortnight are lunar days <note>original: "Tithis"</note> that are considered favorable. Regarding the days of the week: the days of benefic planets <note>Monday, Wednesday, Thursday, and Friday</note> are auspicious; Sunday is middling, while Tuesday <note>original: "Kuja," referring to Mars</note> is the most unfavorable. 

The following lunar mansions <note>term: "Nakshatras" — segments of the sky the moon passes through</note> are auspicious for the "taking of the hand" <note>original: "kara-pidanam," a synonym for marriage</note>: Rohini, the three "Uttara" stars <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, Mula, Anuradha, Revati, Mrigashira, Swati, and Hasta. The stars of Magha and the two Phalguni stars are not approved by the sages. (27-29). 

One must avoid the "Ten Flaws" <note>original: "Dasha Dosha"</note>, which include: Obstruction <note>term: "Vedha"</note>, the Fall <note>term: "Pata"</note>, Conjunction <note>term: "Yuti"</note>, and others like the "Burned" days <note>Dagdha</note> and the "Malefic Companion" <note>Upagraha</note>. These ten must be abandoned. 

Obstruction <note>Vedha</note> is calculated using lines drawn in a specific diagram. The diagram known as the **Five-Lattice Chart** <term>Panchashalaka Chakra</term> is the primary tool for calculating these obstructions in marriage. One should draw the lines starting from the second line, including the stars Krittika and the "extra" star Abhijit. One must place the planets in their respective positions on this chart. (22). In the marriage chart, if a planet is well-placed but faces an obstruction <note>Vedha</note> from a malefic planet, that time must be entirely rejected. (30). 

Specific stars obstruct each other: for instance, Uttarashadha and Anuradha, or Abhijit and Rohini. (35). <margin>That</margin> Examples of these obstructions <note>Vedha</note> include: 
*   Revati and Uttara Phalguni 
*   Bharani and Anuradha 
*   Magha and Shravana 
*   Swati and Shatabhisha (36). 

The obstruction between Mula and Punarvasu is also identified through the **Five-Lattice Chart**, as is the obstruction between Hasta and Uttara Bhadrapada. (37). At the time of marriage, a benefic planet should ideally be in the Ascendant <note>term: "Lagna" — the sign rising on the eastern horizon</note> or in the eleventh house of gains. It is also advised that the Ascendant be associated with benefic divisions or a favorable planetary hour <note>term: "Hora"</note>. (38). According to the wise, the **Flaw of Obstruction** <term>Vedha Dosha</term> must be strictly avoided in marriages. 

Next is the **Flaw of the Fall** <term>Pata Dosha</term>. This relates to the specific astronomical conjunctions known as Vyatipata and Vaidhriti, which are considered highly inauspicious. (39). To calculate this "Fall," one looks at stars like Anuradha, Magha, Shravana, and Revati. Lines should be drawn between them in the grid. (40). By dividing the sections and observing where the lines in the chakra intersect a planet's path, the "Fall" <note>Pata</note> is determined with certainty. (41).

<meta>This page continues the technical analysis of astrological "flaws" (doshas) that must be avoided when selecting a wedding date. 1. The first flaw is called *Sagraha* <note>original: "Sagrahabhidhah" — literally, "with a planet." It refers to a constellation being occupied by a planet other than the Moon</note>. It should generally be avoided for auspicious ceremonies, though some sages say it is not a flaw if the planet is Mercury or Jupiter. Exceptions exist: the flaw is canceled if the planet is in its own constellation, its exaltation sign, or a friendly sign. Similarly, it is not a flaw if Jupiter is strong in its own division <note>term: "Varga" — a sub-divisional chart used for fine-tuning planetary strength</note> or if the Moon is strong and positioned in a cardinal house <note>term: "Kendra" — the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses of a horoscope</note>. (42).

If such a strong planet aspects the flaw, it destroys the *Sagraha* obstruction. Another variation is the *Dyuti* (Light) flaw: if the Moon is joined with a malefic planet, it is considered a flaw for as long as they are in the same degree. (44). <unclear>On a certain day...</unclear> 

<margin>32</margin> One should avoid a sign occupied by a malefic planet even if the signs are different. 

### The Flaw of Declination Equality

Now, the **Flaw of Declination Equality** <note>term: "Krantisamya" — a state where the Sun and Moon have the same declination, considered highly inauspicious</note>. One should draw three horizontal lines and list the signs starting from Pisces in order. From those lines, if the Sun and Moon fall on the same line, the occurrence of declination equality is possible; in such a case, a marriage ceremony is not favorable. (46). 

**Example:**
Aries pairs with Libra and Aquarius; Taurus pairs with Capricorn; Cancer pairs with Scorpio; Virgo pairs with Pisces; and Sagittarius pairs with Gemini. If the Sun and the Moon are situated in these respective pairs, one should know that Declination Equality is possible. 

Now, another way it occurs: in the middle of the *Harsha* yoga <note>term: "Yoga" — one of 27 astrological periods based on the combined longitude of the Sun and Moon</note>, or in its fourth part, and likewise at the beginning of *Brahma* yoga. In these cases, it generally occurs. <gloss>The Declination Equality to be avoided is that which is calculated by actual declination, as mentioned before.</gloss> Only the mathematically calculated Declination Equality should be avoided. If it is found through calculation that the declination has passed, it is not considered a flaw. (49). 

Declination Equality is not desired when it occurs even in a moderate degree. If the difference in declination between the Sun and Moon is less than thirty minutes <note>original: "Kala" — a unit of measurement where 60 minutes make 1 degree</note>, the flaw is active. (50). No greater flaw has been spoken of than this Declination Equality of the Sun and Moon.

### The Kick of the Planets

Now, the **Latta** <note>term: "Latta" — literally "a kick." This refers to a planet's "kick" or influence on a constellation a certain distance away</note>. Counting from their own current constellation:
*   The **Sun** kicks the 10th <note>note: traditional texts often say 12th, but this manuscript specifies the 10th</note> constellation ahead.
*   **Jupiter** kicks the 6th constellation ahead.
*   **Saturn** kicks the 8th constellation ahead.
*   **Mars** (Son of the Earth) kicks the 3rd constellation ahead.

The following planets kick backward:
*   **Mercury** kicks the 7th constellation behind.
*   **Rahu** (Son of Simhika) kicks the 9th constellation behind.
*   **Venus** kicks the 5th constellation behind.
*   The **Moon** (Lord of the Night) kicks the 22nd constellation behind. 

A constellation "hit" by these kicks should be avoided by wise people, as it is plagued by destruction. (52).

### The Flaw of the Single Bolt

Now, the **Ekargala** <note>term: "Ekargala" — literally "a single bolt" or "bar." A complex mathematical flaw involving the relationship between the day's Yoga and the Moon's constellation</note> **Flaw**. Based on the number of the Yoga, if it is odd or even... one should place half that number in the "Date-Palm" diagram <note>original: "Khajurika-Chakra" — a specific mnemonic diagram shaped like a date-palm tree used to calculate this flaw</note>. (55). 

Example:
When the *Vyatipata* yoga occurs in Ashvini, or *Vyaghata* occurs in Punarvasu, or *Atiganda* in Anuradha, or *Parigha* in Magha, or *Vishkumbha* in Ashvini or Pushya, or *Vajra* in Chitra, or *Vaidhriti* in Hasta or Mrigashirsha — in these cases, the "bolt" flaw is said to exist. And so it is said: in these Yogas...

<meta>This page continues the catalog of astrological "doshas" (flaws or obstructions) that must be avoided when timing important life events like weddings. ### Now, the Diagram for the "Bolt" Obstruction
<note>original: "Argala" — literally a "bolt" or "bar" used to lock a door. In astrology, it refers to a configuration that "locks" or obstructs the positive influence of a planet or time.</note>

One should draw one vertical line and thirteen horizontal lines. Place the **constellations** <note>term: "Nakshatras" — the 27 lunar mansions of the zodiac</note> upon these lines to determine the "conquering" positions. (57).

If the Sun is positioned at the end of a line in this **Single-Bolt** <note>original: "Ekargala"</note> configuration, it is considered highly inauspicious. One should never perform a **wedding** or other auspicious deeds during the time designated as a "Single-Bolt" obstruction. (58).

### The Fivefold Flaw
<note>term: "Panchaka" — a group of five inauspicious categories calculated based on the Sun's transit and the lunar day.</note>

By calculating the numbers six, three, one, eight, and four from the Sun’s **ingress** <note>term: "Sankranti" — the moment the Sun enters a new zodiac sign</note> into a sign, one determines the "Mercury-Fivefold" through a specific division. (59).

These five categories are, in order: **Disease**, **Fire**, **The King**, **The Thief**, and **Death**. If a remainder remains after the calculation, the "Fivefold" flaw is present, like a thorn in the side. (60).

The lunar days <note>term: "Tithi"</note> and the **ascendant** <note>term: "Lagna"</note> are divided by the planets. If the remainders are two, four, six, or one, the "Fivefold" flaw is active. (61).

**Disease**, **Fire**, **The King**, **The Thief**, and **Death** occur according to the lunar day. This is a well-known teaching among scholars; one should strictly avoid these during any auspicious work. (62).

At night, avoid the flaws of **The Thief** and **Disease**. During the day, avoid **The King**. At both twilights, avoid **Death**, and at all times, avoid the flaw known as **Fire**. (63).

At night, one should specifically avoid **Disease**, **The King**, and **Death** when they fall on a Wednesday or Saturday. Avoid **Fire** and **The Thief** on a Tuesday, especially when the ascendant is weak and lacks planetary aspects. (64).

If the **ascendant** is endowed with seven strengths, the flaw of **Fire** is neutralized. Avoid **Disease** when dealing with illness, **Fire** when at home, and **The Thief** when traveling. In **weddings**, avoid the flaw of **Death**, and in the **service** of others, avoid **The King**. (65).

### Regarding Burnt Lunar Days
<note>term: "Dagdha Tithi" — "burnt" or "combust" lunar days that are considered energetically void and unlucky for specific solar months.</note>

The second lunar day is "burnt" when the Sun is in **Sagittarius**. The fourth is burnt in **Taurus** and **Aquarius**. The sixth is declared burnt in **Cancer** and **Pisces**. The eighth is burnt in **Gemini** and **Virgo**. (66).

The tenth is known as burnt in **Leo** and **Libra**, and the twelfth in **Capricorn** <note>original: "Nakra" — the crocodile, synonymous with the sign of Capricorn</note> and **Aries**. These lunar days are "burnt" and are not favorable for weddings or similar ceremonies. (67).

### Regarding the Shadow Planets
<note>term: "Upagraha" — secondary or "shadow" planetary points that do not have physical bodies but exert a subtle, usually malefic, influence.</note>

Count from the constellation occupied by the Sun to the eighth, fifth, tenth, or fourteenth; also the twenty-second, nineteenth, and twenty-third, as well as the twenty-fourth. These eight constellations are called **Shadow Planets**. They must be avoided during auspicious ceremonies and the commencement of study. (68).

These are also known to be calculated by the numbers of days, lunar days... <unclear>thieves</unclear>

These systems are used to identify high-risk times to be avoided for weddings, travel, and starting new ventures.</summary>

<margin>33</margin>
<margin>33</margin>
Regarding the secondary planets <note>term: "Upagrahas" — shadowy or secondary mathematical points in the sky, such as Mandi or Gulika, often considered inauspicious</note>, the calculation is seventy (70). Subtract the number of the degree from the planet’s position. Then, the secondary planets and their descensions <note>original: "pata" — nodes or points of impact</note> should be discarded. (70-71).

If the **Moon** <note>original: "Chandra"</note> or the **Ascendant** <note>term: "Lagna" — the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon</note> is in the seventh house from a planet, that configuration is called the "Yamanna" flaw. It is considered highly censured and brings misfortune. (72).

Some teachers say this flaw occurs when the **constellation** <note>term: "Nakshatra"</note> is the fourteenth or in the seventh house. Others speak of the ninth division <note>original: "Navamsha"</note> or the fifty-fifth division. Then there is the "Seventh-House House" flaw, the "Secondary Planet" flaw, the "Node," the "Single-Bolt," and the **"Scissor"** <note>term: "Kartari" — literally a pair of shears or scissors; a yoga where a house or planet is squeezed between two malefic planets</note>. These flaws are destroyed if the Ascendant is endowed with strength in its **centers** <note>term: "Kendra" — the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses of a birth chart</note>. (73-75).

One should avoid the "Secondary Planet Scissor" in the regions of Kuru and Dwika. Avoid the "Scissor" in the Saurashtra region. Avoid it in the Vanga and Kalinga regions. The "Single-Bolt" <note>original: "Ekargala"</note> is avoided in Kashmir, and the Vidarbha flaw is avoided in all countries. (76).

### The Twenty-One Great Flaws
Now, the **Twenty-One Great Flaws** <note>original: "Ekavimshati Mahadosha" — a canonical list of 21 major astrological obstructions to avoid when planning a wedding or ritual</note>.

1. The "Vangaja" flaw, 2. The Solar Ingress <note>term: "Sankranti" — when the Sun moves into a new sign</note>, 3. The **Scissor** (Kartari), 4. The Planet-occupied Ascendant, 5. The "Kuna-vamsha" division, 6. The "Junction-Point" <note>term: "Gandanta" — the transition between water and fire signs</note>, 7. The "Single-Bolt" (Ekargala), 8. The "Bad-Hearing" (Dusrino), 9. The "Varaja" flaw, 10. The Malefic Constellation, 11. The Poisonous Channels <note>term: "Visha-nadika"</note>, 12. The Poisonous Season, 13. The Equality of Declination <note>original: "Kanti-samya"</note>, 14. The Aspect <note>original: "Drishti"</note>, 15. The Untimely Event, 16. The Eclipse <note>original: "Grahana"</note>, 17. The Six Malefic Divisions <note>original: "Papa-shadvarga"</note>, 18. The Eight Ascendants, 19. The Eighth-House Ascendant, 20. The Moon in the 6th or 8th house, 21. Venus in the 6th or Mars in the 8th.

These are proclaimed as the twenty-one in number. (77-80).

These Great Flaws must be discarded during **weddings** <note>original: "Vivaha"</note> and other auspicious rites. In weddings particularly, one should not even think of the seventh-house flaw if the Moon is strong. (81).

### The Scissor Flaw (Kartari)
If malefic planets <note>original: "Papa-khechara"</note> are placed in the second and twelfth houses from the Ascendant, it is known as the **"Scissor"** configuration, and it is a bringer of death. (81).

If a cruel planet in the second house is **retrograde** <note>term: "Vakra" — appearing to move backward</note> and a planet in the twelfth house is moving **direct** <note>original: "Margi"</note>, this is known as the "Great Scissor." One must avoid this with great effort. (82).

<margin>33</margin>

### Exceptions to the Flaws
Now, the exceptions <note>original: "Apavada"</note>:
If a planet in the second house is moving direct and one in the twelfth is retrograde, or if both are retrograde/direct, or if a **benefic** <note>original: "Saumya" — gentle planets like Venus, Mercury, or Jupiter</note> is in the Ascendant or the twelfth, then the "Scissor" does not exist.

If **Venus** and **Mercury** are in the centers or the trines <note>term: "Kona" — the 1st, 5th, and 9th houses</note>, then the Scissor brings no sorrow. If the planet causing the Scissor is in its debilitation or is "combust" <note>term: "Astamita" — hidden by the Sun's glare</note>, there is no flaw arising from the Scissor. (83).

### The Poisonous Hours (Visha-ghati)
<note>This section begins the calculation of specific "poisonous" or inauspicious times within certain constellations.</note>

It also provides regional exceptions and planetary conditions that neutralize these negative influences.</summary>

These are the durations of the "Poisonous Channels" <note>term: "Visha-nadi" — specific moments within a lunar day or constellation that are considered highly inauspicious for starting new ventures</note>: twenty, eighteen, twenty-one, twenty, fourteen, eleven, sixteen, fourteen, twenty-four, ten, thirteen, seven, and thirty units of time <note>term: "Ghatika" — a traditional unit of time equal to 24 minutes</note> are remembered. From these, four units are to be avoided as the peak of the "poison." (8–9).
<margin>= consumed</margin>
All these auspicious units are mixed. If these time-units are multiplied and divided by sixty, the results are the "Clear Minutes" and the "Poisonous Minutes" <note>original: "Visha-nadika"</note>. (10).

### Regarding the Lunar Days
The starting points for the "Poisonous Channels" across the lunar days <note>term: "Tithi" — a lunar day in the Hindu calendar</note> are: five, four, seven, six, five, four, seven, eight, ten, eight, and fourteen units respectively. From the start of the first lunar day <note>original: "Pratipada"</note>, four units of time are considered "poison." For the days of the week—Sunday and the others—the points are twenty, two, ten, seven, five, and fifteen units respectively. From these, four units of time are "poison" starting from the time of sunrise on that day.

### The Exceptions
If the planet Mercury <note>original: "Nabhash"</note> is situated in a corner <note>term: "Kona" — the 5th or 9th houses, which are considered very powerful and auspicious</note>, or if the Moon <note>original: "Vidhu"</note> is in its own sign or the Ascendant, or if a planet is placed in the "Center" or "Corner" houses <note>note: houses 1, 4, 7, 10, 5, or 9</note>, then it destroys all the flaws arising from the Poisonous Channels. (11).

### Regarding the Planetary Aspects
A planet casts a quarter-glance <note>term: "Drishti" — the 'aspect' or influence a planet exerts on other houses in a chart</note> on the third and tenth houses; a half-glance on the "triangle" <note>the 5th and 9th houses</note>; a three-quarter glance on the fourth and eighth houses; and a full glance on the seventh house. (12).
Specifically: **Saturn** <note>original: "Manda" — the Slow One</note> has a special full aspect on the third and tenth; **Jupiter** <note>original: "Guru"</note> on the fifth and ninth; **Mars** <note>original: "Bhauma"</note> on the eighth and fourth; and all planets cast a full aspect on the seventh house. (13).

### Regarding the Purity of the Seventh House
If the **Ascendant** <note>term: "Lagna"</note> or the Lord of the Ascendant is joined with **Venus** <note>original: "Sita"</note>, or if the Lord of the Ascendant is glimpsed by a "Great Friend" <note>a planet in a highly favorable relationship</note>, it is auspicious for the master. If the seventh house <note>term: "Dyuna" — the house of marriage and partnerships</note> or the Lord of the seventh house is joined with Venus, or if the Lord of the seventh is glimpsed by Mercury <note>original: "Budha"</note> or other wise planets, it is considered auspicious. If the Lord of the Ascendant and the Moon are in their own divisions and look upon each other, that "secret" is known as auspicious; otherwise, it is not beautiful. If the Lord of the seventh or the planet occupying the seventh division looks upon the seventh house, or if they are in mutual [aspect]...

### Regarding the Ascendants to be Abandoned: Lame and Others
<term>Pangu-adi-tyajya-lagnani</term> <note>The "lame" and other impaired Ascendants; in Hindu astrology, certain zodiac signs are symbolically considered physically impaired during specific hours, making them unsuitable for starting important tasks.</note> During the day, Aquarius is known as "lame"; Libra and Scorpio are "deaf."
<margin>20</margin>
<margin>35</margin>
Aries, Taurus, and Leo are "blind." During the two twilights <note>term: "Sandha" — the transitional periods of dawn and dusk</note>, the three signs beginning with Capricorn <note>Capricorn, Aquarius, and Pisces</note> are "hunched." At night, Taurus is "lame"; Virgo, Gemini, and Cancer are "blind." Sagittarius and Capricorn are "deaf." One should utterly avoid these at those specific times. In the regions of the Iravati, Vipasha, and Shatadru rivers <note>Modern-day Ravi, Beas, and Sutlej rivers in the Punjab region</note>, and at the three Pushkaras, one should avoid these for auspicious acts like marriage when the Sun is the lord of the day.

### Exceptions to Certain Flaws
<term>Apavada</term> <note>A rule that nullifies a previous restriction; a remedy or "exception."</note> At night, the flaws of "blind" or "lame" for the months and the sign of Virgo are to be abandoned... <note>original: "tyajyam matava gode na"</note> is remembered as giving auspicious results. A lunar day <note>term: "Tithi"</note> born of a "lion-like" power destroys flaws just as a lion kills a twice-born <note>original: "dvijam," likely referring to an animal or bird here in a metaphorical sense</note>. If Jupiter is situated in a center <note>term: "Kendra" — the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses of a birth chart</note>...

| Lame by Day | Deaf by Day | Blind by Day | Hunched at Twilight | Lame by Night | Deaf by Night | Blind by Night |

...born from the lunar day, a benefic in the house of gain is auspicious. (6).

### Regarding the Flaw Born of Junction Points
<term>Gandanta-dosha</term> <note>The "end of the cheek/node"—inauspicious junction points between specific lunar mansions (Nakshatras) and zodiac signs.</note> The sage Vasistha says that although there are three kinds of junction points, the period of *Abhijit* <note>a short mid-day period</note> is nonetheless auspicious. (7).

### Exceptions for Kulika and Yama-Ghanta
Regarding the <term>Kulik</term> and <term>Yama-Ghanta</term> <note>specific "shadow" planets or segments of time considered highly malefic</note>: If the lord of the day is endowed with strength and occupies the Ascendant, it then destroys the flaw born of <term>Kulika</term>. (8). If benefic planets are in the centers or trines <note>term: "Trikona" — the 1st, 5th, or 9th houses</note> from the Ascendant or from the Moon, it destroys the flaw born of *Yama-Ghanta*, which is like a flaw born of poison <term>Garala</term>. <margin>=</margin> (9).

### Exceptions for Ascendant Flaws
Taurus, Scorpio, Pisces, Virgo, Capricorn, and Cancer—there is no flaw in these Ascendants even for those born in Taurus or Aries. (10).

### Exceptions for Flaws like "Blind Times"
<term>Andha-kala-adi-dosha-apavada</term> <note>Remedies for "blind times" caused by seasonal or solar movements.</note> Flaws arising from the Sun's course <note>solstices</note>, seasons, months, or the "burned" lunar fortnights, as well as "blind times," are only harmful if the ninth-part divisions <term>Navamshas</term> <margin>24</margin> are occupied by malefic planets. (20). These flaws are destroyed by Jupiter, Mercury, or Venus when they are in the centers or trines.

### Exceptions for Ninth-Part Flaws
When the messengers of the planet *Kulika* are in the sixth house from the Ascendant, and the Moon is in the eleventh house, all flaws without exception are destroyed. (12).

### Exceptions for All Flaws
If the Ascendant is in the "Best Division" <note>term: "Vargottama" — when a planet occupies the same sign in both the main chart and the ninth-part chart</note>, or if the planet in the eleventh house is auspicious, or if Jupiter is in a center or trine, all flaws are destroyed. (13). Mercury in a center or trine destroys a multitude of flaws; Venus destroys two hundred; and Jupiter <note>original: "Vachas-pati" — the Lord of Speech</note> destroys a great many. In the Ascendant...

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for choosing an auspicious marriage time. If the lord of the first and eleventh houses is positioned in the eleventh house or in a central house <note>term: "Kendra" — the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses, which are the pillars of a birth chart</note>, he destroys a multitude of faults as quickly as fire consumes a heap of cotton.

### Regarding the Aquarius Sub-division
The ninth-part sub-divisions <note>term: "Navamsha" — a 3.33-degree division of a zodiac sign used for fine-tuning predictions</note> of Virgo, Gemini, Libra, and Sagittarius are remembered as giving auspicious results. For the purpose of marriage, they declare the sub-division of Pisces to be auspicious. (18). Excluding the "best-in-class" <note>term: "Vargottama" — when a planet occupies the same sign in both the main chart and the ninth-part sub-division</note> portion, one may perform a marriage in these sub-divisions. Even in a movable sub-division or a movable Ascendant, when the Moon is situated in Libra or Capricorn, it is acceptable. (19).

### Regarding Planets that Break the Ascendant's Merit
<term>Lagna-bhangada-graha</term> <note>Planets that "break" or nullify the auspicious power of the chosen time.</note> 
Saturn or the Sun in the Ascendant; the Moon in the sixth or eighth houses from the Ascendant; Mars in the Ascendant or the eighth house; Mercury or Jupiter in the eighth house; (20) Rahu in the seventh or the Ascendant; Venus in the eighth or sixth house; or if all the planets are in the seventh house—these are remembered as "merit-breakers" for a marriage. (21). Mars in the tenth or twelfth house; Saturn in the Ascendant; the Moon in the eighth or sixth; the Moon in the third house... like a path... these are not beautiful in the twelfth house. (20). <note>The verse numbering in the original manuscript repeats "20" and "21" in this section.</note>

### Exceptions to Merit-breaking Planets
<term>Bhanga-graha-apavada</term> <note>Rules that cancel out the negative placements mentioned above; effectively "remedies" or mitigating factors.</note>
If Venus is in an enemy's sign, in its sign of debilitation, or in the sixth house, it does not cause a fault. Mars in the eighth house is not inauspicious if it is in an enemy sign, its sign of debilitation, or is combust <note>term: "Astago" — rendered invisible by being too close to the Sun</note>. (21). A debilitated Moon, or a Moon in a debilitated sub-division, is not inauspicious in the sixth, eighth, or twelfth houses. If a strong Jupiter, Venus, or Moon is in the Ascendant, even if in an inauspicious division, (22) or if Mercury is aspected by a benefic planet, it utterly destroys the fault... <note>original: "vishkari chandrajah"</note>.

### Regarding Planets that Grant Benefic Marks in a Marriage Ascendant
The Sun, Saturn, Ketu, and Rahu are beneficial in the eighth, sixth, third, and eleventh houses. Mars is beneficial in the eleventh, third, and sixth. The Moon is beneficial in the eleventh and third. (23). Mercury is beneficial except in the seventh, eighth, or twelfth. Jupiter is beneficial except in the seventh, eighth, or twelfth houses. Venus, having gone to the seventh, eighth, sixth, or twelfth... (24) ...leaving those, Venus grants benefic marks <note>term: "Rekha" — literally "lines" or "points" of merit in a system used to calculate the strength of a moment</note>. (25). In other positions, the planets give middling results. Jupiter should not travel through the three signs... of a woman. (26). For the body, the faults... five... of the wicked ones... one and a half. An auspicious Ascendant for marriage should have more than twenty points <note>term: "Vimshopaka" — a unit of measure for planetary strength</note>. (31).

### Regarding the Knowledge of the Desired Time
<term>Ishta-kala</term> <note>The specific time elapsed from sunrise to a given event, crucial for casting an accurate chart.</note>
Take the measure of the day's duration, multiply by nine... divided by eleven. The result in finger-widths <note>term: "Angula" — a traditional unit of measurement</note> becomes the midday shadow. Subtract the midday shadow from the current shadow... multiply by seven... the result is the time in 24-minute increments <note>term: "Nadika"</note>.

<meta>This page continues the technical discourse on marriage astrology. While the previous page listed specific planetary positions that "break" or ruin the merit of the marriage hour, this page transitions into the mathematical calculation of the Ascendant (Lagna) and provides a list of specific "Yogas"—planetary combinations—that are either highly auspicious or strictly forbidden for a wedding ceremony.</meta>

# Calculation of the Ascendant at a Given Time
<margin>35</margin>
Take the degrees of the Sun including the precession <note>term: "Sayana" — the tropical longitude of a planet, calculated by adding the precession of the equinoxes (Ayanamsha) to the sidereal position.</note>. Multiply the remaining degrees of the current sign by the rising time <note>term: "Udaya" — the time it takes for a specific zodiac sign to rise over the horizon at a particular latitude.</note> of that sign. Divide this by thirty <note>original: "kha-tri" — a Sanskrit numerical code where 'kha' (space) is 0 and 'tri' (three) is 3, meaning 30.</note>. The result is the "remaining time" expressed in palas <note>term: "Pala" — an ancient Indian unit of time equal to 24 seconds; 60 palas make one Ghati.</note>. (35).

Subtract the desired elapsed time <note>term: "Ghati" — a unit of time equal to 24 minutes.</note> from the sum of the rising times of the subsequent signs. For the remainder, multiply by thirty and divide by the rising time of the sign. (35). 

<margin>35</margin>
Add the resulting degrees and minutes to the signs that have already risen. Subtract the precession from this total; the result is the precise Ascendant <note>term: "Lagna" — the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the moment of an event, considered the most important point in a chart.</note>.

### Another Method for the "Time to be Passed"
Regarding the "time to be passed" <note>original: "bhogya-kala"</note>, the best time is measured in palas. Multiply the degrees by thirty <note>original: "kha-rama" — 'kha' (0) and 'rama' (3) signifies 30.</note> and divide by the rising time of the sign to find the degrees and minutes. Add this to the Sun’s position. The precise Ascendant is found by subtracting the precession. The same process is followed for a birth or event occurring at night, using the Moon or Sun as the starting point as before. (35).

# On the Calculation of the Time of the Ascendant
The "time to be passed" of the Sun and the "time already passed" of the Ascendant sign should be added together. The sum of these, including the rising times of the signs in between, gives the precise time. (36). 
If the difference in degrees between the Sun and the Ascendant is multiplied by the rising time of the sign and divided by thirty, the resulting "desired time" is made precise. (37).

# Auspicious Combinations for Marriage
**The Flag Combination** <note>term: "Dhvaja Yoga" — a combination suggesting high status and honor.</note>
When benefic planets <note>such as Jupiter, Venus, or Mercury</note> are placed in the first house and malefic planets <note>such as Saturn or Mars</note> are placed in the eighth house, the young woman married under this alignment becomes a beloved favorite of her husband. (38).

**The Shrivatsa Combination** <note>term: "Shrivatsa Yoga" — named after the sacred mark on the chest of Lord Vishnu, symbolizing auspiciousness.</note>
If Mars is in a central house <note>the 1st, 4th, 7th, or 10th houses</note>, Saturn is in the sixth house, the Moon is in the second house, and the other planets are in the ninth house <note>the house of fortune and piety</note>, this is the excellent Shrivatsa Yoga. (39).

**The Blissful Combination** <note>term: "Ananda Yoga"</note>
If the Ascendant is Virgo, the Moon is in the third house, and Venus is in the fifth house, this combination is called "The Maker of Bliss." (40).

**The Half-Moon of Yama Combination** <note>term: "Yama-ardha-Chandra Yoga"</note>
If Mars is in the eleventh house <note>the house of gains</note>, Jupiter is in a central house, and Saturn is in the third house, this is the Yama-ardha-Chandra Yoga; the woman born or married under this becomes fortunate and virtuous. (41).

**The Slow-Moving Elephant Combination** <note>term: "Manda-gaja Yoga"</note>
If Jupiter is in the ninth house and the other benefics are in the eleventh house, it is called the "Elephant" combination. A woman with beautiful eyes married now will be a faithful wife, bringing wealth and spiritual merit. (42).

<margin>35</margin>
**The Conch Combination** <note>term: "Shankha Yoga"</note>
When benefics are placed in the tenth, fourth, and ninth houses, this is the Conch combination, which always brings auspicious results.

# Inauspicious Combinations
**The Wheel of Marriage Combination** <note>term: "Vivaha-Chakra Yoga"</note>
When malefic planets like the "Fire" <note>the Sun</note> and the "Crooked" <note>Mars</note> are placed in certain positions <note>the text suggests a specific circular arrangement</note>, a woman married under this "Wheel" combination becomes unfaithful. (43).

**The Pond Combination** <note>term: "Vapi Yoga"</note>
If all the malefic planets are situated in the central houses, the "Pond" combination is formed, which is highly condemned. (44). 
If the Ascendant is occupied by benefics but surrounded by malefics... <note>text continues to next page</note>

<page-num>55</page-num>

<margin>Jaichandra <note>Likely the name of the scribe or a specific scholar whose commentary is being cited.</note></margin>
If Mercury is in Sagittarius <note>term: "Kodanda" — literally "the bow," referring to the sign of Sagittarius.</note> or Aries <note>term: "Mesha" — the first sign of the zodiac.</note>, Venus <note>original: "Shukra"</note> is in the twelfth house, and Saturn <note>original: "Shanaishchara"</note> is likewise positioned, this is known as the **Utara** yoga **47**. This combination is said to be the "Destroyer of the Creeper of Men" <note>An idiomatic way of describing a highly malefic combination for the groom's lineage.</note>. If Venus is in the twelfth or eighth house, and the Moon is in the twelfth or eighth house, it is the **48** yoga named **Karna**. Some call this "Mayota." If Mars <note>original: "Rakta" — literally "the red one."</note> is in the Ascendant, the twelfth, or the seventh house, it is similarly regarded **49**.

## The Perfection of Godhuli
Regarding what has been said there for the maiden <note>term: "Kumari" — the bride.</note>: **The Nature of Godhuli.** When the time is free from the blemish of the *Vishti* <note>term: "Vishti" — an inauspicious astrological period or Karana, often personified as a demoness, during which auspicious works are avoided.</note> and other such stains, and is free from the influence of malefic planets in the Ascendant, but the Moon is in an unfavorable house, one should discard that time **50**. No other faults of the Godhuli time <note>term: "Godhuli" — literally "the dust of the cows." It refers to the twilight hour when cattle return from the fields, kicking up dust. Astrologically, this dust is believed to "cover" or neutralize many minor astrological flaws.</note>, such as the combustion of planets <note>original: "asta-yuta" — when a planet is too close to the sun and loses its power.</note>, are considered **51**, because they are all hidden by the dust of the cows on every side. 

However, if the Moon is situated in the sixth or eighth house, or is in the Navamsha <note>term: "Navamsha" — the one-ninth division of a zodiac sign used for fine-tuned predictions.</note> of Scorpio <note>original: "Vrischika"</note>, the bride meets her death in the Godhuli hour. This is stated in the *Samhitasara* <note>term: "Jyotish Samhitasara" — a classic compendium of Vedic astrology.</note>. There is another specific rule: avoid the Godhuli hour if Jupiter <note>original: "Jiva"</note>, Mars <note>original: "Kshonisuta" — literally "son of the Earth."</note>, or Saturn <note>original: "Tarunandana" — literally "son of the Sun."</note> are in the eighth or sixth houses, for death occurs in such a situation. If the Moon is in any place other than the tenth house from the Ascendant, the faults are considered moderate **52**. When the Ascendant and the eighth house and their respective Navamshas are fully understood, only then should one grant the Godhuli time **53**.

## The Time of Godhuli
The "dust of the cows" occurs for half a *ghatika* <note>term: "Ghatika" — a unit of 24 minutes; half a ghatika is 12 minutes.</note> before the Sun sets and half a *ghatika* after. When the Sun and Jupiter are both set, this is auspicious for ceremonies such as weddings on that day **53**. Some say this auspicious time lasts for two *ghatikas* <note>48 minutes</note> **54**. In the summer <note>original: "Nidaghe"</note>, it is when half the solar disk is visible; in the winter <note>original: "Himangame"</note>, it is as the Sun sets; and in the rainy season <note>original: "Meghakale"</note>, it is as the Sun disappears. This is the tradition of the Godhuli hour **55**.

**Marriage for Kings in Times of Difficulty; The Rakshasa Marriage**
When the Ascendant is purified, and during the northern course of the sun <note>term: "Soumyayana" or "Uttarayana" — the six-month period when the sun moves north, considered auspicious.</note>, the "cows' dust" time is specifically praised for the "Rakshasa" style of marriage <note>note: A "Rakshasa" marriage is one of the eight classical types of Hindu marriage, traditionally involving the abduction of the bride, often associated with the warrior class in historical contexts.</note> for kings. **Special rules for those of other castes:** For those of mixed origins, the specific dates and half-months are always relevant; such marriages shall bring wealth, children, long life, and affection **59**. **Regarding remarriage for women among Shudras and others:** One should perform the ceremony at night, observing the specific lunar day and month **60**. These are not desired; the wise have woven these rules into verses.

**Necessary Components of the Marriage Ceremony**
In the various rites of marriage, one should avoid the third, sixth, and ninth days. One should perform the rituals under the stars of Vishakha, Anuradha, Chitra, Jyeshtha, Revati, Ashwini, Shatabhisha, and the "Uttara" stars <note>term: "Nakshatras" — lunar mansions or star clusters used in Vedic astrology.</note>. These include the offering of sprouts <note>term: "Ankurarpana" — a ritual of sowing seeds to symbolize fertility and growth.</note>, the tying of the ceremonial bracelet <note>term: "Kankana" — a protective thread or bangle tied to the wrist.</note>, and the construction of the Vedic pavilion <note>term: "Mandapa" — the temporary ritual hall or canopy where the wedding takes place.</note>.

<insert>On a single seat</insert> In the second, fourth, or fifth year from the marriage, the entry of the bride <note>term: "Vadhūpraveśa" — the formal ritual of the bride entering her husband's home for the first time.</note> is remembered as bestowing sons. This should occur during the constellations <note>term: "Nakshatra" — one of the 27 lunar mansions used in Vedic astrology.</note> of Shravana, Mula, Anuradha, or Rohini **72**. Now, concerning the Venus influence: <insert>regarding the removal of errors</insert> there is a fault **74**. One should avoid the month of Jyeshtha <note>term: "Jyeshtha" — a lunar month falling in May–June.</note>; if the eldest son lives there, the husband becomes like a beast, and it similarly destroys the woman or her father-in-law. <insert>During the month of Ashadha</insert> or during an intercalary month <note>term: "Adhimāsa" — an extra month added to the lunar calendar to keep it in sync with the solar year.</note>, it surely destroys the husband; in the month of Urja <note>note: Another name for the month of Kartika (October–November).</note>, it destroys herself; and in the month of Magha, it destroys her father **75**.

**The Second Entry**

When the Sun is in Aquarius, Aries, or Scorpio, and when the Sun, Moon, and Jupiter are strong, it is auspicious. The constellations Ashwini, Mrigashirsha, Rohini, Revati, Pushya, the three "Latter" stars, the three Shravana stars, Punarvasu, Mula, and Anuradha are recommended. # 15. The Chapter on Marriage, the Bride’s Entry, and the Second Home-Coming
## The Rite of Conception

->During the Northern Solstice <note>original: "Saumyayane"</note> under the Vishakha constellation 6<-

<margin>21</margin>
The entry of the bride into her respective home during the Festival of Lights <note>original: "Dipotsava" (Diwali)</note> [is permitted]. Now, regarding the exception to the fault of facing Venus <note>term: "Prati-shukra" — an astrological taboo against traveling or performing rituals in the direction where the planet Venus is currently located.</note>: in a single city, a village, a 
<margin>3</margin>
country, during a royal war, a famine, or a pilgrimage, the entry of a new bride—or even the marriage itself—is not considered to be at fault. Regarding 
the position of Venus, as previously stated: in the lineages <note>original: "Gotra"</note> of the lineages of Vatsa, Triparna, or the descendants of the Sage Agastya, Venus does not cause harm. If a woman is in her father's house and her menstrual flow <note>term: "Pushpa rajas" — literally "flower-dust," a traditional poetic term for menstruation.</note> begins, her travel to her husband's house is highly recommended even if facing Venus. Thus says the illustrious **Shankara Suri**, the Crown-Jewel of Astrologers.

<margin>Notes</margin>
The Rite of Conception <note>term: "Garbhādhāna" — the first of the sixteen traditional Indian rites of passage (samskaras), performed to ensure a healthy and virtuous child.</note> is desired under the constellations of **Rohini**, Mrigashirsha, Punarvasu, Revati, Jyeshtha, and Pushya **1**. It should be performed on Tuesday, Sunday, Wednesday, or the days of Jupiter or Venus, provided these planets are not in their debilitation <note>term: "Nicha" — the sign in which a planet’s influence is at its weakest and most unfavorable.</note>. It should not be performed on "Empty" lunar days <note>term: "Riktā tithī" — the 4th, 9th, and 14th days of the lunar fortnight.</note> nor when the Moon is in the eighth house **2**. One should perform the Rite of Conception when the planets occupy the trines or centers <note>term: "Kendra" — the 1st, 4th, 7th, and 10th houses of an astrological chart, considered the pillars of strength.</note>, specifically the Sun, Jupiter, Mars, and the Moon **3**. The Rite of Conception is excellent when the eighth house is pure, when Jupiter is in the Ascendant <note>term: "Lagna" — the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at a specific time.</note> or in Sagittarius, or when Mars is in the seventh house **4**. If the Moon is in the third, sixth, or eleventh house, or if the Sun is well-placed, the woman becomes consecrated.

## Thus ends the Rite of Conception. Now, the Coronation of a King.

When Mercury, Jupiter, and Venus have risen, in a fixed sign, and during the Northern Solstice <note>term: "Uttarāyana" — the six-month period when the sun travels northward, considered spiritually auspicious for new beginnings.</note>... **1**. The constellations of Rohini, the two Punarvasus, Pushya, Magha, the three "Latter" stars <note>original: "Uttaratraya" — referring to Uttaraphalguni, Uttarashadha, and Uttarabhadrapada.</note>, the pair of Revatis, Hasta, Chitra, or Shravana [are auspicious]. On a favorable lunar day and weekday, particularly Friday, in a fixed and "Increasing" <note>term: "Upachaya" — the 3rd, 6th, 10th, and 11th houses, representing growth and improvement.</note> Ascendant, or when the Ascendant matches the king's birth name **3**.

### Auspicious Ascendants for Expeditions

For the understanding of the good and bad results for men, one looks to the Zodiac signs, such as Aries <note>original: "Mesha"</note>, and their respective lords. 

<margin>At that time 4</margin> In the rising sign <note>term: "Lagna" — the zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at a specific moment.</note> of the King, he will surely find success. Whether from the birth sign or the birth ascendant, when the Sun is favorable, he may travel. In those signs, during the appropriate age, his enemies will be destroyed. In a rising sign that is upward-moving <note>term: "Urdhvamukha" — certain signs are categorized by the direction they 'face,' which influences the success of specific actions.</note>, even if he goes quickly, he should proceed. 4. In a 'fixed' rising sign <note>term: "Sthira" — signs like Taurus or Leo that represent stability; usually considered unfavorable for starting a journey where movement is required.</note>, movement should not happen, unless aspected by benefics. Even if he has already departed, if he sees certain omens, he should return, lest he be destroyed by his enemies. 7.

### On the Results of Travel according to Aries and other Signs

Travel is praised as auspicious for men when the Moon is in Aries, Leo, or Sagittarius. It is considered mediocre or average when the Moon is in Aquarius, Gemini, or Libra. 13. Travel is said to be very long or arduous when the Moon is in Cancer, Scorpio, or Capricorn.

### On the Two Types of Travel Results based on the Strength of the Region

Depending on the goodness of the region, travel is known to be of a middling result; if the strength of the planetary transits is low, it results in disease or harm. 15.

### On the Purification of Moon, Stars, and Lunar Days for Travel

The three "empty" stars <note>referring to specific inauspicious Nakshatras</note>, the "empty" lunar days <term>Rikta Tithis — the 4th, 9th, and 14th lunar days, traditionally avoided for new ventures</term>, and the 8th lunar day are joined. 
The 12th and 6th days are also used for departure into the forest. 7.

The second lunar day leads to the success of the work. 
The third brings security and wealth. 
The fourth is known to cause distress. 
The ninth always brings death.

The eleventh is remembered as auspicious. 20.

### On the Best, Mediocre, and Undesirable Lunar Mansions

Now, the excellent, mediocre, and undesirable lunar mansions <term>Nakshatras — the 27 sectors of the sky through which the Moon passes</term> for a journey. Citrā, the trio starting with Citrā <note>likely Citrā, Svāti, and Viśākhā</note>, Maghā, Āśleṣā, Kṛttikā, and Bharaṇī are to be avoided. One's own birth mansion <note>original: "janma bham," the mansion the Moon occupied at the moment of one's birth</note> must also be avoided during a journey. 

Now, regarding the forbidden mansions: if a journey is absolutely necessary <note>original: "avashyake"</note>, there are certain hours <term>Ghatikas — a traditional unit of time equal to 24 minutes</term> that must be abandoned. In Svāti, Āśleṣā, Viśākhā, and Jyeṣṭhā, fourteen hours [must pass]... if the power of the journey's Ascendant <note>the zodiac sign rising at the start of the trip</note> is strong. 29. 

Now, another school of thought: some say Kṛttikā, Maghā, and Svāti should be avoided. Others say the same of the "Subsequent" mansions, Citrā, Bharaṇī, and Āśleṣā. 30. According to the sages Uśanas and Soma, one should always avoid Svāti and Maghā.

Aśvinī, Puṣya, Mṛgaśīrṣa, Hasta, and Śravaṇa are always auspicious for a journey. 45.

[Whether on land] or in water, having leveled the ground entirely, one should determine the directions there; only then should one begin the construction of the house. In a house that is empty or devoid of greenery, or if there is a reservoir [improperly placed], it will cause loss. Therefore, first, one must perform the calculation of directions. 17. Take a cord measured to twice the length, and [fixing it] at two sides... having taken a third of the length, one should pull it swiftly. 18. From the cord placed at the center of the field, [measure] half the width and half the corner; having fixed two stakes <note>original: "Shanku" — a gnomon or wooden stake used in ancient Indian surveying to cast shadows and determine cardinal directions</note> and made loops, one should use the method of pulling [the cord]. 19. At a distance of half a corner’s height, one should place the stakes in the field. By the twelve [sun-signs], the four corners of the field are manifested. 20.

### The Consideration of the Eight "Aya" Proportions

**Now, the arrival of the "Aya" results:**
1. Banner (*Dhvaja*)
2. Smoke (*Dhumra*)
3. Lion (*Simha*)
4. Dog (*Shvana*)
5. Bull (*Vrishabha*)
6. Donkey (*Khara*)
7. Elephant (*Gaja*)
8. Crow (*Dhvanksha*)

These should be known by those performing house construction and related works. 21. Multiply the width of the field by the length, and divide [the area] by eight. The remainder determines the "Aya," starting with the Banner. The "Aya" is considered excellent when it is an odd number <note>The odd-numbered results (1, 3, 5, 7) are generally auspicious, while even-numbered results are considered unfavorable</note>. 22.

<margin>40 50</margin>

The ancient strength of the "Aya" is known in the sequence starting from the Banner. The Banner is known to be effective in all directions. <unclear>ke</unclear> In the East or South, the house [if incorrectly aligned] dies. A house is called "Ancient" <note>Facing East</note>... [measuring] up to one hundred and twenty-two, or eleven barleycorns <term>Yava — a small unit of measurement based on the width of a grain of barley</term> beyond, one should consider the months and so on. 23.

In the East... <unclear>ve gnaiya</unclear>... in the South... these two are auspicious for houses. If the "Aya" is the Banner, the house should face West; if it is the Lion, it should face North. 24. For the Elephant (*Naga*), the house faces South; for the Bull, the house faces East. [Measuring] up to one hundred and thirty-two, or eleven barleycorns beyond, one should consider the months and so on; beyond that, it is not to be considered. For houses with balconies or temples, the "Ayadi" calculations must be performed, and the months should also be considered.

### Calculations of the Field Area

**Now, for the determination of the field area:**
Multiply the width by the length of the house to find the field area.

**Now, the sequence for calculating the "Ayadi" from the area:**
<margin>40</margin>
The multipliers: 1, 8, 1, 8, 1, 4, 1, 8, 1, 4, 1, 8, 1, 4, 8.
When the area is multiplied and divided by 9, the remainders are:
8, 1, 8, 18, 1, 28, 1, 22, 1, 25, 1, 22, 1.

### Now, the Auspicious Time for Commencing a House

One should build the house with doors facing East, West, and so on.

# Now, the Sixteen Houses Beginning with Dhruva

The sixteen types of houses are: *Dhruva* (The Constant), *Dhanya* (The Fortunate), *Jaya* (The Victorious), *Nanda* (The Joyful), *Khara* (The Harsh), *Kanta* (The Beloved), *Manorama* (The Heart-pleasing), *Sumukha* (The Well-faced), *Durmukha* (The Ugly-faced), *Krauncha* (The Heron), *Arishtha* (The Unlucky), *Vittada* (The Wealth-bestower), then the three of destruction <note>original: "nasha trayam" likely referring to the three following ill-omened types</note>, *Dwivipula* (The Twice-Spacious), and *Vijaya* (The Triumphant). 

To understand these houses beginning with *Dhruva*, one must look to the directions. There, the days are measured by the eyes and the elephants <note>original: "netra-abhidhi-gajair-mitah" is a Sanskrit numerical code where 'eyes' equals 2 and 'elephants' equals 8</note>. The origin of these types is said by those who know the essence to be the combination of patterns. By adding one to the combination, the nature of the *Dhruva* house is determined.

## Regarding the Sequence and Numbers of the Dhruva Houses

The first is 11, then 2, 13, 14, 15, and 6. The tenth is the priestly (*Vipra*) house 12. Thus, the remaining names are written in order; the seventh is the "Four-Sided" (*Chaturastaram*).

## The Diagram for Understanding the Dhruva Houses

To create the diagram, four heavy units (*Guru*) should be placed. A light unit (*Laghu*) is then placed beneath the first heavy unit.

<margin>
This should be known just as the Moon [phases], and the rest should be filled 68. In this way, fill the following positions with light units repeatedly; thus, all the variations are produced until all units are light 69. By this method, the designations of the houses and their porches (*Alinda*) are known. The light unit signifies the position.
</margin>

| Con | For | Vic | Joy | Har | Bel | Ple | Wel | Ugl | Her | Unl | Wea | Rui | Spa | Dbl | Tri | <gloss>Designation</gloss> |

# On the Strength of the Ascendant for Beginning a House

When the Ascendant <term>Lagna: The zodiac sign rising on the eastern horizon at the time of starting construction</term> is in a fixed sign <note>fixed signs like Taurus or Leo suggest stability</note> that suits the owner's nature, and is joined by benefic planets, the wise man should begin building the dwelling. However, if a malefic planet is positioned in the twelfth house <note>the house of loss and expenditure</note>, he should avoid starting the work.

## Auspicious and Inauspicious Planetary Combinations

If Jupiter is in the Ascendant, or if Venus is in the sixth house, or if benefics are placed in the seventh house or the trines <note>the 1st, 5th, and 9th houses</note>, the temple or house shall stand for a hundred years. (82). 

If Venus is in the Ascendant and Jupiter is in the fifth house, a house begun under this alignment shall stand for two hundred years. (83).

When Venus is in the eleventh house of gain, and the Moon is in the Ascendant, a house built then will be filled with new riches. (84).

If Mars and Saturn are in the eleventh house, the house will last for eighty years. If Venus is in its own sign or the sign of a friend, the house will be blessed with a long life.

## The Undesirable Combinations

<margin>1, 6</margin> If a planet is in its sign of debilitation <note>its weakest state</note> or in the eighth house <note>the house of death</note>, building a house is not beneficial. No matter how many planets are present, if they are poorly positioned, it leads to ruin. (85). It brings great misfortune if the Ascendant is weak and devoid of power.

## The Results of Specific Lunar Mansions

Now, the results based on the particular Lunar Mansion <term>Nakshatra: The 27 sectors of the moon's path</term>:
If the house is started when the Moon is in *Purvashadha*, *Uttarashadha*, *Pushya*, *Rohini*, *Shravana*, or *Mrigashira*, and joined by Jupiter, it bestows a kingdom, wealth, and sons. If it is in *Vishakha*, *Chitra*, or *Magha*, the house built by a brave man will provide an abundance of wealth, grain, and children. (88).

<margin>42</margin> However, if Mars <note>original: "kuja"</note> is in *Purvashadha*, *Magha*, *Hasta*, *Pushya*, *Mula*, or *Revati*, the house built under this influence will surely be reduced to ashes by fire. (82).

If Mercury <note>original: "budha"</note> is in <unclear>Ashvini</unclear>, *Chitra*, *Rohini*, or *Mrigashira*, beginning a temple or house leads to the attainment of happiness, sons, and wealth. (82).

If it is *Bharani*, *Anuradha*, or *Jyeshtha*... under the influence of Saturn... the house built...

### Now, the Installation of the Doorframe
<note>original: "dvāra-śākhā rohaḥ"</note>
The doorframe should be installed during the lunar mansions <term>Nakshatra: A lunar mansion or sector of the sky through which the Moon passes</term> of Ashvini, Uttara Phalguni, Hasta, Pushya, Mrigashira, and Rohini. In these constellations, one should fix the doorframe in place.

### Now, the Cycle of the Door
<note>The "Door Cycle" is a diagnostic tool used to predict the owner's future based on the Sun's position at the time of installation.</note>
From the position of the Sun, one calculates: prosperity, patience, stability, wealth, and success. 
<margin>Short</margin> 
From the houses comes wealth, and from that wealth comes happiness—unless a planet blocks it. If the threshold is placed in the "poverty" sector, it will not serve the master of the house. From the four central positions, however, one gains wealth and happiness. One should examine this cycle before constructing the door in their own home.

### Now, Determining the Auspicious Orientation for the Door
For those whose houses correspond to the zodiac signs of Pisces, Scorpio, or Cancer <note>original: "mīna-ali-karka"</note>, the door should face the West. For Virgo, Gemini, and Capricorn <note>original: "kanyā-mithuna-nakra"</note>, the door should face the South. For those under Aquarius, it is traditionally known to face the West. For Sagittarius, Aries, and Leo, the door should be placed facing the North. 90.

### Now, Determining the Measurements and Placement of the Door
When the length of the wall is divided into nine parts, the door should be placed according to the specific direction. On the East side, it is placed in the third part from the corner. On the South, the third; on the West, the fifth; and on the North, the third part. This is how the wise person should arrange the path. 91.

### Now, the Height of the Door
The height of the door should be twice its width, or a quarter of the house's height. This measurement includes the frame <note>original: "karaiḥ," literally 'hands' or 'cubits'</note>. In a house, these proportions must be known: whether they are equal, greater, or lesser [than the standard].

### Now, the Consideration of Door Obstructions
<note>original: "vedha-vicāraḥ"</note>
An obstruction <term>Vedha: A 'piercing' or structural interference where an object blocks the energy flow of the main entrance</term> is caused by a corner, a road, a mud-pit, a well, a pillar, or a temple of the gods located directly in front of the door. 94.

### Now, the Exception to Obstructions
If the distance between the house and the obstruction is more than twice the height of the house, then the "piercing" caused by corners, roads, or walls no longer applies.

### Now, the Construction of the Sixteen Rooms
Beginning from the East, one should sequentially arrange the room for bathing <note>East</note>, the kitchen <note>Southeast</note>, the bedroom <note>South</note>, the library for the learned <note>Southwest</note>, the dining area <note>West</note>, and the <unclear>room for the deities</unclear> <note>Northwest</note>...

### On the Acquisition of Knowledge and Success
<note>original: "vidyābhyāsa"</note> 
Regarding the study of sciences and the attainment of victory in the city of the people, one should observe the sequence of planetary strengths. When the central planets are weak, even the wise may suffer a decline in their vital energy <insert>97</insert>. One should consult the learned regarding the strength of Mercury <note>original: "Budha"</note> and Venus <note>original: "Shukra"</note>, especially when Mercury coincides with a Wednesday. 

Likewise, the learned <gloss>technical division 3, and again 1</gloss> should determine the strength of the North. For those who follow the ancient timings, <margin>42</margin> the southern part of the house is considered auspicious for kings.

### Now, the Digging and Inauguration of the Well
<note>In Vastu Shastra, the placement of a water source is critical for the flow of energy.</note>
The digging of a well <note>original: "kūpa khanana"</note> and its first use brings various results based on its direction from the house:
*   **Northeast:** Brings nourishment, lordship, and the growth of wealth.
*   **East:** May lead to the death of women in the household <note>This reflects ancient superstitions regarding specific directional imbalances.</note>.
*   **Southeast:** Leads to loss of wealth.
*   **South:** Brings affection and happiness.
*   **Southwest:** Causes the death of the master.
*   **West:** Leads to prosperity.
*   **Northwest:** Brings love and joy.
*   **North:** Brings happiness.

If a well is placed exactly in the center of the house, it leads to the total destruction of wealth. Therefore, one should not dig it there.

### Now, the Laying of Bricks, Gems, and Plastering
<note>original: "iṣṭakā-ratna sudhā-lepañca"</note>
One should perform the ritual of laying bricks and "gems" <note>The practice of burying small precious stones in the foundation for planetary alignment.</note> during the lunar mansions <term>Nakshatras: Lunar constellations</term> of the three Uttaras, Vishvadeva, Jyeshtha, Rohini, and Hasta. This should be done on a Sunday, Wednesday, or Thursday. The plastering <note>original: "sudhā-lepa"; literally "nectar-shmearing," referring to white lime-plaster</note> of the house should be done under the same auspicious stars as the laying of the bricks.

### Now, the Household Utensils and the Kitchen
<note>original: "gehopakaraṇa culhī"</note>
The installation of the stove <note>original: "culhī"</note> and the arrangement of household tools should occur when the Moon is in Rohini, Pushya, or the three Uttaras. These are auspicious times for establishing the kitchen <term>Mahanasa: The great kitchen area of a traditional Indian home</term> along with all its necessary equipment. 103.

### On Painting and Decoration
Under the constellations of Chitra, Revati, the three Hastas, Anuradha, Mrigashira, Shravana, Punarvasu, Rohini, and Purva Ashadha, one should decorate the home. If the house is decorated with paintings and various colors <note>original: "citraṃ"</note> without these considerations, the prosperity of the master may not remain stable.

### Now, Auspicious Trees for the Courtyard
<note>original: "gṛhāṅgaṇe śubhavṛkṣāḥ"</note>
The following trees should be planted in the house courtyard to provide health and good fortune:
*   **Punnaga** <note>Alexandrian Laurel</note>
*   **Champaka** <note>Magnolia</note>
*   **Ashoka**
*   **Pippali** <note>Long pepper</note>
*   **Dadimi** <note>Pomegranate</note>
*   **Bakula** <note>Spanish cherry</note>
*   **Tilaka**
*   **Draksha** <note>Grapevine</note>
*   **Pichumanda** <note>Neem</note>
*   **Maricha** <note>Black pepper</note>
*   **Narikela** <note>Coconut</note>
*   **Jati** <note>Jasmine</note>
*   **Patala** <note>Trumpet flower</note>
<margin>42</margin>
*   **Mallika** <note>Arabian Jasmine</note>
*   **Gavedhuka** <note>A type of wild grass or grain</note>

These trees, when grown in the courtyard, are givers of health and auspiciousness. 

### On the Planting Locations
The master of the house should plant these trees in specific directions. For instance, the **Udumbara** <note>Cluster fig tree</note> should be planted in the Southeast <note>original: "āgneyāṃ"</note>, while in the North...

This page transitions from the final landscape considerations of the courtyard to the auspicious timings for the ceremonial entry into a new or renovated home. It concludes the eleventh chapter of the "Muhurtaganapati" by Mahadevajna and begins the twelfth chapter.</meta>

...as a moonless night. 

### Inauspicious Trees in the House Courtyard
<note>original: "gṛhāṅgaṇe aśubha vṛkṣāḥ"</note>
The Citron <note>original: "mātuliṅga"</note>, the Spurge <note>original: "snuhī"</note>, the Banana tree, Turmeric, and the Agasti tree <note>original: "munidruma"</note>—these bring bad dreams if they are found growing or are planted within the house grounds. 

**Now, according to some opinions**, all trees with thorns or milky sap are inauspicious in the courtyard. Some scholars state that thorny trees should be strictly avoided in the courtyard of a residence. The Kovidara tree <note>Bauhinia variegata</note> and others are likewise not considered favorable for the owner of the house. 20.

### Timing for the Construction of Cities and Ramparts
<note>original: "pura grāma prākārādīnāṃ nirmāṇe"</note>
For the creation of ramparts, towns, and villages, one should use the specialized timing calculations <note>original: "satra sādhana"</note>. One should begin these works under the lunar mansions <term>Nakshatras: constellations in the moon's path</term> prescribed for starting a house, ideally on a Saturday, or when the planetary strengths are balanced across all temples and shrines.

### Beginning Shrines and Fortifications
Under the three "Prior" constellations <note>original: "pūrvā-traya"; these are Purva Phalguni, Purva Ashadha, and Purva Bhadrapada</note>, Bharani, Pushya <note>original: "dhiṣṇya"</note>, and Rohini, during a stable rising sign <term>Sthira Lagna: a zodiac sign that promotes longevity and permanence</term>, the construction of a victory-shrine or city walls is most auspicious. 21.

**Thus ends the eleventh chapter on Architecture <note>original: "Vāstu"</note> in the "Muhurtaganapati" by the illustrious Mahadevajna.**

***

### On Entering the House
<note>original: "gṛhapraveśa"; the ritual first entry into a home</term>
One should perform the entry into a new house during the Northern Solstice <note>original: "saumyāyana" or Uttarayana, when the sun moves north</note>. This should be done in the months mentioned previously, after first performing the worship of the site-deity <note>original: "Vāstu pūjana"</note>. 

The months of Magha <note>Jan–Feb</note>, Phalguna <note>Feb–Mar</note>, Vaishakha <note>Apr–May</note>, and Jyeshtha <note>May–Jun</note> are best for a new house. For the entry ceremony, the lunar months of Shravana, Margashirsha, and Kartika are also praised by the learned. 2.

In the case of a house that has been renovated, an old house, or when returning from a long journey, one must still carefully consider the planetary positions and the setting of the stars. 

One should enter the home especially under the three "Latter" constellations <note>Uttara Phalguni, Uttara Ashadha, and Uttara Bhadrapada</note>, Anuradha, Revati, the two Rohinis, and Chitra. 

### Entry into an Old or Renovated House
Under Dhanishta, Punarvasu <note>original: "aditi"</note>, Pushya, the three "Latter" constellations, the two Rohinis, Chitra, Swati, and Anuradha, one may safely enter a renovated temple or residence.

### Results of Different Constellations during Entry
*   **Ashwini, Magha, or Mula:** May lead to a risk of death.
*   **Swati, Pushya, and the three Hastas:** Bring happiness and human comforts.
*   **Bharani and Vishakha:** May lead to loss of wealth or violence <note>original: "vadha"</note>.
*   **Jyeshtha, Ashlesha, and Mula:** The master’s son or dependents may face ruin.
*   **Krittika:** The new house may be at risk of fire.

Such are the results of the constellations for those moving into a home.

### Results of the Days of the Week
Entry on Thursday, Friday, Wednesday, or Monday grants wealth and happiness. If one enters on a Saturday, there is a slight fear of theft or loss. 

### Times to Avoid for Entry
One should avoid the month of Chaitra <note>Mar–Apr</note>, Tuesdays, Sundays, the "Empty" and "Burnt" lunar days <term>Riktā and Dagdhā Tithis: specific dates in the lunar month considered unlucky</term>, and the New Moon for entering a new house. 

**Now, the Ascendant...**

<meta>This page continues the technical instructions for building and inhabiting a home, specifically transitioning from the construction of city walls to the precise astrological timing and rituals for the first entry into a house (Griha Pravesha). It marks the conclusion of the nineteenth chapter of this treatise.</meta>

One should enter the house or the forest-dwelling based on the strength of the rising sign <term>Lagna: the zodiacal sign ascending on the eastern horizon</term>. If the entry occurs during a beneficent division <note>original: "shubhamshaka"</note> or in the houses of growth <note>original: "upachaya"; the 3rd, 6th, 10th, and 11th houses</note>, it subdues the bile <note>original: "pittam"; here likely referring to physical and environmental heat or agitation</note>. 18.

Entry is auspicious when the beneficent planets are in the angles <note>original: "kendra"; houses 1, 4, 7, 10</note> or trines <note>original: "trikona"; houses 1, 5, 9</note>, and the malefic planets are placed in the third, sixth, or eleventh houses. One should enter the house accompanied by the sound of Vedic chanting, carrying a lamp and a **pot filled with water** <note>original: "jala pūrṇa ghaṭaṃ"; a symbol of abundance and the presence of the goddess Lakshmi</note>. 19.

The direction of the Sun determines the auspiciousness of the entry. Starting from the East, the Sun moves through the directions in sequence; one should know its position to ensure the path is clear of obstacles.

<margin>44</margin> ### On the Auspicious Lunar Days for Selecting the House Door
<note>original: "atha gṛhadvāra varaṇa tithayaḥ"</note>
An entrance facing the East is auspicious during the *Putra* and *Hasta* constellations. For men, facing the entrance is beneficial on *Bhadra* <term>Bhadra: "fortunate" lunar days (2nd, 7th, 12th)</term> and *Jaya* <term>Jaya: "victorious" lunar days (3rd, 8th, 13th)</term> dates.

### On the Various Rules for House Entry
<note>original: "atha gṛha tatpraveśe viviśayaḥ"</note>
Obstructions <note>original: "vedha"</note> should be avoided during the day. Generally, entering the home at night is forbidden, except under specific stellar alignments when the Sun is in certain positions. One should not enter the house at night if the sky is hidden by clouds or if there is no clear light.

### On the Time-Wheel for Entering the House Door
<note>original: "atha gṛha dvāra praveśa kāla cakram"</note>
When the Sun's rays fall upon the door, the position of Rahu <note>original: "vahi rāhu"; the shadow planet</note> must be calculated. If Rahu resides in the East, South, or West during the entry, it can cause strife <note>original: "kali"</note> or destruction <note>original: "nāśa"</note>. However, if it resides in the "neck" <note>a specific calculation in the astrological diagram</note>, it is considered auspicious.

### On the Ritual for Entering the House
<note>original: "atha gṛha praveśa vidhiḥ"</note>
At the appointed time, the owner <note>original: "raja"; literally 'king' or 'lord of the house'</note> should enter the auspicious home under a canopy, amidst the sounds of Vedic hymns and joyful celebrations. 20. 

There, one should worship the **Shiva-linga** and **Ganesha**, and honor the family priest <note>original: "purodhasam"</note> who is well-versed in the rituals. Offerings should be made to the deities of the ten directions with gold and gems. 21.

### On the Different Types of Entry
<note>original: "atha vividha praveśaḥ"</note>
The sages have described "New Entry" <note>original: "apūrva gṛha praveśo"</note> for a brand-new house, and "Entry after Absence" <note>original: "sapurva"</note> for returning to a home after a long journey or renovation. The best constellations for these are Anuradha, Revati, Mrigashira, 22, and Rohini. According to the wise, these three types of entry bring merit.

### On the Worship of the Site Deity
<note>original: "atha vāstu pūjan"</note>
One must never enter a house without first performing the **Vastu Puja** <note>original: "vāstu pūjana"; a ritual to appease the spirit of the land and building</note>. A wise man should not enter a house that is not yet roofed, or one that lacks a door <note>original: "kapāṭa rahitam"</note>, or one where the woodwork is incomplete. 23.

### On Vastu Worship
<note>original: "atha vāstu pūjana"</note>
The worship of the Vastu should be performed under the constellations of Punarvasu, Uttara, Rohini, or Ashwini. 24. This worship is most effective when preceded by the offering of a ritual sacrifice <note>original: "bali dāna"</note>.

### On Entering a New Fortified City
<note>original: "atha navadurgapura praveśaḥ"</note>
To enter a new "Victory City" <note>original: "vijaya nagaraṃ"</note>, one should choose the constellations of Vishakha, Krittika, or Uttarashadha. 25.

Thus ends the nineteenth chapter on "House Entry" <note>original: "gṛha praveśa prakaraṇam"</note> in the work composed by the son of the illustrious astrologer Harishankara Suri, the scholar **Shankar Suri**.
